9//! Procfs files that Detcore renders without the backend opening them
10//! (<https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4213>).
11//!
12//! For a file whose every byte comes from Detcore's own state
13//! (`ProcfsFile::rendered_path`), Detcore decides on every backend that it
14//! exists, what its metadata is and what it holds. (`/proc/cpuinfo` is one
15//! only where Detcore answers the guest's CPUID instruction from its own
16//! table, `procfs::RenderedFiles`; elsewhere it describes the host's CPU
17//! and the kernel opens it.) The backend opens no
18//! procfs file. The guest's descriptor is a placeholder: a memfd named
19//! `PLACEHOLDER_NAME_PREFIX` and the file's path, reopened read-only with
20//! the guest's own flags, a kernel object every backend has. Reopened that
21//! way, the kernel itself refuses writes, write locks and size changes, and
22//! reports the guest's status flags and a read-only descriptor link, as it
23//! does for the procfs file. Detcore answers the rest as Linux answers for a
24//! procfs file only global root may write (0444, or 0644 for the fixed
25//! tunables of `procfs::fixed`), to a process that is not global root. A
26//! per-process file (`/proc/<pid>/...`, `procfs::ProcfsTask`) belongs instead
27//! to its process's owner, the guest's own identity, so the guest owns it and
28//! its capabilities decide write access (`FileAccess`); a placeholder it opens
29//! for writing is reopened writable, and Detcore answers every write through
30//! it. Either way Detcore answers:
31//!
32//! - opening it, including the flag errors Linux reports;
33//! - reads, from the procfs snapshot, as for every procfs file;
34//! - `stat` of the path and `fstat` of the descriptor, with Detcore's procfs
35//!   device (`procfs_inode::VIRTUAL_PROC_DEVICE`) and an inode that its path
36//!   alone names (`procfs_inode::rendered_inode`), the same on every backend
37//!   and for every spelling that reaches the file;
38//! - the descriptor's link in any process's `/proc/*/fd`, and reopening
39//!   through it;
40//! - its `fdinfo`, `statfs`, and the calls such a procfs file refuses:
41//!   `mmap`, `fsync`, `fchmod`, extended attributes, file `ioctl`s and seals.
42//!
43//! Not under record and replay, whose Replayer stands its own placeholder in
44//! for every open, and not after the process changed its root with `chroot`,
45//! where the kernel decides whether the file exists. `epoll` refuses the
46//! placeholder (`EPERM`), which has no poll method, where it accepts the
47//! procfs file: a known limitation, see
48//! <https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4263>.
49// TODO-HUMAN-REVIEW(#4213): Review Detcore-rendered procfs files, a new
50// determinization strategy: Detcore serves a file the kernel never opened.
51

97/// The largest extended attribute value Linux accepts (`XATTR_SIZE_MAX`).
98const XATTR_SIZE_MAX: usize = 65536;
99
100/// Linux's longest extended-attribute name.
101const XATTR_NAME_MAX: usize = 255;
102
103/// The rendered path a path within a procfs mount names, if it names one the
104/// session renders (`files`). Detcore names procfs files by their path within
105/// the mount (`ProcfsEntry::At`), as `/sys/kernel/random/uuid`.
106pub(crate) fn rendered_path_within(within: &Path, files: RenderedFiles) -> Option<&'static Path> {
107    let relative = within.strip_prefix("/").unwrap_or(within);
108    ProcfsFile::from_path(&Path::new("/proc").join(relative))?.rendered_path(files)
109}
110
111/// The seals a placeholder carries where the backend supports seals: nothing
112/// can change the empty file, or its seals. (`MFD_NOEXEC_SEAL`, where the
113/// kernel knows it, adds `F_SEAL_EXEC`.)
114pub(crate) const PLACEHOLDER_SEALS: libc::c_int =
115    libc::F_SEAL_SEAL | libc::F_SEAL_SHRINK | libc::F_SEAL_GROW | libc::F_SEAL_WRITE;
116
117/// The bytes of the buffer a placeholder's memfd name, with its NUL, is built
118/// in: the most a memfd name holds (`NAME_MAX` less `memfd:`), and its NUL.
119const PLACEHOLDER_NAME_CAPACITY: usize = 250;
120
121/// How many low bits of a carried uptime the nanoseconds of a placeholder's
122/// modification time hold ([`carried_uptime_mtime`]): fewer than 10^9 values,
123/// as `utimensat` requires.
124const CARRIED_UPTIME_NSEC_BITS: u32 = 29;
125
126/// The modification time that makes a placeholder carry `uptime_seconds`, the
127/// uptime of the first read of a file whose snapshot that read takes
128/// (`ProcfsFile::snapshot_at_first_read`): a time before 1970, the uptime's
129/// high bits counting down from -1 seconds and its low bits in the
130/// nanoseconds. Every `u64` has one. A memfd's own modification time is the
131/// host's time at its creation, which Linux never lets be before 1970
132/// (`settimeofday` refuses a negative time), so no placeholder carries an
133/// uptime it was not given.
134fn carried_uptime_mtime(uptime_seconds: u64) -> Timespec {
135    Timespec {
136        tv_sec: -1 - (uptime_seconds >> CARRIED_UPTIME_NSEC_BITS) as i64,
137        tv_nsec: (uptime_seconds & ((1 << CARRIED_UPTIME_NSEC_BITS) - 1)) as i64,
138    }
139}
140
141/// Whether nothing can change what a descriptor number names between two of
142/// Detcore's injected calls: threads are sequentialized, so Detcore holds the
143/// scheduler turn across both, and no guest code runs inside a Tool handler
144/// (`BackendCapabilities::runs_guest_code_between_injections`: SaBRe's and
145/// in-guest LiteInst's accepted native signal handlers do).
146fn descriptor_stable_across_injections(sequentialized: bool, guest_code_between: bool) -> bool {
147    sequentialized && !guest_code_between
148}
149
150/// Whether the tool may read a task's kernel `comm` by path at all: not
151/// where it shares the guest's root (`shares_root`: DBT, SaBRe, LiteInst)
152/// and the guest has changed that root (`changed_root`), because the path is
153/// then looked up through a root the guest controls, where no check on the
154/// opened file authenticates the task (`Detcore::kernel_task_comm`). A
155/// tracer's own root is not the guest's.
156fn comm_read_is_trusted(shares_root: bool, changed_root: bool) -> bool {
157    !(shares_root && changed_root)
158}
159
160/// Whether the open file `file` is on a procfs (`fstatfs` reports
161/// `PROC_SUPER_MAGIC`), which refuses an ordinary file mounted over a
162/// task's `comm` path.
163fn comm_file_is_on_procfs(file: &crate::util::RawFd) -> bool {
164    file.fs_type()
165        .is_ok_and(|kind| kind == reverie::syscalls::linux_abi::PROC_SUPER_MAGIC as u64)
166}
167
168/// Whether a first read may read and write the uptime carried on the
169/// placeholder behind its descriptor (`Detcore::first_read_uptime`): only
170/// where the descriptor number keeps naming the same file between Detcore's
171/// look and its write ([`descriptor_stable_across_injections`]), and only if
172/// the descriptor is, by its kernel identity, the placeholder of `path` this
173/// tool process registered (`placeholder_identity`), so that neither another
174/// file's time is read as a carried uptime nor the carry written to another
175/// file.
176fn may_carry_uptime(stable: bool, identity: Option<&Path>, path: &Path) -> bool {
177    stable && identity == Some(path)
178}
179
180/// The uptime a placeholder whose modification time is `seconds` and
181/// `nanoseconds` carries ([`carried_uptime_mtime`]), if it carries one.
182fn carried_uptime(seconds: i64, nanoseconds: i64) -> Option<u64> {
183    if seconds >= 0 || !(0..1 << CARRIED_UPTIME_NSEC_BITS).contains(&nanoseconds) {
184        return None;
185    }
186    let high = (-1 - seconds) as u64;
187    (high < 1 << (64 - CARRIED_UPTIME_NSEC_BITS))
188        .then_some((high << CARRIED_UPTIME_NSEC_BITS) | nanoseconds as u64)
189}
190
191/// The placeholders this tool process made, by their memfd's kernel identity
192/// `(dev, ino)`, and the rendered file each stands in for. A kernel object
193/// with one of these identities is Detcore's placeholder however the guest
194/// reached it, which a memfd's name alone cannot show. Under backends whose
195/// tool runs in each guest process, a process knows the placeholders it made
196/// or inherited at `fork`, and those it rediscovers after `exec`.
197static PLACEHOLDERS: std::sync::Mutex<std::collections::BTreeMap<(u64, u64), &'static Path>> =
198    std::sync::Mutex::new(std::collections::BTreeMap::new());
199
200/// Record that the memfd `(dev, ino)` is a placeholder for `path`.
201pub(crate) fn register_placeholder(dev: u64, ino: u64, path: &'static Path) {
202    PLACEHOLDERS
203        .lock()
204        .expect("placeholder registry poisoned")
205        .insert((dev, ino), path);
206}
207
208/// The open file description each placeholder this tool process opened
209/// belongs to, and the snapshot its name carries, by the placeholder's kernel
210/// identity: every rendered open makes its own placeholder, so one identity
211/// is one open file description (`Detcore::adopt_received_placeholders`).
212/// One small entry per rendered open of the run, as `PLACEHOLDERS` keeps one;
213/// the handle keeps no description alive.
214static PLACEHOLDER_OPENS: std::sync::Mutex<
215    std::collections::BTreeMap<(u64, u64), PlaceholderOpen>,
216> = std::sync::Mutex::new(std::collections::BTreeMap::new());
217
218/// The open file description a placeholder belongs to, and the snapshot its
219/// name carries (`PLACEHOLDER_OPENS`).
220type PlaceholderOpen = (crate::fd::OpenFileHandle, Vec<u8>);
221
222/// Record that the placeholder `identity` belongs to the open file
223/// description `handle`, its name carrying `snapshot`.
224pub(crate) fn register_placeholder_open(
225    identity: (u64, u64),
226    handle: crate::fd::OpenFileHandle,
227    snapshot: Vec<u8>,
228) {
229    PLACEHOLDER_OPENS
230        .lock()
231        .expect("placeholder open registry poisoned")
232        .insert(identity, (handle, snapshot));
233}
234
235/// The open file description the placeholder `identity` belongs to, and the
236/// snapshot its name carries, if this tool process opened it.
237fn placeholder_open(identity: (u64, u64)) -> Option<PlaceholderOpen> {
238    PLACEHOLDER_OPENS
239        .lock()
240        .expect("placeholder open registry poisoned")
241        .get(&identity)
242        .cloned()
243}
244
245/// Whether this tool process knows any placeholder.
246pub(crate) fn placeholders_exist() -> bool {
247    !PLACEHOLDERS
248        .lock()
249        .expect("placeholder registry poisoned")
250        .is_empty()
251}
252
253/// The rendered file that the kernel object `(dev, ino)` is Detcore's
254/// placeholder for, if it is one.
255pub(crate) fn placeholder_identity(dev: u64, ino: u64) -> Option<&'static Path> {
256    PLACEHOLDERS
257        .lock()
258        .expect("placeholder registry poisoned")
259        .get(&(dev, ino))
260        .copied()
261}
262
263/// The procfs files Detcore renders from its run state, as opposed to the
264/// fixed files of `procfs::fixed`.
265const DYNAMIC_PATHS: [&std::ffi::CStr; 4] = [
266    c"/proc/sys/kernel/random/uuid",
267    c"/proc/sys/kernel/pty/nr",
268    c"/proc/uptime",
269    c"/proc/stat",
270];
271
272/// The paths of every procfs file a session renders (`files`):
273/// `/proc/cpuinfo` too where it renders that.
274pub(crate) fn rendered_paths(
275    files: RenderedFiles,
276) -> impl Iterator<Item = &'static std::ffi::CStr> {
277    DYNAMIC_PATHS
278        .into_iter()
279        .chain(files.cpuinfo.then_some(c"/proc/cpuinfo"))
280        .chain(
281            FixedProcfsFile::ALL
282                .into_iter()
283                .map(FixedProcfsFile::c_path),
284        )
285}
286
287/// The last component of a rendered file's path.
288fn rendered_name(path: &std::ffi::CStr) -> &[u8] {
289    let bytes = path.to_bytes();
290    bytes.rsplit(|byte| *byte == b'/').next().unwrap_or(bytes)
291}
292
293/// The rendered file that the kernel's file with metadata `stat` is, if it
294/// is one: the guest reached it through a spelling Detcore did not resolve,
295/// as a symbolic link or a directory listing, under a backend whose procfs
296/// files Detcore does not name by path
297/// (`procfs_inode::guest_tids_name_host_tasks`).
298///
299/// Each candidate costs a stat, so only a regular file with one link and
300/// permissions 0444 or 0644, as every rendered file is in the kernel's
301/// procfs, is checked, and an identity found not to be a rendered file is
302/// remembered (`NOT_RENDERED`): the checks of one procfs file cost their
303/// stats once per tool process. The search covers every file Detcore can
304/// render, so that what it remembers holds for any session; the answer is
305/// then the file only if this session renders it (`files`).
306pub(crate) fn rendered_path_of_raw(stat: &DetStat, files: RenderedFiles) -> Option<&'static Path> {
307    let has = |field| stat.mask.contains(field);
308    if (has(StatxMask::STATX_TYPE) && stat.mode & libc::S_IFMT != libc::S_IFREG)
309        || (has(StatxMask::STATX_NLINK) && stat.nlink != 1)
310        || (has(StatxMask::STATX_MODE) && !matches!(stat.mode & 0o7777, 0o444 | 0o644))
311    {
312        return None;
313    }
314    let identity = (stat.dev, stat.inode);
315    if NOT_RENDERED
316        .lock()
317        .expect("raw identity cache poisoned")
318        .contains(&identity)
319    {
320        return None;
321    }
322    let found = rendered_path_among(stat.dev, stat.inode, rendered_paths(RenderedFiles::ALL));
323    if found.is_none() && proc_device() == Some(stat.dev) {
324        NOT_RENDERED
325            .lock()
326            .expect("raw identity cache poisoned")
327            .insert(identity);
328    }
329    found
330        .and_then(ProcfsFile::from_path)
331        .and_then(|file| file.rendered_path(files))
332}
333
334/// The kernel procfs identities `(dev, ino)` this tool process found not to be
335/// a rendered file. procfs does not reuse an inode number during a run (sysctl
336/// and per-process inodes take `get_next_ino`; `/proc/version`'s and
337/// `/proc/cmdline`'s are fixed), so an identity that was not a rendered file
338/// never becomes one. A rendered file's inode that the kernel drops and makes
339/// again gets a new number, which is not here.
340static NOT_RENDERED: std::sync::Mutex<std::collections::BTreeSet<(u64, u64)>> =
341    std::sync::Mutex::new(std::collections::BTreeSet::new());
342
343/// The device of the guest's procfs mount, read once.
344pub(super) fn proc_device() -> Option<u64> {
345    static PROC_DEVICE: std::sync::OnceLock<Option<u64>> = std::sync::OnceLock::new();
346    *PROC_DEVICE.get_or_init(|| GuestProcfs::stat(c"/proc").ok().map(|stat| stat.st_dev))
347}
348
349/// The rendered file among `paths` that the kernel's procfs inode
350/// `(dev, ino)` is, if it is one.
351fn rendered_path_among(
352    dev: u64,
353    ino: u64,
354    mut paths: impl Iterator<Item = &'static std::ffi::CStr>,
355) -> Option<&'static Path> {
356    if proc_device() != Some(dev) {
357        return None;
358    }
359    paths.find_map(|path| {
360        let stat = GuestProcfs::stat(path).ok()?;
361        (stat.st_dev == dev && stat.st_ino == ino)
362            .then(|| Path::new(OsStr::from_bytes(path.to_bytes())))
363            .and_then(|path| ProcfsFile::from_path(path)?.rendered_path(RenderedFiles::ALL))
364    })
365}
366
367/// Facts about the guest's procfs mount that a rendered file shares with the
368/// kernel's procfs files. The tool runs in the guest's mount and user
369/// namespaces on every backend outside record and replay, so its `/proc` is
370/// the guest's. Read with raw system calls: under in-guest backends the tool
371/// shares the guest's C library.
372///
373/// Each fact is read once, when `/proc` is first reachable, and kept: the
374/// container's procfs mount does not change during the run, while the tool's
375/// own view of it can disappear, as when an in-guest tool runs in a process
376/// that later changed its root. A fact that cannot be read is the errno the
377/// guest's call gets, never a failure of the run.
378struct GuestProcfs;
379
380impl GuestProcfs {
381    fn stat(path: &std::ffi::CStr) -> Result<libc::stat, Errno> {
382        let mut stat = std::mem::MaybeUninit::<libc::stat>::zeroed();
383        // SAFETY: the path is NUL-terminated and `stat` is writable.
384        let result = unsafe {
385            raw_syscall(
386                libc::SYS_newfstatat,
387                [
388                    libc::AT_FDCWD as u64,
389                    path.as_ptr() as u64,
390                    stat.as_mut_ptr() as u64,
391                    0,
392                    0,
393                    0,
394                ],
395            )
396        };
397        raw_errno(result)?;
398        // SAFETY: the call succeeded and initialized the structure.
399        Ok(unsafe { stat.assume_init() })
400    }
401
402    /// The owner Linux reports for the guest's procfs sysctl files, and for
403    /// the other system-wide procfs files (`/proc/version`, `/proc/cmdline`):
404    /// global root as the guest's user namespace sees it.
405    fn sysctl_owner() -> Result<(u32, u32), Errno> {
406        static OWNER: std::sync::OnceLock<(u32, u32)> = std::sync::OnceLock::new();
407        if let Some(owner) = OWNER.get() {
408            return Ok(*owner);
409        }
410        let stat = Self::stat(c"/proc/sys/kernel")?;
411        Ok(*OWNER.get_or_init(|| (stat.st_uid, stat.st_gid)))
412    }
413
414    /// The guest's procfs statistics, and its mount flags (`f_flags`, which
415    /// the C library's structure keeps private).
416    fn statfs() -> Result<(libc::statfs, u64), Errno> {
417        // The kernel's `struct statfs` on x86_64: fifteen 64-bit words, of
418        // which the eleventh is `f_flags`.
419        const WORDS: usize = 15;
420        const _: () = assert!(std::mem::size_of::<libc::statfs>() == WORDS * 8);
421        static WORDS_READ: std::sync::OnceLock<[u64; WORDS]> = std::sync::OnceLock::new();
422        let words = match WORDS_READ.get() {
423            Some(words) => *words,
424            None => {
425                let mut words = [0_u64; WORDS];
426                // SAFETY: the path is NUL-terminated and `words` is writable
427                // and as large as the kernel's structure.
428                let result = unsafe {
429                    raw_syscall(
430                        libc::SYS_statfs,
431                        [
432                            c"/proc".as_ptr() as u64,
433                            words.as_mut_ptr() as u64,
434                            0,
435                            0,
436                            0,
437                            0,
438                        ],
439                    )
440                };
441                raw_errno(result)?;
442                *WORDS_READ.get_or_init(|| words)
443            }
444        };
445        let flags = words[10];
446        // SAFETY: the two have the same size and layout, of plain integers.
447        let statfs = unsafe { std::mem::transmute::<[u64; WORDS], libc::statfs>(words) };
448        Ok((statfs, flags))
449    }
450
451    /// The raw mount ID of the guest's procfs mount, as Linux reports it in
452    /// the fdinfo of a procfs file.
453    fn mount_id() -> Result<u64, Error> {
454        static MOUNT_ID: std::sync::OnceLock<u64> = std::sync::OnceLock::new();
455        if let Some(mount_id) = MOUNT_ID.get() {
456            return Ok(*mount_id);
457        }
458        let mut statx = std::mem::MaybeUninit::<reverie::syscalls::linux_abi::Statx>::zeroed();
459        // SAFETY: the path is NUL-terminated and `statx` is writable.
460        let result = unsafe {
461            raw_syscall(
462                libc::SYS_statx,
463                [
464                    libc::AT_FDCWD as u64,
465                    c"/proc".as_ptr() as u64,
466                    0,
467                    u64::from(reverie::syscalls::linux_abi::STATX_MNT_ID),
468                    statx.as_mut_ptr() as u64,
469                    0,
470                ],
471            )
472        };
473        raw_errno(result)?;
474        // SAFETY: the call succeeded and initialized the structure.
475        let statx = unsafe { statx.assume_init() };
476        if statx.stx_mask & reverie::syscalls::linux_abi::STATX_MNT_ID == 0 {
477            // A kernel too old to report it: the guest's read fails with
478            // the errno a missing feature gives, not the run.
479            return Err(Errno::EOPNOTSUPP.into());
480        }
481        Ok(*MOUNT_ID.get_or_init(|| statx.stx_mnt_id))
482    }
483
484    /// Whether the guest's user namespace maps user ID `id` (`gid` false) or
485    /// group ID `id` (`gid` true). -1 never names an ID.
486    fn maps_id(gid: bool, id: u32) -> bool {
487        static UID_MAP: std::sync::OnceLock<Vec<u8>> = std::sync::OnceLock::new();
488        static GID_MAP: std::sync::OnceLock<Vec<u8>> = std::sync::OnceLock::new();
489        if id == u32::MAX {
490            return false;
491        }
492        let (cell, path) = if gid {
493            (&GID_MAP, c"/proc/self/gid_map")
494        } else {
495            (&UID_MAP, c"/proc/self/uid_map")
496        };
497        let map = match cell.get() {
498            Some(map) => map,
499            None => match Self::read_small(path) {
500                Some(map) => cell.get_or_init(|| map),
501                None => return false,
502            },
503        };
504        String::from_utf8_lossy(map).lines().any(|line| {
505            let fields: Vec<u64> = line
506                .split_whitespace()
507                .filter_map(|field| field.parse().ok())
508                .collect();
509            matches!(fields.as_slice(), [inside, _, count]
510                if (*inside..inside.saturating_add(*count)).contains(&u64::from(id)))
511        })
512    }
513
514    /// The first 4 KiB of the small procfs file at `path`, read with raw
515    /// system calls.
516    fn read_small(path: &std::ffi::CStr) -> Option<Vec<u8>> {
517        // SAFETY: the path is NUL-terminated.
518        let fd = unsafe {
519            raw_syscall(
520                libc::SYS_openat,
521                [
522                    libc::AT_FDCWD as u64,
523                    path.as_ptr() as u64,
524                    (libc::O_RDONLY | libc::O_CLOEXEC) as u64,
525                    0,
526                    0,
527                    0,
528                ],
529            )
530        };
531        let fd = raw_errno(fd).ok()?;
532        let mut buffer = vec![0_u8; 4096];
533        // SAFETY: `buffer` is writable for its length.
534        let length = unsafe {
535            raw_syscall(
536                libc::SYS_read,
537                [fd, buffer.as_mut_ptr() as u64, buffer.len() as u64, 0, 0, 0],
538            )
539        };
540        // SAFETY: closing the descriptor opened above.
541        unsafe { raw_syscall(libc::SYS_close, [fd, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0]) };
542        buffer.truncate(raw_errno(length).ok()? as usize);
543        Some(buffer)
544    }
545
546    /// Read every fact while `/proc` is reachable.
547    fn remember() {
548        let _ = Self::sysctl_owner();
549        let _ = Self::statfs();
550        let _ = Self::mount_id();
551        let _ = Self::maps_id(false, 0);
552        let _ = Self::maps_id(true, 0);
553    }
554}
555
556/// A raw system call's result as an errno.
557fn raw_errno(result: i64) -> Result<u64, Errno> {
558    raw_result(result)
559        .map_err(|error| Errno::new(error.raw_os_error().expect("a raw result is an errno")))
560}
561
562/// The raw mount ID of the guest's procfs mount, for a rendered file's
563/// `fdinfo`.
564pub(super) fn guest_procfs_mount_id() -> Result<u64, Error> {
565    GuestProcfs::mount_id()
566}
567
568/// The metadata Linux gives the procfs file at `path`, owned by `owner`,
569/// with Detcore's procfs device, the inode its path names, and every time at
570/// `epoch`, on every backend.
571fn rendered_stat(path: &Path, epoch: Timespec, owner: (u32, u32)) -> DetStat {
572    let permissions = path
573        .to_str()
574        .and_then(FixedProcfsFile::from_path)
575        .map_or(0o444, FixedProcfsFile::permissions);
576    let permissions = if rendered_path_is_owner_only(path) {
577        0o400
578    } else if rendered_path_is_owner_writable(path) {
579        0o644
580    } else {
581        permissions
582    };
583    DetStat {
584        // What a procfs file's `statx` reports.
585        mask: StatxMask::STATX_BASIC_STATS | StatxMask::STATX_MNT_ID,
586        mode: libc::S_IFREG | permissions,
587        uid: owner.0,
588        gid: owner.1,
589        attributes: StatxAttributes::empty(),
590        // What `vfs_getattr_nosec` and `vfs_statx` report as supported.
591        attributes_mask: StatxAttributes::STATX_ATTR_AUTOMOUNT
592            | StatxAttributes::STATX_ATTR_MOUNT_ROOT
593            | StatxAttributes::STATX_ATTR_DAX,
594        dev: procfs_inode::VIRTUAL_PROC_DEVICE,
595        rdev: 0,
596        inode: procfs_inode::rendered_inode(path),
597        nlink: 1,
598        size: 0,
599        blksize: procfs_inode::PROC_BLOCK_SIZE,
600        blocks: 0,
601        atime: epoch,
602        btime: epoch,
603        ctime: epoch,
604        mtime: epoch,
605    }
606}
607
608/// Whether the rendered file `path` is readable by its owner only (0400): a
609/// process's `auxv` (`proc_pid_auxv`'s `S_IRUSR`).
610pub(super) fn rendered_path_is_owner_only(path: &Path) -> bool {
611    crate::procfs::per_process_name(path).is_some_and(|name| name.filename == "auxv")
612}
613
614/// Whether the rendered file `path` has Linux's mode 0644: a task's `comm`
615/// (`S_IRUGO|S_IWUSR`, fs/proc/base.c:3335 and 3677 in 6.17). Detcore
616/// renders it for reading only: it refuses a write open itself
617/// ([`asks_to_write_comm`]), and the access checks it answers grant write
618/// permission by `CAP_DAC_OVERRIDE`, as for every rendered file
619/// ([`FileAccess::may_write`]), not by the owner's write bit (declared).
620fn rendered_path_is_owner_writable(path: &Path) -> bool {
621    crate::procfs::per_process_name(path).is_some_and(|name| name.filename == "comm")
622}
623
624/// Where Detcore's lexical walk of an absolute `/proc` spelling that holds a
625/// `..` and ends in `comm` lands ([`walk_comm_alias`]): package K slice A+,
626/// so that no such spelling of a `comm` Detcore renders reaches the kernel
627/// beneath, whose answer to it is a host lookup's
628/// (<https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4263> is the general class).
629#[derive(Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
630pub(super) enum CommAliasWalk {
631    /// Not a spelling the walk decides: no `..`, not under `/proc` as
632    /// spelled, or a last component other than `comm`.
633    NotApplicable,
634    /// The `comm` of `task`, provided every task in `checks` exists, in
635    /// order (else Linux's lookup fails with ENOENT there first).
636    Comm {
637        checks: Vec<crate::procfs::BoundTask>,
638        task: crate::procfs::BoundTask,
639    },
640    /// Linux's lookup fails with `errno`, provided every task in `checks`
641    /// exists (else ENOENT first).
642    Fails {
643        checks: Vec<crate::procfs::BoundTask>,
644        errno: Errno,
645    },
646    /// The walk passes a component Detcore does not model (a link such as
647    /// `cwd` or `fd/N`, a directory it does not list, or a step out of
648    /// `/proc`), after which a `..` or the last component may land anywhere,
649    /// a rendered `comm` included; `checks` are the tasks it passed first.
650    Undetermined(Vec<crate::procfs::BoundTask>),
651}
652
653/// A procfs directory the walk models: Linux's `proc_pid_lookup` and
654/// `proc_task_lookup` decide what is below `/proc` and `task`.
655#[derive(Clone, Copy)]
656enum ProcNode {
657    /// The root directory, `/proc`'s parent.
658    Root,
659    Proc,
660    Pid(i32),
661    TaskDir(i32),
662    Task(i32, i32),
663}
664
665/// A task number as procfs's `name_to_int` takes one: decimal digits, no
666/// leading zero unless the number is 0, at most `i32::MAX`. `None` for any
667/// other name (which procfs's lookup of a number refuses with ENOENT).
668fn proc_number(name: &str) -> Option<i32> {
669    if name.is_empty()
670        || !name.bytes().all(|byte| byte.is_ascii_digit())
671        || (name.len() > 1 && name.starts_with('0'))
672    {
673        return None;
674    }
675    name.parse().ok()
676}
677
678/// Walk `path`, an absolute spelling, lexically through the procfs
679/// directories Detcore models, for a caller whose own process and thread
680/// are `own` (`self`, `thread-self`), as Linux's path walk goes through them:
681/// `..` goes to the parent directory (procfs has no other way back up:
682/// `self` and `thread-self` are links to `<pid>` and `<pid>/task/<tid>`, which
683/// the walk follows); a number below `/proc` names a task that must exist,
684/// and a number below `<pid>/task` a task of that process; a name below
685/// `task` that is no number is ENOENT (`proc_task_lookup`); `comm` is a file,
686/// so a component after it is ENOTDIR. Only a spelling under `/proc` as
687/// spelled, holding a `..`, whose last component is `comm`
688/// ([`CommAliasWalk::NotApplicable`] otherwise).
689pub(super) fn walk_comm_alias(path: &Path, own: (i32, i32)) -> CommAliasWalk {
690    use std::path::Component;
691    let components: Vec<Component<'_>> = path
692        .components()
693        .filter(|component| *component != Component::CurDir)
694        .collect();
695    let applies = path.is_absolute()
696        && components.get(1) == Some(&Component::Normal("proc".as_ref()))
697        && components.contains(&Component::ParentDir)
698        && components.last() == Some(&Component::Normal("comm".as_ref()));
699    if !applies {
700        return CommAliasWalk::NotApplicable;
701    }
702    let mut checks = Vec::new();
703    let mut node = ProcNode::Root;
704    let count = components.len();
705    for (index, component) in components.iter().enumerate() {
706        let last = index + 1 == count;
707        let name = match component {
708            Component::RootDir | Component::CurDir | Component::Prefix(_) => continue,
709            Component::ParentDir => {
710                node = match node {
711                    ProcNode::Root | ProcNode::Proc => ProcNode::Root,
712                    ProcNode::Pid(_) => ProcNode::Proc,
713                    ProcNode::TaskDir(process) => ProcNode::Pid(process),
714                    ProcNode::Task(process, _) => ProcNode::TaskDir(process),
715                };
716                continue;
717            }
718            Component::Normal(name) => match name.to_str() {
719                Some(name) => name,
720                None => return CommAliasWalk::Undetermined(checks),
721            },
722        };
723        node = match (node, name) {
724            (ProcNode::Root, "proc") => ProcNode::Proc,
725            (ProcNode::Proc, "self") => ProcNode::Pid(own.0),
726            (ProcNode::Proc, "thread-self") => ProcNode::Task(own.0, own.1),
727            (ProcNode::Proc, name) if name.bytes().all(|byte| byte.is_ascii_digit()) => {
728                match proc_number(name) {
729                    Some(process) => {
730                        checks.push(crate::procfs::BoundTask {
731                            process,
732                            thread: None,
733                        });
734                        ProcNode::Pid(process)
735                    }
736                    None => {
737                        return CommAliasWalk::Fails {
738                            checks,
739                            errno: Errno::ENOENT,
740                        };
741                    }
742                }
743            }
744            (ProcNode::Pid(process), "task") => ProcNode::TaskDir(process),
745            (ProcNode::TaskDir(process), name) => match proc_number(name) {
746                Some(thread) => {
747                    checks.push(crate::procfs::BoundTask {
748                        process,
749                        thread: Some(thread),
750                    });
751                    ProcNode::Task(process, thread)
752                }
753                None => {
754                    return CommAliasWalk::Fails {
755                        checks,
756                        errno: Errno::ENOENT,
757                    };
758                }
759            },
760            (ProcNode::Pid(process), "comm") | (ProcNode::Task(process, _), "comm") => {
761                if !last {
762                    return CommAliasWalk::Fails {
763                        checks,
764                        errno: Errno::ENOTDIR,
765                    };
766                }
767                let thread = match node {
768                    ProcNode::Task(_, thread) => Some(thread),
769                    _ => None,
770                };
771                return CommAliasWalk::Comm {
772                    checks,
773                    task: crate::procfs::BoundTask { process, thread },
774                };
775            }
776            _ => return CommAliasWalk::Undetermined(checks),
777        };
778    }
779    // The last component is `comm`, which every model node answers above.
780    CommAliasWalk::Undetermined(checks)
781}
782
783/// What [`Detcore::resolve_comm_alias`] makes of a spelling.
784pub(super) enum CommAlias {
785    /// Not a spelling it decides ([`CommAliasWalk::NotApplicable`]), or
786    /// Detcore does not render `comm` on this backend.
787    NotApplicable,
788    /// The spelling of a task's `comm`, without its `..`:
789    /// `/proc/<pid>/comm` or `/proc/<pid>/task/<tid>/comm`.
790    Resolved(std::path::PathBuf),
791    /// [`CommAliasWalk::Undetermined`]: answered by the kernel beneath for
792    /// reading, refused by name for writing
793    /// ([`Detcore::refuse_undetermined_comm_write`]).
794    Undetermined,
795}
796
797/// Whether an open with `flags` asks to write the file it opens: a write or
798/// read-write access mode, or `O_TRUNC`, which needs write permission
799/// (`may_open`), but not `O_PATH`, which opens for neither.
800pub(super) fn open_asks_to_write(flags: OFlag) -> bool {
801    !flags.contains(OFlag::O_PATH)
802        && (flags & OFlag::O_ACCMODE != OFlag::O_RDONLY || flags.contains(OFlag::O_TRUNC))
803}
804
805/// Whether an open of the rendered file `procfs` with `flags` is an open of
806/// a task's `comm` for writing (package K slice A+), which Detcore refuses
807/// with EACCES itself, before any injected call or request
808/// (`Detcore::open_rendered_procfs`), by every route that reaches a rendered
809/// `comm`: its path (`openat`, `open`, `creat`; `openat2` is Detcore's fixed
810/// ENOSYS) and a descriptor link to one (`/proc/*/fd/N`, `/dev/fd/N`).
811/// Detcore renders a `comm` from its record and writes no name, so the
812/// record never falls behind a write it did not see, and the answer depends
813/// on neither the host nor the kernel beneath (the KVM executor's own answer
814/// to that path is a host lookup's: its EACCES only where the host has the
815/// path). DECLARED: Linux renames the task (`comm_write`); Detcore's EACCES
816/// is its named difference.
817pub(super) fn asks_to_write_comm(procfs: &ProcfsFile, flags: OFlag) -> bool {
818    procfs.is_task_comm() && open_asks_to_write(flags)
819}
820
821/// What decides another process's open of a rendered `/proc/<pid>/auxv`
822/// ([`auxv_access_answer`]).
823#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, Eq, PartialEq)]
824pub(super) enum AuxvAccess {
825    /// The open's answer, as the kernel beneath gave it.
826    Answer(Result<(), Errno>),
827    /// The kernel beneath serves no `/proc` here: `CAP_SYS_PTRACE` in the
828    /// caller's own effective set decides.
829    CallerCapability,
830}
831
832/// What decides another process's open of a rendered `/proc/<pid>/auxv`
833/// ([`Detcore::ptrace_read_allowed`]), from the caller's own opens of the
834/// kernel's files: `target`, its open of the kernel's `/proc/<pid>/auxv`, and
835/// `serves`, its open of its own `/proc/thread-self/stat` (mode 0444, which
836/// no credential or dumpability refuses on a real kernel).
837/// - The target's opens: allowed.
838/// - It is refused while `serves` opens: the kernel beneath serves this
839///   caller's `/proc` and applied Linux's whole rule; its errno is the answer.
840/// - `serves` is refused with EACCES, the provider's backstop for every
841///   `/proc` file (KVM): the caller's `CAP_SYS_PTRACE` decides.
842/// - Any other errno of `serves` (EMFILE, ENFILE, ENOMEM, ...): the open's
843///   answer, as Linux's own open would fail with it.
844pub(super) fn auxv_access_answer(
845    target: Result<(), Errno>,
846    serves: Result<(), Errno>,
847) -> AuxvAccess {
848    match (target, serves) {
849        (Ok(()), _) => AuxvAccess::Answer(Ok(())),
850        (Err(refused), Ok(())) => AuxvAccess::Answer(Err(refused)),
851        (Err(_), Err(Errno::EACCES)) => AuxvAccess::CallerCapability,
852        (Err(_), Err(errno)) => AuxvAccess::Answer(Err(errno)),
853    }
854}
855
856/// The owner Linux reports for the rendered file `path`: a per-process file
857/// belongs to its process's owner (`task_dump_owner`), which for every guest
858/// process is Detcore's virtual root, 0 (the credential calls are emulated to
859/// it); a system-wide file to global root as the guest's user namespace sees
860/// it (`GuestProcfs::sysctl_owner`).
861fn rendered_owner(path: &Path) -> Result<(u32, u32), Errno> {
862    if crate::procfs::rendered_path_is_per_process(path) {
863        Ok((0, 0))
864    } else {
865        GuestProcfs::sysctl_owner()
866    }
867}
868
869/// What decides Linux's answer to a call that asks for write access to a
870/// rendered file, or for its owner: whether the caller owns it, and the
871/// capabilities that override its permissions.
872///
873/// A system-wide file belongs to global root, whom the guest's user
874/// namespace does not map, so no capability of the guest overrides its
875/// permissions and the guest is not its owner ([`FileAccess::GLOBAL_ROOT`]).
876/// A per-process file belongs to its process's owner, Detcore's virtual root,
877/// which is every guest's own identity, so the guest owns it, and
878/// `CAP_DAC_OVERRIDE` overrides its 0444 mode; `CAP_SYS_ADMIN` in procfs's
879/// user namespace decides a `security.` attribute. Both come from the
880/// guest's own capability sets, as `capget` reports them
881/// (`Detcore::rendered_file_access`).
882#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, Eq, PartialEq)]
883pub(super) struct FileAccess {
884    /// The caller owns the file.
885    owner: bool,
886    /// The caller may read the file: every rendered file's mode lets anyone
887    /// read it (0444) but a process's `auxv`'s (0400), which only its owner
888    /// may ([`rendered_path_is_owner_only`]).
889    readable: bool,
890    /// `CAP_DAC_OVERRIDE`: write access despite the mode.
891    dac_override: bool,
892    /// `CAP_SYS_ADMIN`: a `security.` attribute may be changed.
893    sys_admin: bool,
894}
895
896impl FileAccess {
897    /// A system-wide rendered file, owned by global root.
898    pub(super) const GLOBAL_ROOT: Self = Self {
899        owner: false,
900        readable: true,
901        dac_override: false,
902        sys_admin: false,
903    };
904
905    /// A per-process file, which the caller owns, with the capabilities
906    /// `capabilities` (a `capget` set) of the caller.
907    fn owned_with(capabilities: u64) -> Self {
908        Self {
909            owner: true,
910            readable: true,
911            dac_override: capabilities & (1 << CAP_DAC_OVERRIDE) != 0,
912            sys_admin: capabilities & (1 << CAP_SYS_ADMIN) != 0,
913        }
914    }
915
916    /// Whether the caller may write the file: every rendered file's mode
917    /// grants no one write access (0444; a fixed tunable's 0644 grants only
918    /// global root), so only `CAP_DAC_OVERRIDE` over an owner the caller's
919    /// namespace maps does.
920    fn may_write(&self) -> bool {
921        self.dac_override
922    }
923}
924
925/// `CAP_DAC_OVERRIDE`'s bit in a capability set.
926const CAP_DAC_OVERRIDE: u32 = 1;
927
928/// `CAP_SYS_ADMIN`'s bit in a capability set.
929const CAP_SYS_ADMIN: u32 = 21;
930
931/// `CAP_SYS_PTRACE`'s bit in a capability set.
932const CAP_SYS_PTRACE: u32 = 19;
933
934/// `_LINUX_CAPABILITY_VERSION_3`, whose `capget` reports two 32-bit words of
935/// each set.
936const LINUX_CAPABILITY_VERSION_3: u32 = 0x2008_0522;
937
938/// Which of the caller's capability sets a check uses: the effective set, or,
939/// for `access` and `faccessat` without `AT_EACCESS`, the set Linux checks
940/// with the real IDs (`access_override_creds`): for a real user ID of 0 in
941/// the caller's namespace, which every guest's is, the permitted set.
942#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, Eq, PartialEq)]
943pub(super) enum CapabilitySet {
944    Effective,
945    Permitted,
946}
947
948/// The configured epoch as a stat time.
949pub(super) fn epoch_time(epoch: chrono::DateTime<chrono::Utc>) -> Timespec {
950    Timespec {
951        tv_sec: epoch.timestamp(),
952        tv_nsec: i64::from(epoch.timestamp_subsec_nanos()),
953    }
954}
955
956/// Whether the final component of `path` is followed by a slash, which only a
957/// directory accepts.
958fn names_a_directory(path: &Path) -> bool {
959    let bytes = path.as_os_str().as_bytes();
960    bytes.ends_with(b"/") || bytes.ends_with(b"/.")
961}
962
963/// Linux's answer to opening an existing rendered procfs file (writable by
964/// no one but global root, `access`) with `flags`, from a process that is not
965/// global root, before it opens anything: `build_open_flags`, then the
966/// lookup's `O_EXCL` and type checks, then `may_open` and `do_dentry_open`.
967/// On success, the flags the placeholder is reopened with: read-only, or with
968/// the guest's access mode where it may write (a per-process file under
969/// `CAP_DAC_OVERRIDE`, whose writes Detcore then answers,
970/// `answer_rendered_procfs_operation`), never with `O_TRUNC`, which truncates
971/// nothing in procfs.
972fn rendered_open_flags(flags: OFlag, access: FileAccess) -> Result<OFlag, Errno> {
973    if let Some(errno) = open_flags_refused(flags) {
974        return Err(errno);
975    }
976    // `O_PATH` keeps only its own flags (`O_TMPFILE` includes `O_DIRECTORY`).
977    if flags.contains(OFlag::O_PATH) {
978        if flags.contains(OFlag::O_DIRECTORY) {
979            return Err(Errno::ENOTDIR);
980        }
981        return Ok(OFlag::O_PATH | (flags & OFlag::O_CLOEXEC));
982    }
983    // A temporary file is made in a directory, which a file is not.
984    if flags.contains(OFlag::O_TMPFILE) {
985        return Err(Errno::ENOTDIR);
986    }
987    if flags.contains(OFlag::O_CREAT | OFlag::O_EXCL) {
988        return Err(Errno::EEXIST);
989    }
990    if flags.contains(OFlag::O_DIRECTORY) {
991        return Err(Errno::ENOTDIR);
992    }
993    // No mode bit allows writing; `O_TRUNC` asks for write access. Reading
994    // an owner-only file (`auxv`) needs its owner (`FileAccess::readable`).
995    let accmode = flags & OFlag::O_ACCMODE;
996    let reads =
997        accmode == OFlag::O_RDONLY || accmode == OFlag::O_RDWR || accmode == OFlag::O_ACCMODE;
998    let writes = accmode != OFlag::O_RDONLY || flags.contains(OFlag::O_TRUNC);
999    if (writes && !access.may_write()) || (reads && !access.readable) {
1000        return Err(Errno::EACCES);
1001    }
1002    // `O_NOATIME` needs the file's owner.
1003    if flags.contains(OFlag::O_NOATIME) && !access.owner {
1004        return Err(Errno::EPERM);
1005    }
1006    // Procfs files cannot do direct I/O.
1007    if flags.contains(OFlag::O_DIRECT) {
1008        return Err(Errno::EINVAL);
1009    }
1010    let kept = OFlag::O_APPEND
1011        | OFlag::O_ASYNC
1012        | OFlag::O_CLOEXEC
1013        | OFlag::O_DSYNC
1014        | OFlag::O_LARGEFILE
1015        | OFlag::O_NOATIME
1016        | OFlag::O_NONBLOCK
1017        | OFlag::O_SYNC;
1018    let mode = flags & OFlag::O_ACCMODE;
1019    Ok(mode | (flags & kept))
1020}
1021
1022/// What `build_open_flags` refuses with EINVAL before any path is looked up,
1023/// once `build_open_how` has kept only `O_PATH`'s own flags: a malformed
1024/// `__O_TMPFILE` (it needs `O_DIRECTORY` and no `O_CREAT`), a temporary file
1025/// without write access, and `O_CREAT` with `O_DIRECTORY`.
1026pub(super) fn open_flags_refused(flags: OFlag) -> Option<Errno> {
1027    if flags.contains(OFlag::O_PATH) {
1028        return None;
1029    }
1030    let tmpfile = OFlag::O_TMPFILE & !OFlag::O_DIRECTORY;
1031    if flags.intersects(tmpfile)
1032        && (flags & (OFlag::O_TMPFILE | OFlag::O_CREAT) != OFlag::O_TMPFILE
1033            || flags & OFlag::O_ACCMODE == OFlag::O_RDONLY)
1034    {
1035        return Some(Errno::EINVAL);
1036    }
1037    flags
1038        .contains(OFlag::O_CREAT | OFlag::O_DIRECTORY)
1039        .then_some(Errno::EINVAL)
1040}
1041
1042/// The checks `do_mmap` makes of a mapping of a procfs file before it looks
1043/// for a mapping the range overlaps (`MAP_FIXED_NOREPLACE`'s `EEXIST`), in
1044/// its order: the arguments and the address. Returns the page-aligned length.
1045fn rendered_mmap_address_checks(
1046    addr: usize,
1047    len: usize,
1048    offset: libc::off_t,
1049    flags: MapFlags,
1050) -> Result<usize, Errno> {
1051    // `ksys_mmap_pgoff` refuses MAP_HUGETLB for a file that is not on
1052    // hugetlbfs before `do_mmap` looks at anything.
1053    if flags.contains(MapFlags::MAP_HUGETLB) || len == 0 || offset & 4095 != 0 {
1054        return Err(Errno::EINVAL);
1055    }
1056    // A length that cannot fit the address space fails before the file is
1057    // looked at (`PAGE_ALIGN` overflowing, then `__get_unmapped_area`).
1058    // The 47-bit user address space of x86_64 without 5-level paging, which
1059    // Hermit assumes: on a host with LA57, Linux would take a higher fixed
1060    // address and answer ENODEV where this answers ENOMEM.
1061    const TASK_SIZE: usize = 0x7fff_ffff_f000;
1062    let Some(len) = len.checked_add(4095).map(|length| length & !4095) else {
1063        return Err(Errno::ENOMEM);
1064    };
1065    if len > TASK_SIZE {
1066        return Err(Errno::ENOMEM);
1067    }
1068    // A fixed address is taken as given; then it must leave room for the
1069    // mapping below TASK_SIZE, and be page aligned (measured: EINVAL before
1070    // ENODEV).
1071    if flags.intersects(MapFlags::MAP_FIXED | MapFlags::MAP_FIXED_NOREPLACE) {
1072        if addr > TASK_SIZE - len {
1073            return Err(Errno::ENOMEM);
1074        }
1075        if addr & 4095 != 0 {
1076            return Err(Errno::EINVAL);
1077        }
1078        // `security_mmap_addr`: below `vm.mmap_min_addr` (the machine's, which
1079        // does not depend on the configuration) needs CAP_SYS_RAWIO, which a
1080        // guest lacks.
1081        let mmap_min_addr = crate::machine::MachineDescription::default()
1082            .limits
1083            .mmap_min_addr;
1084        if (addr as u64) < mmap_min_addr {
1085            return Err(Errno::EPERM);
1086        }
1087    }
1088    Ok(len)
1089}
1090
1091/// The checks `do_mmap` and `mmap_region` make of a mapping of a procfs file
1092/// Detcore renders after the overlap check, in their order, and the error the
1093/// mapping ends with: a sysctl file has no `mmap` method (`ENODEV`); a
1094/// system-wide `seq_file` has `proc_reg_mmap` (`procfs::rendered_path_is_seq_file`),
1095/// so a growing-down mapping is refused (`EINVAL`) and otherwise the method
1096/// finds nothing behind it (`EIO`); a per-process file's operations have no
1097/// `mmap` (`ENODEV`, `procfs::rendered_path_is_per_process`). `len` is page
1098/// aligned. `noexec` says whether procfs is mounted `noexec`. `readable` and
1099/// `writable` are the descriptor's access mode: only a per-process file's can
1100/// be writable, or not readable.
1101fn rendered_mmap_file_checks(
1102    len: usize,
1103    offset: libc::off_t,
1104    prot: ProtFlags,
1105    flags: MapFlags,
1106    noexec: bool,
1107    seq_file: bool,
1108    (readable, writable): (bool, bool),
1109) -> Errno {
1110    // `file_mmap_ok`: the mapping must end within the largest offset a
1111    // regular file can have (`MAX_LFS_FILESIZE`), the offset taken unsigned.
1112    const MAX_LFS_FILESIZE: u64 = i64::MAX as u64;
1113    if offset as u64 / 4096 > (MAX_LFS_FILESIZE - len as u64) >> 12 {
1114        return Errno::EOVERFLOW;
1115    }
1116    let map_type = flags.bits() & libc::MAP_TYPE;
1117    if map_type != libc::MAP_SHARED
1118        && map_type != libc::MAP_PRIVATE
1119        && map_type != libc::MAP_SHARED_VALIDATE
1120    {
1121        return Errno::EINVAL;
1122    }
1123    // MAP_SHARED_VALIDATE refuses every flag outside `LEGACY_MAP_MASK`, as
1124    // procfs supports none (`mmap_supported_flags`): MAP_SYNC and
1125    // MAP_FIXED_NOREPLACE among them.
1126    if map_type == libc::MAP_SHARED_VALIDATE && flags.bits() & !LEGACY_MAP_MASK != 0 {
1127        return Errno::EOPNOTSUPP;
1128    }
1129    if map_type != libc::MAP_PRIVATE && prot.contains(ProtFlags::PROT_WRITE) && !writable {
1130        return Errno::EACCES;
1131    }
1132    // Every mapping needs a readable descriptor (`MAP_SHARED` falls through
1133    // to `MAP_PRIVATE`'s check).
1134    if !readable {
1135        return Errno::EACCES;
1136    }
1137    if noexec && prot.contains(ProtFlags::PROT_EXEC) {
1138        return Errno::EPERM;
1139    }
1140    if !seq_file {
1141        return Errno::ENODEV;
1142    }
1143    if flags.contains(MapFlags::MAP_GROWSDOWN) {
1144        return Errno::EINVAL;
1145    }
1146    Errno::EIO
1147}
1148
1149/// `include/linux/mman.h`'s `LEGACY_MAP_MASK`: the flags every file accepts
1150/// under MAP_SHARED_VALIDATE, with the type bits.
1151const LEGACY_MAP_MASK: libc::c_int = libc::MAP_SHARED
1152    | libc::MAP_PRIVATE
1153    | libc::MAP_FIXED
1154    | libc::MAP_ANONYMOUS
1155    | libc::MAP_DENYWRITE
1156    | libc::MAP_EXECUTABLE
1157    | 0x400_0000 // MAP_UNINITIALIZED
1158    | libc::MAP_GROWSDOWN
1159    | libc::MAP_LOCKED
1160    | libc::MAP_NORESERVE
1161    | libc::MAP_POPULATE
1162    | libc::MAP_NONBLOCK
1163    | libc::MAP_STACK
1164    | libc::MAP_HUGETLB
1165    | libc::MAP_32BIT
1166    | 0x80 // MAP_ABOVE4G
1167    | (21 << 26) // MAP_HUGE_2MB
1168    | (30 << 26); // MAP_HUGE_1GB
1169
1170/// Linux's answer to mapping a procfs file Detcore renders, where the range
1171/// overlaps no mapping (`MAP_FIXED_NOREPLACE` then has nothing to refuse):
1172/// [`rendered_mmap_address_checks`], then [`rendered_mmap_file_checks`]. The
1173/// unit tests' view of `answer_rendered_procfs_mapping`.
1174#[cfg(test)]
1175fn rendered_mmap_answer(
1176    addr: usize,
1177    len: usize,
1178    offset: libc::off_t,
1179    prot: ProtFlags,
1180    flags: MapFlags,
1181    noexec: bool,
1182    seq_file: bool,
1183) -> Result<i64, Errno> {
1184    let len = rendered_mmap_address_checks(addr, len, offset, flags)?;
1185    Err(rendered_mmap_file_checks(
1186        len,
1187        offset,
1188        prot,
1189        flags,
1190        noexec,
1191        seq_file,
1192        (true, false),
1193    ))
1194}
1195
1196/// Linux's answer to a positioned write of a rendered `seq_file`, which is
1197/// opened without `FMODE_PWRITE` (`seq_open`): a negative position is
1198/// `EINVAL`, then `ESPIPE`, before the descriptor's access mode is looked at.
1199/// `None` for `pwritev2` at position -1, which writes at the file position
1200/// as `writev` does and is refused by the read-only placeholder as Linux
1201/// refuses it (`EBADF`).
1202fn rendered_seq_file_pwrite_answer(sysno: Sysno, position: i64) -> Option<Result<i64, Errno>> {
1203    if sysno == Sysno::pwritev2 && position == -1 {
1204        return None;
1205    }
1206    Some(Err(if position < 0 {
1207        Errno::EINVAL
1208    } else {
1209        Errno::ESPIPE
1210    }))
1211}
1212
1213/// Linux's answer to setting the extended attribute `name` of a procfs file,
1214/// which has no attribute handlers, to `value` (`size` bytes, `None` if the
1215/// guest's buffer cannot be read), or to removing it (`size` is `None`), in
1216/// the kernel's order: the name, the size, the value, then the namespace.
1217/// Measured against /proc/sys/kernel/random/uuid as a user its user namespace
1218/// does not own ([`FileAccess::GLOBAL_ROOT`]), and against /proc/self/cgroup
1219/// as its owner with and without the capabilities `access` names.
1220fn xattr_change_answer(
1221    name: &[u8],
1222    size: Option<usize>,
1223    value: Option<&[u8]>,
1224    access: FileAccess,
1225) -> Result<i64, Errno> {
1226    if name.is_empty() || name.len() > 255 {
1227        return Err(Errno::ERANGE);
1228    }
1229    if size.is_some_and(|size| size > XATTR_SIZE_MAX) {
1230        return Err(Errno::E2BIG);
1231    }
1232    if size.is_some_and(|size| size > 0) && value.is_none() {
1233        return Err(Errno::EFAULT);
1234    }
1235    let setting = size.is_some();
1236    if name.starts_with(b"trusted.") {
1237        Err(Errno::EPERM)
1238    } else if name == b"system.posix_acl_access" || name == b"system.posix_acl_default" {
1239        // A list is parsed first; procfs then has no way to keep it.
1240        if setting && posix_acl_parser_refuses(value.unwrap_or_default()) {
1241            Err(Errno::EINVAL)
1242        } else {
1243            Err(Errno::EOPNOTSUPP)
1244        }
1245    } else if name == b"security.capability" && setting {
1246        // The capability module parses the value first.
1247        Err(Errno::EINVAL)
1248    } else if name.starts_with(b"security.") {
1249        // Changing a security attribute needs CAP_SYS_ADMIN; procfs then has
1250        // no way to keep it.
1251        Err(if access.sys_admin {
1252            Errno::EOPNOTSUPP
1253        } else {
1254            Errno::EPERM
1255        })
1256    } else if name.starts_with(b"system.") {
1257        Err(Errno::EOPNOTSUPP)
1258    } else {
1259        // `user.` and unknown namespaces need write permission first.
1260        Err(if access.may_write() {
1261            Errno::EOPNOTSUPP
1262        } else {
1263            Errno::EACCES
1264        })
1265    }
1266}
1267
1268/// Whether the kernel's parser (`posix_acl_from_xattr`) refuses `value` as a
1269/// POSIX access control list: a value too short for its header, a size that
1270/// is not a whole number of entries, an unknown tag, or a named user or group
1271/// the caller's user namespace does not map. A bad version, an empty value or
1272/// an empty list are not refused here; procfs then has no way to keep any
1273/// list (EOPNOTSUPP). The checks of a list's order, permissions and counts
1274/// (`posix_acl_valid`) come after that, so a procfs file never reaches them.
1275/// Measured on Linux 7.1, as a user and as root in a user namespace.
1276fn posix_acl_parser_refuses(value: &[u8]) -> bool {
1277    if value.is_empty() {
1278        return false;
1279    }
1280    let Some((header, entries)) = value.split_first_chunk::<4>() else {
1281        return true;
1282    };
1283    if u32::from_le_bytes(*header) != 2 {
1284        return false;
1285    }
1286    if entries.len() % 8 != 0 {
1287        return true;
1288    }
1289    entries.as_chunks::<8>().0.iter().any(|entry| {
1290        let tag = u16::from_le_bytes([entry[0], entry[1]]);
1291        let id = u32::from_le_bytes([entry[4], entry[5], entry[6], entry[7]]);
1292        match tag {
1293            // ACL_USER_OBJ, ACL_GROUP_OBJ, ACL_MASK, ACL_OTHER.
1294            0x01 | 0x04 | 0x10 | 0x20 => false,
1295            0x02 => !GuestProcfs::maps_id(false, id),
1296            0x08 => !GuestProcfs::maps_id(true, id),
1297            _ => true,
1298        }
1299    })
1300}
1301
1302/// Linux's answer to reading the extended attribute `name` of a procfs file,
1303/// which has no attribute handlers: a `trusted.` attribute is invisible
1304/// without the global capability; a `security.` or `system.` one is left to
1305/// the file system; any other name first needs read permission on the file
1306/// (`xattr_permission`), which a `readable` of false (a zombie's owner-only
1307/// file) refuses. Measured natively against a zombie's `/proc/<pid>/auxv`.
1308fn xattr_get_answer(name: &[u8], readable: bool) -> Result<i64, Errno> {
1309    if name.is_empty() || name.len() > 255 {
1310        Err(Errno::ERANGE)
1311    } else if name.starts_with(b"trusted.") {
1312        Err(Errno::ENODATA)
1313    } else if !readable && !name.starts_with(b"security.") && !name.starts_with(b"system.") {
1314        Err(Errno::EACCES)
1315    } else {
1316        Err(Errno::EOPNOTSUPP)
1317    }
1318}
1319
1320/// A NUL-terminated string in a fixed buffer, to push on the guest's stack.
1321pub(super) fn c_string<const N: usize>(text: &[u8]) -> [u8; N] {
1322    assert!(text.len() < N, "{} bytes do not fit in {N}", text.len());
1323    let mut buffer = [0; N];
1324    buffer[..text.len()].copy_from_slice(text);
1325    buffer
1326}
1327
1328/// Write `link`, truncated to `buffer_len`, as `readlink` writes a link.
1329fn write_link<M: MemoryAccess>(
1330    memory: &mut M,
1331    link: &Path,
1332    buffer: Option<AddrMut<'_, libc::c_char>>,
1333    buffer_len: usize,
1334) -> Result<i64, Error> {
1335    let link = link.as_os_str().as_bytes();
1336    let copied = link.len().min(buffer_len);
1337    let buffer = buffer.ok_or(Errno::EFAULT)?;
1338    memory.write_exact(buffer.cast::<u8>(), &link[..copied])?;
1339    Ok(copied as i64)
1340}
1341
1342/// A rendered procfs file that a path names, and how.
1343pub(super) struct RenderedTarget {
1344    pub(super) procfs: ProcfsFile,
1345    /// Through a descriptor link, `/proc/*/fd/N` or `/dev/fd/N`.
1346    pub(super) via_link: bool,
1347}
1348
1349impl<T: RecordOrReplay> Detcore<T> {
1350    /// Whether Detcore renders procfs files for this guest thread now.
1351    pub(super) fn renders_procfs<G: Guest<Self>>(&self, guest: &G) -> bool {
1352        !self.cfg.recordreplay_modes
1353            && !guest
1354                .thread_state()
1355                .changed_root
1356                .load(std::sync::atomic::Ordering::SeqCst)
1357    }
1358
1359    /// What Detcore's process table says now of the task the rendered
1360    /// per-process path `path` names, and whether that is still the task the
1361    /// path was bound to (it exists and its numbers have the incarnations
1362    /// they had: no other task has taken them since). `None` for a path that
1363    /// names no per-process file.
1364    pub(super) async fn rendered_task_state<G: Guest<Self>>(
1365        &self,
1366        guest: &mut G,
1367        path: &Path,
1368    ) -> Option<(crate::procfs::listing::ProcfsTaskAnswer, bool)> {
1369        let name = crate::procfs::per_process_name(path)?;
1370        let answer = crate::tool_global::procfs_task(guest, name.task).await;
1371        let current = name
1372            .incarnation
1373            .is_some_and(|bound| answer.still_names(bound));
1374        Some((answer, current))
1375    }
1376
1377    /// Linux's answer to taking a new snapshot of the rendered file open as
1378    /// `fd`, where it is a per-process file: its `show` method finds its task
1379    /// again (`get_proc_task`), and a task that is gone, or whose pid another
1380    /// task now holds, is ESRCH. Linux takes a snapshot at a read with no
1381    /// buffered bytes from the start, and at a read or seek to a position the
1382    /// buffer does not hold (`traverse`). Only a file whose `show` runs at a
1383    /// read (`ProcfsFile::shows_at_read`): a process's `auxv` reads the
1384    /// memory map its open pinned, whatever became of the task since.
1385    pub(crate) async fn require_rendered_task<G: Guest<Self>>(
1386        &self,
1387        guest: &mut G,
1388        fd: RawFd,
1389    ) -> Result<(), Errno> {
1390        let Some(path) = guest
1391            .thread_state()
1392            .with_detfd(fd, |detfd| detfd.procfs_rendered_path())
1393            .ok()
1394            .flatten()
1395        else {
1396            return Ok(());
1397        };
1398        if !ProcfsFile::from_rendered_path(path).is_some_and(|file| file.shows_at_read()) {
1399            return Ok(());
1400        }
1401        match self.rendered_task_state(guest, path).await {
1402            Some((_, false)) => Err(Errno::ESRCH),
1403            _ => Ok(()),
1404        }
1405    }
1406
1407    /// The owner Linux reports for the rendered file `path`
1408    /// ([`rendered_owner`]), for a per-process file from its task's lifecycle
1409    /// in Detcore's process table: Detcore's virtual root while the task has
1410    /// its memory map, and global root once it has exited (a zombie, whose
1411    /// files Linux gives to global root) or is gone.
1412    pub(super) async fn rendered_file_owner<G: Guest<Self>>(
1413        &self,
1414        guest: &mut G,
1415        path: &Path,
1416    ) -> Result<(u32, u32), Errno> {
1417        match self.rendered_task_state(guest, path).await {
1418            Some((answer, true)) if !answer.without_mm => Ok((0, 0)),
1419            Some(_) => GuestProcfs::sysctl_owner(),
1420            None => rendered_owner(path),
1421        }
1422    }
1423
1424    /// The metadata of the rendered file `path` ([`rendered_stat`]), with its
1425    /// owner ([`Self::rendered_file_owner`]) and, for a per-process file, the
1426    /// modification time its owner set, which Detcore keeps in its global
1427    /// tool for every alias of the file (`RenderedFileState`).
1428    pub(super) async fn rendered_metadata<G: Guest<Self>>(
1429        &self,
1430        guest: &mut G,
1431        path: &Path,
1432    ) -> Result<DetStat, Error> {
1433        let owner = self.rendered_file_owner(guest, path).await?;
1434        let epoch = epoch_time(guest.config().epoch);
1435        let mut stat = rendered_stat(path, epoch, owner);
1436        if crate::procfs::per_process_name(path).is_some() {
1437            let state = crate::tool_global::rendered_file_state(
1438                guest,
1439                stat.inode,
1440                crate::procfs::listing::RenderedFileChange::Read,
1441            )
1442            .await;
1443            if let Some((tv_sec, tv_nsec)) = state.mtime {
1444                stat.mtime = Timespec { tv_sec, tv_nsec };
1445            }
1446        }
1447        Ok(stat)
1448    }
1449
1450    /// The procfs file Detcore renders that `path`, absolute in the guest's
1451    /// view, names: directly, or as this process's `/proc/*/fd/N` or
1452    /// `/dev/fd/N` link to a descriptor Detcore rendered. A spelling that
1453    /// asks for a directory fails as Linux fails it.
1454    pub(super) async fn rendered_procfs_at<G: Guest<Self>>(
1455        &self,
1456        guest: &mut G,
1457        path: &Path,
1458    ) -> Result<Option<RenderedTarget>, Error> {
1459        // A `..` component is the kernel's to resolve: it walks through the
1460        // components before it, which need not exist or be directories, while
1461        // Detcore's names drop them. (A valid `..` spelling of a rendered file
1462        // so reaches the kernel's own: a known limitation, see
1463        // <https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4263>.) Except a
1464        // spelling of a task's `comm` where Detcore renders it (package K
1465        // slice A+): Detcore walks it itself, checking every task it passes
1466        // as Linux's lookup does, and answers it as the file it reaches, or
1467        // fails it as Linux would ([`Self::resolve_comm_alias`]).
1468        if !self.renders_procfs(guest) {
1469            return Ok(None);
1470        }
1471        let resolved;
1472        let path = if path
1473            .components()
1474            .any(|component| component == std::path::Component::ParentDir)
1475        {
1476            match self.resolve_comm_alias(guest, path).await? {
1477                CommAlias::Resolved(comm) => {
1478                    resolved = comm;
1479                    resolved.as_path()
1480                }
1481                CommAlias::NotApplicable | CommAlias::Undetermined => return Ok(None),
1482            }
1483        } else {
1484            path
1485        };
1486        if let Some(mut procfs) = ProcfsFile::from_path(path) {
1487            // A per-process file belongs to the task its path names, which
1488            // Detcore's process table decides exists, as Linux's lookup of
1489            // `/proc/<pid>` and `task/<tid>` does: a task that is not there
1490            // has no file (ENOENT), before any other check. In a nested PID
1491            // namespace, whose numbers are its own, the kernel decides.
1492            // AUTONOMOUS-BOT-IMPLEMENTED
1493            // TODO-HUMAN-REVIEW(#4213): Review per-process rendered files: the
1494            // task a spelling names, its existence and identity from Detcore's
1495            // process table, and its owner's access (FileAccess).
1496            if procfs.per_process_name().is_some() {
1497                if self.kernel_serves_every_comm(&procfs) {
1498                    return Ok(None);
1499                }
1500                let Some((pid, tid)) = self.procfs_caller_ids(guest) else {
1501                    return Ok(None);
1502                };
1503                procfs.bind_task(pid, tid);
1504                let Some(task) = procfs.bound_task() else {
1505                    return Err(Errno::ENOENT.into());
1506                };
1507                let answer = crate::tool_global::procfs_task(guest, task).await;
1508                if !answer.exists {
1509                    return Err(Errno::ENOENT.into());
1510                }
1511                let without_mm = answer.without_mm;
1512                self.bind_task_identity(guest, &mut procfs, answer).await?;
1513                if self.kept_by_kernel(guest, &procfs, without_mm).await {
1514                    return Ok(None);
1515                }
1516                // A process's `mountinfo` or `mounts` shows the mounts below
1517                // its own root: where that process (a child that chrooted)
1518                // changed its root, the kernel serves its view, and the open
1519                // pins it (`Detcore::mount_view_is_kernel`). The caller's own
1520                // root is checked above (`renders_procfs`).
1521                if procfs.mount_table_rendering().is_some()
1522                    && crate::tool_global::root_changed(
1523                        guest,
1524                        detcore_model::pid::DetPid::from_raw(task.thread.unwrap_or(task.process)),
1525                    )
1526                    .await
1527                {
1528                    return Ok(None);
1529                }
1530            }
1531            if procfs.rendered_path(self.rendered_files(guest)).is_none() {
1532                return Ok(None);
1533            }
1534            if names_a_directory(path) {
1535                return Err(Errno::ENOTDIR.into());
1536            }
1537            // `/proc/mounts` is a symbolic link to the caller's `self/mounts`:
1538            // its own metadata, `readlink` and `O_NOFOLLOW` are the link's.
1539            let via_link = crate::procfs::is_proc_mounts_link(path);
1540            return Ok(Some(RenderedTarget { procfs, via_link }));
1541        }
1542        // procfs names descriptors without leading zeros, so `fd/03` does not
1543        // exist even where descriptor 3 is a placeholder. Detcore answers so
1544        // on every backend (KVM's own procfs resolves the spelling).
1545        if let Some((subject, fd)) = proc_fd_target_with_leading_zeros(path) {
1546            let own = match subject {
1547                Some(subject) => self.guest_visible_own_pid(guest).await? == i64::from(subject),
1548                None => true,
1549            };
1550            let rendered = guest
1551                .thread_state()
1552                .with_detfd(fd, |detfd| detfd.procfs_rendered_path().is_some())
1553                .unwrap_or(false);
1554            if own && rendered {
1555                return Err(Errno::ENOENT.into());
1556            }
1557            return Ok(None);
1558        }
1559        let Some((subject, fd)) = proc_fd_target(path) else {
1560            return Ok(None);
1561        };
1562        if let Some(subject) = subject
1563            && self.guest_visible_own_pid(guest).await? != i64::from(subject)
1564        {
1565            return Ok(None);
1566        }
1567        // The descriptor is a placeholder, so the file it reopens is one
1568        // Detcore opened as rendered: the target keeps that state, whatever
1569        // the session renders by path.
1570        let target = guest
1571            .thread_state()
1572            .with_detfd(fd, |detfd| detfd.procfs_rendered_path())
1573            .ok()
1574            .flatten()
1575            .and_then(ProcfsFile::from_rendered_path)
1576            .map(|procfs| RenderedTarget {
1577                procfs: procfs.into_rendered(),
1578                via_link: true,
1579            });
1580        // The link reaches a file, which a trailing slash or `/.` refuses.
1581        if target.is_some() && names_a_directory(path) {
1582            return Err(Errno::ENOTDIR.into());
1583        }
1584        Ok(target)
1585    }
1586
1587    /// The rendered procfs file that descriptor `fd`, which the kernel just
1588    /// opened, is a placeholder for: one the guest reached through a link
1589    /// Detcore does not resolve itself, such as another process's
1590    /// `/proc/<pid>/fd/N` or `/proc/*/task/*/fd/N`. A placeholder this tool
1591    /// process made is known by its kernel identity. One it did not (another
1592    /// process's, under a backend whose tool runs in each guest process) is
1593    /// known by its memfd's name and seals, which a placeholder carries where
1594    /// the backend supports seals.
1595    pub(super) async fn rendered_procfs_behind<G: Guest<Self>>(
1596        &self,
1597        guest: &mut G,
1598        fd: RawFd,
1599    ) -> Result<Option<&'static Path>, Error> {
1600        if !self.renders_procfs(guest) {
1601            return Ok(None);
1602        }
1603        if let Ok(stat) = self.inject_fstat(guest, fd).await
1604            && let Some(path) = placeholder_identity(stat.st_dev, stat.st_ino)
1605        {
1606            return Ok(Some(path));
1607        }
1608        // Where the tool runs in one process for every guest, it made every
1609        // placeholder, and its identity is the whole answer. Elsewhere a name
1610        // and seals a guest could imitate decide: a known limitation, see
1611        // <https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4263>.
1612        if !self.cfg.backend.tool_shares_guest_descriptor_table {
1613            return Ok(None);
1614        }
1615        let named = self.placeholder_named(guest, fd).await?;
1616        if named.is_none() {
1617            return Ok(None);
1618        }
1619        let seals = guest
1620            .inject(
1621                syscalls::Fcntl::new()
1622                    .with_fd(fd)
1623                    .with_cmd(syscalls::FcntlCmd::F_GET_SEALS),
1624            )
1625            .await;
1626        // `MFD_NOEXEC_SEAL` adds `F_SEAL_EXEC` to the seals Detcore sets. An
1627        // `O_PATH` descriptor cannot show its seals (EBADF); its name decides.
1628        let wanted = i64::from(PLACEHOLDER_SEALS);
1629        Ok(named.filter(|_| match seals {
1630            Ok(seals) => seals & wanted == wanted,
1631            Err(errno) => errno == Errno::EBADF,
1632        }))
1633    }
1634
1635    /// The rendered procfs file that descriptor `fd`'s memfd is named for,
1636    /// read from its link.
1637    async fn placeholder_named<G: Guest<Self>>(
1638        &self,
1639        guest: &mut G,
1640        fd: RawFd,
1641    ) -> Result<Option<&'static Path>, Error> {
1642        const TARGET_CAPACITY: usize = crate::procfs::PLACEHOLDER_LINK_CAPACITY;
1643        let mut stack = guest.stack().await;
1644        // The calling thread's descriptor, by its own table: `/proc/self/fd`
1645        // is the thread group leader's, another table once a thread made
1646        // without CLONE_FILES has its own.
1647        let link = stack.push(c_string::<32>(
1648            format!("/proc/thread-self/fd/{fd}").as_bytes(),
1649        ));
1650        let target = stack.reserve::<[u8; TARGET_CAPACITY]>();
1651        let stack_guard = stack.commit()?;
1652        let length = guest
1653            .inject(
1654                syscalls::Readlinkat::new()
1655                    .with_dirfd(libc::AT_FDCWD)
1656                    .with_path(PathPtr::from_ptr(link.as_raw() as *const libc::c_char))
1657                    .with_buf(Some(target.cast::<libc::c_char>()))
1658                    .with_buf_len(TARGET_CAPACITY),
1659            )
1660            .await;
1661        let path = match length {
1662            Ok(length) => {
1663                let mut bytes = vec![0; (length as usize).min(TARGET_CAPACITY)];
1664                guest.memory().read_exact(target.cast::<u8>(), &mut bytes)?;
1665                placeholder_link_path(&bytes, self.rendered_files(guest))
1666            }
1667            Err(_) => None,
1668        };
1669        drop(stack_guard);
1670        Ok(path)
1671    }
1672
1673    /// The rendered file whose placeholder the guest's path at `path` (guest
1674    /// address, relative to `dirfd`) reaches through a spelling Detcore does
1675    /// not resolve itself, such as a symbolic link to a descriptor link or
1676    /// `/dev/stdin`: by the kernel identity of what it reaches. Only while
1677    /// this process holds a rendered descriptor, so a process that holds none
1678    /// pays nothing.
1679    pub(super) async fn placeholder_reached<G: Guest<Self>>(
1680        &self,
1681        guest: &mut G,
1682        dirfd: RawFd,
1683        path: Option<PathPtr<'_>>,
1684        follow: bool,
1685    ) -> Option<&'static Path> {
1686        let pointer = path?;
1687        // No placeholder known to this tool process, which serves every
1688        // guest process: nothing can reach one. (Checked first: it costs
1689        // nothing, while the next looks at every descriptor.) Where the tool
1690        // runs in each guest process, another process's may.
1691        let in_guest = self.cfg.backend.tool_shares_guest_descriptor_table;
1692        if (!placeholders_exist() && !in_guest) || !self.renders_procfs(guest) {
1693            return None;
1694        }
1695        // Another process's descriptor link (or `/dev/std*`) may reach a
1696        // placeholder this process does not hold. Where the tool runs in each
1697        // guest process, any spelling may reach one this image inherited and
1698        // has not yet looked at (descriptors are discovered when used).
1699        if !in_guest && !guest.thread_state().holds_a_rendered_procfs_file() {
1700            let text: std::path::PathBuf = pointer.read(&guest.memory()).ok()?;
1701            let names_a_link = super::names_descriptor_link(&text)
1702                || text.starts_with("/dev/std")
1703                || text.starts_with("/dev/fd/");
1704            if !names_a_link {
1705                return None;
1706            }
1707        }
1708        let path = pointer;
1709        let flags = if follow {
1710            AtFlags::empty()
1711        } else {
1712            AtFlags::AT_SYMLINK_NOFOLLOW
1713        };
1714        let stat = Self::inject_statat(guest, dirfd, path, [Some(path), None], flags)
1715            .await
1716            .ok()?;
1717        let target = StatTarget::Path {
1718            dirfd,
1719            path: Some(path),
1720            follow,
1721        };
1722        self.recognize_placeholder(guest, &DetStat::from(stat), target)
1723            .await
1724    }
1725
1726    /// The rendered file whose placeholder the kernel object with metadata
1727    /// `stat`, reached through `target`, is. By its kernel identity, where this
1728    /// tool process made or learned the placeholder. Where the tool runs in
1729    /// each guest process, a placeholder another process made, or an earlier
1730    /// image of this one (inherited across `fork` or `exec`), is learned here:
1731    /// an empty regular file with no link, as a placeholder is, is checked by
1732    /// its memfd's name and seals, and then known by its identity. A path is
1733    /// opened `O_PATH` for that, which no file notices; an `O_PATH`
1734    /// descriptor shows no seals, so for a path, or an `O_PATH` descriptor,
1735    /// the name alone decides. (A guest could imitate the name and seals: a known
1736    /// limitation, see <https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4263>.)
1737    pub(super) async fn recognize_placeholder<G: Guest<Self>>(
1738        &self,
1739        guest: &mut G,
1740        stat: &DetStat,
1741        target: StatTarget<'_>,
1742    ) -> Option<&'static Path> {
1743        if let Some(path) = placeholder_identity(stat.dev, stat.inode) {
1744            return Some(path);
1745        }
1746        let has = |field| stat.mask.contains(field);
1747        let may_be_placeholder = (!has(StatxMask::STATX_TYPE)
1748            || stat.mode & libc::S_IFMT == libc::S_IFREG)
1749            && (!has(StatxMask::STATX_NLINK) || stat.nlink == 0)
1750            && (!has(StatxMask::STATX_SIZE) || stat.size == 0);
1751        if !self.cfg.backend.tool_shares_guest_descriptor_table
1752            || self.cfg.recordreplay_modes
1753            || !may_be_placeholder
1754        {
1755            return None;
1756        }
1757        let (fd, opened) = match target {
1758            StatTarget::Descriptor(fd) => (fd, false),
1759            StatTarget::Path {
1760                dirfd,
1761                path: Some(path),
1762                follow,
1763            } => {
1764                let flags = OFlag::O_PATH
1765                    | OFlag::O_CLOEXEC
1766                    | if follow {
1767                        OFlag::empty()
1768                    } else {
1769                        OFlag::O_NOFOLLOW
1770                    };
1771                let fd = guest
1772                    .inject(
1773                        syscalls::Openat::new()
1774                            .with_dirfd(dirfd)
1775                            .with_path(Some(path))
1776                            .with_flags(flags),
1777                    )
1778                    .await
1779                    .ok()?;
1780                (fd as RawFd, true)
1781            }
1782            _ => return None,
1783        };
1784        let path = self.rendered_procfs_behind(guest, fd).await.ok().flatten();
1785        if opened {
1786            let _ = guest.inject(syscalls::Close::new().with_fd(fd)).await;
1787        }
1788        if let Some(path) = path {
1789            register_placeholder(stat.dev, stat.inode, path);
1790        }
1791        path
1792    }
1793
1794    /// The rendered file whose placeholder descriptor `fd` is, though Detcore
1795    /// did not record it as one (an alias the kernel opened while metadata is
