1796    /// not virtualized). Where one tool process serves every guest, only while
1797    /// this process holds a rendered descriptor; where the tool runs in each
1798    /// guest process, for any descriptor (an `fstat` per call, only without
1799    /// virtualized metadata).
1800    async fn placeholder_descriptor<G: Guest<Self>>(
1801        &self,
1802        guest: &mut G,
1803        fd: RawFd,
1804    ) -> Option<&'static Path> {
1805        let in_guest = self.cfg.backend.tool_shares_guest_descriptor_table;
1806        if !in_guest
1807            && (!placeholders_exist() || !guest.thread_state().holds_a_rendered_procfs_file())
1808        {
1809            return None;
1810        }
1811        let stat = self.inject_fstat(guest, fd).await.ok()?;
1812        self.recognize_placeholder(guest, &DetStat::from(stat), StatTarget::Descriptor(fd))
1813            .await
1814    }
1815
1816    /// Who owns the rendered file `path` and what the caller may override
1817    /// ([`FileAccess`]). A per-process file whose task has exited or is gone
1818    /// belongs to global root. For a live task's file, the caller's capability set
1819    /// `set` decides the overrides, asked of the kernel (or the provider that
1820    /// answers `capget`, as it answers the guest's own) only where `needed`,
1821    /// a call that asks for write access or a `security.` attribute: every
1822    /// other call's answer depends on ownership alone, so a read pays nothing.
1823    /// A set that cannot be read overrides nothing.
1824    pub(super) async fn rendered_file_access<G: Guest<Self>>(
1825        &self,
1826        guest: &mut G,
1827        path: &Path,
1828        needed: bool,
1829        set: CapabilitySet,
1830    ) -> Result<FileAccess, Error> {
1831        if !crate::procfs::rendered_path_is_per_process(path) {
1832            return Ok(FileAccess::GLOBAL_ROOT);
1833        }
1834        // A task that has exited (a zombie), or is gone, has no memory map,
1835        // and Linux gives its files to global root (`task_dump_owner`): then
1836        // the caller owns nothing and no capability of its namespace applies,
1837        // so it reads an owner-only file (`auxv`, 0400) no more than it
1838        // writes any (EACCES).
1839        match self.rendered_task_state(guest, path).await {
1840            Some((answer, true)) if !answer.without_mm => {}
1841            _ => {
1842                return Ok(FileAccess {
1843                    readable: !rendered_path_is_owner_only(path),
1844                    ..FileAccess::GLOBAL_ROOT
1845                });
1846            }
1847        }
1848        // A set that cannot be read overrides nothing; scratch that cannot be
1849        // put back is the failure policy's (`inject_capget`).
1850        let capabilities = if needed {
1851            Self::inject_capget(guest, set).await?.unwrap_or(0)
1852        } else {
1853            0
1854        };
1855        Ok(FileAccess::owned_with(capabilities))
1856    }
1857
1858    /// The caller's capability set `set` (its low 64 bits), as `capget`
1859    /// reports it for the calling thread. `Ok(None)` where it cannot be read
1860    /// with nothing changed: no stack scratch to borrow, or `capget` refused.
1861    /// `Err` where the borrowed scratch could not be put back; the failure
1862    /// policy has then been applied (`scratch_failure`), so the caller
1863    /// returns it as it is, never as an ordinary answer.
1864    pub(super) async fn inject_capget<G: Guest<Self>>(
1865        guest: &mut G,
1866        set: CapabilitySet,
1867    ) -> Result<Option<u64>, Error> {
1868        // The header (8 bytes) and the data (two 12-byte words) in borrowed
1869        // stack scratch, whose original bytes go back on every path.
1870        let (scratch, original, stack_guard) = match Self::borrow_scratch::<G, 32>(guest).await {
1871            Ok(Some(borrowed)) => borrowed,
1872            Ok(None) => return Ok(None),
1873            Err(reason) => {
1874                return Err(
1875                    Self::scratch_failure(guest, reverie::syscalls::Sysno::capget, reason).await,
1876                );
1877            }
1878        };
1879        // Moved into the block by value: a borrowed address would make the
1880        // future not `Send`.
1881        let caller = &mut *guest;
1882        let words = async move {
1883            let guest = caller;
1884            let header = scratch;
1885            let data = AddrMut::<u8>::from_raw(scratch.as_raw() + 8)?;
1886            let mut bytes = [0_u8; 32];
1887            bytes[..4].copy_from_slice(&LINUX_CAPABILITY_VERSION_3.to_ne_bytes());
1888            guest.memory().write_exact(header, &bytes).ok()?;
1889            guest
1890                .inject(
1891                    syscalls::Capget::new()
1892                        .with_header(AddrMut::from_raw(header.as_raw()))
1893                        .with_data(AddrMut::from_raw(data.as_raw())),
1894                )
1895                .await
1896                .ok()?;
1897            let mut read = [0_u8; 24];
1898            guest.memory().read_exact(data, &mut read).ok()?;
1899            Some(read)
1900        }
1901        .await;
1902        let restored = guest.memory().write_exact(scratch, &original);
1903        drop(stack_guard);
1904        if let Err(errno) = restored {
1905            let reason = format!(
1906                "the guest stack scratch under an injected capget could not be restored: {errno}"
1907            );
1908            return Err(
1909                Self::scratch_failure(guest, reverie::syscalls::Sysno::capget, reason).await,
1910            );
1911        }
1912        // Each 32-bit word holds `effective`, `permitted`, `inheritable`.
1913        let Some(words) = words else {
1914            return Ok(None);
1915        };
1916        let word =
1917            |index: usize| u32::from_ne_bytes(words[index * 4..index * 4 + 4].try_into().unwrap());
1918        let index = match set {
1919            CapabilitySet::Effective => 0,
1920            CapabilitySet::Permitted => 1,
1921        };
1922        Ok(Some(
1923            u64::from(word(index)) | (u64::from(word(index + 3)) << 32),
1924        ))
1925    }
1926
1927    /// `N` bytes of the guest's stack scratch (below the red zone), borrowed
1928    /// for an injected call: the scratch address, the bytes it held before,
1929    /// which the borrower must write back on every path, error paths
1930    /// included, and the stack guard, which the borrower keeps until it has.
1931    /// `Ok(None)` where the scratch cannot be used and nothing is left
1932    /// changed: it cannot be read and written through the guest's own
1933    /// protections (a stack pointer just above a guard page), or its commit
1934    /// failed and what it wrote was put back. `Err` where a failed commit's
1935    /// partial write could not be put back.
1936    pub(super) async fn borrow_scratch<G: Guest<Self>, const N: usize>(
1937        guest: &mut G,
1938    ) -> Result<
1939        Option<(
1940            AddrMut<'static, u8>,
1941            [u8; N],
1942            <G::Stack as Stack>::StackGuard,
1943        )>,
1944        String,
1945    > {
1946        let mut stack = guest.stack().await;
1947        let scratch = stack.reserve::<[u8; N]>().cast::<u8>();
1948        let mut original = [0_u8; N];
1949        // `commit` writes the reserved bytes, so save them before it.
1950        if !super::save_procfs_scratch(&mut guest.memory(), scratch, &mut original) {
1951            return Ok(None);
1952        }
1953        match stack.commit() {
1954            Ok(guard) => Ok(Some((scratch, original, guard))),
1955            // A commit that failed part way may have written some of them.
1956            Err(_) => match guest.memory().write_exact(scratch, &original) {
1957                Ok(()) => Ok(None),
1958                Err(errno) => Err(format!(
1959                    "a failed stack commit's partial write to the guest stack scratch could not \
1960                     be put back: {errno}"
1961                )),
1962            },
1963        }
1964    }
1965
1966    /// Apply the failure policy to an injected call Detcore could not finish
1967    /// cleanly (`reason`): guest stack scratch it could not put back, or a
1968    /// probe descriptor it could not close. Logged by name; a fail-closed run
1969    /// stops with the policy's exit, and a run with
1970    /// `--allow-unsupported-syscalls` gives the guest's call EOPNOTSUPP.
1971    /// Never an ordinary answer such as EACCES.
1972    async fn scratch_failure<G: Guest<Self>>(
1973        guest: &mut G,
1974        sysno: reverie::syscalls::Sysno,
1975        reason: String,
1976    ) -> Error {
1977        error!("Detcore refuses {sysno}: {reason}");
1978        let panic_on_unsupported = guest.config().panic_on_unsupported_syscalls;
1979        match crate::syscalls::helpers::refuse_unserviceable_operation_with(
1980            guest,
1981            panic_on_unsupported,
1982            sysno,
1983            Errno::EOPNOTSUPP,
1984            Some(&reason),
1985        )
1986        .await
1987        {
1988            Ok(_) => Errno::EOPNOTSUPP.into(),
1989            Err(error) => error,
1990        }
1991    }
1992
1993    /// Open the rendered procfs file `target` names with the guest's `flags`.
1994    pub(super) async fn open_rendered_procfs<G: Guest<Self>>(
1995        &self,
1996        guest: &mut G,
1997        flags: OFlag,
1998        target: RenderedTarget,
1999    ) -> Result<i64, Error> {
2000        // A descriptor link is a symbolic link: `O_NOFOLLOW` refuses it, once
2001        // the flags themselves passed. (With `O_PATH`, the caller let the
2002        // kernel open the link itself.)
2003        if let Some(errno) = open_flags_refused(flags) {
2004            return Err(errno.into());
2005        }
2006        // `O_CREAT|O_EXCL` follows no final link: the link exists (EEXIST).
2007        if target.via_link
2008            && flags.contains(OFlag::O_CREAT | OFlag::O_EXCL)
2009            && !flags.contains(OFlag::O_PATH)
2010        {
2011            return Err(Errno::EEXIST.into());
2012        }
2013        if target.via_link && flags.contains(OFlag::O_NOFOLLOW) {
2014            return Err(Errno::ELOOP.into());
2015        }
2016        let procfs = target.procfs.into_rendered();
2017        let path = procfs
2018            .opened_path()
2019            .expect("a file opened as rendered has a path");
2020        // A rendered `comm` is read only (package K slice A+): an open of
2021        // it for writing, by its path or a descriptor link, is Detcore's
2022        // EACCES, with nothing injected and nothing asked of the global tool
2023        // ([`asks_to_write_comm`], declared).
2024        if asks_to_write_comm(&procfs, flags) {
2025            return Err(Errno::EACCES.into());
2026        }
2027        let writes = flags & OFlag::O_ACCMODE != OFlag::O_RDONLY || flags.contains(OFlag::O_TRUNC);
2028        let access = self
2029            .rendered_file_access(guest, path, writes, CapabilitySet::Effective)
2030            .await?;
2031        let reopen = rendered_open_flags(flags, access)?;
2032        // A process's `mountinfo` or `mounts` takes the task's mount namespace
2033        // at its open (`mounts_open_common`), which a task that has exited no
2034        // longer has: EINVAL, after the permission check.
2035        if procfs.mount_table_rendering().is_some()
2036            && !flags.contains(OFlag::O_PATH)
2037            && let Some((answer, current)) = self.rendered_task_state(guest, path).await
2038            && (!current || answer.without_mm)
2039        {
2040            return Err(Errno::EINVAL.into());
2041        }
2042        // A process's `auxv`: its snapshot is the process's auxiliary vector
2043        // now, which the open pins (`open_process_auxv`).
2044        // Boxed: the access check's injected calls would otherwise grow every
2045        // open's future, which some backends poll on a small stack.
2046        let opened = if procfs.is_process_auxv() && !flags.contains(OFlag::O_PATH) {
2047            Some(Box::pin(self.open_process_auxv(guest, &procfs)).await?)
2048        } else {
2049            None
2050        };
2051        GuestProcfs::remember();
2052        // Read before the placeholder exists, so that a failure leaves the
2053        // guest nothing open.
2054        let metadata = self.rendered_metadata(guest, path).await?;
2055        // The snapshot this open takes, which its placeholder's name carries,
2056        // so that every backend has it after `exec`; a file whose every
2057        // snapshot is the same is rendered again there
2058        // (`ProcfsFile::snapshot_is_rerendered`). A file Linux renders when it
2059        // is read takes none until its first read
2060        // (`ProcfsFile::snapshot_at_first_read`).
2061        let snapshot = match opened {
2062            Some(bytes) => Some(bytes),
2063            None if procfs.is_process_auxv() || procfs.is_process_cmdline() => None,
2064            None => {
2065                (!procfs.snapshot_at_first_read()).then(|| self.render_snapshot(guest, &procfs))
2066            }
2067        };
2068        // A process's `auxv`'s snapshot does not fit a memfd name: it stays
2069        // with the open file description (and `PLACEHOLDER_OPENS`). A
2070        // `cmdline` takes none: its open reads no memory
2071        // (`proc_pid_cmdline_ops` has no `open`), and every read reads the
2072        // memory of the process it names (`Self::read_rendered_cmdline`).
2073        let carried: Vec<u8> = match &snapshot {
2074            Some(snapshot)
2075                if !procfs.snapshot_is_rerendered()
2076                    && !procfs.is_process_auxv()
2077                    && !procfs.is_process_cmdline() =>
2078            {
2079                snapshot.clone()
2080            }
2081            _ => Vec::new(),
2082        };
2083        let (fd, identity) = self
2084            .inject_placeholder(guest, path, &carried, reopen)
2085            .await?;
2086        // What a descriptor rebuilt from the placeholder's identity reads
2087        // (`adopt_received_placeholder`): a process's `auxv` keeps its whole
2088        // snapshot there, which its name cannot carry.
2089        let registered = match &snapshot {
2090            Some(snapshot) if procfs.is_process_auxv() => snapshot.clone(),
2091            _ => carried,
2092        };
2093        // The status flags Linux keeps, which include `O_NOFOLLOW`: the
2094        // placeholder cannot be reopened with it through its link.
2095        guest.thread_state().add_fd(
2096            fd,
2097            reopen | (flags & OFlag::O_NOFOLLOW),
2098            FdType::Regular,
2099            Some(metadata),
2100        )?;
2101        let procfs = match snapshot {
2102            Some(snapshot) => procfs.with_rendered_snapshot(snapshot),
2103            None => procfs,
2104        };
2105        let handle = guest.thread_state().with_detfd(fd, |detfd| {
2106            detfd.set_path(path);
2107            detfd.set_procfs(procfs.clone());
2108            detfd.open_file_handle()
2109        })?;
2110        register_placeholder_open(identity, handle, registered);
2111        Ok(fd as i64)
2112    }
2113
2114    /// What Linux's `auxv_open` does for the rendered `/proc/<pid>/auxv`
2115    /// `procfs` names, after the open's permission check: it looks for the
2116    /// task's memory map (`proc_mem_open`, `mm_access`) and pins it, so the
2117    /// file reads that map's `saved_auxv` for as long as it is open, through
2118    /// any later exec or the task's exit. Another thread group's map needs
2119    /// `ptrace_may_access` ([`Self::ptrace_read_allowed`]). Returns the
2120    /// auxiliary vector the open snapshots.
2121    ///
2122    /// A zombie's map is gone (ESRCH in Linux), but its file is global
2123    /// root's, which no guest in Hermit's user namespace can read
2124    /// (`rendered_file_access`), so that open has already failed with EACCES.
2125    async fn open_process_auxv<G: Guest<Self>>(
2126        &self,
2127        guest: &mut G,
2128        procfs: &ProcfsFile,
2129    ) -> Result<Vec<u8>, Error> {
2130        let task = procfs.bound_task().ok_or(Errno::ENOENT)?;
2131        let target = detcore_model::pid::DetPid::from_raw(task.process);
2132        let entry = crate::tool_global::process_auxv(guest, target)
2133            .await
2134            .ok_or(Errno::ESRCH)?;
2135        // The caller's own thread group needs no permission
2136        // (`__ptrace_may_access`'s `same_thread_group`).
2137        if self.own_detpid(guest) != entry.process {
2138            Self::ptrace_read_allowed(guest, task).await??;
2139        }
2140        Ok(entry.bytes)
2141    }
2142
2143    /// Linux's `ptrace_may_access(task, PTRACE_MODE_READ_FSCREDS)` for the
2144    /// caller and `task`, the task a `/proc/<pid>/auxv` path names (the
2145    /// thread below `task/`, else the number at the top of `/proc`), in
2146    /// another thread group: `Ok(Ok(()))` where the caller may open the file,
2147    /// `Ok(Err(errno))` where the open fails with `errno`.
2148    ///
2149    /// Where the kernel beneath serves the caller's `/proc` (ptrace), the
2150    /// kernel decides, by the caller's own open of the kernel's
2151    /// `/proc/<pid>/auxv` ([`Self::kernel_procfs_open_answer`], by the
2152    /// kernel's number, `kernel_procfs_number`): Linux's whole rule,
2153    /// credentials, user namespaces (a guest may make one with
2154    /// `clone(CLONE_NEWUSER)`), the memory map's namespace and its
2155    /// dumpability, applied by Linux itself. Whether the kernel beneath
2156    /// serves this caller's `/proc` is a fact the kernel reports, not a
2157    /// backend switch: a refused probe is followed by the caller's open of
2158    /// its own `/proc/thread-self/stat` (mode 0444, which no credential or
2159    /// dumpability refuses). Where that is refused with EACCES too (KVM's
2160    /// provider refuses every `/proc` file with EACCES, its backstop), the
2161    /// answer is `CAP_SYS_PTRACE` in the caller's own effective set
2162    /// (`capget`), else EACCES: KVM's named difference from Linux, which also
2163    /// asks about the target's dumpability and capabilities. Any other errno
2164    /// from either probe (EMFILE, ENFILE, ENOMEM, ...) is the open's answer,
2165    /// as Linux's own open would fail with it ([`auxv_access_answer`]).
2166    ///
2167    /// With sequentialized threads every call runs in the caller's turn, so
2168    /// the answer is the guest's own history's. Without them
2169    /// (`--no-sequentialize-threads`, a declared relaxation) a target's
2170    /// thread may change its credentials, dumpability or namespace
2171    /// concurrently, and the answer is host-timed; and the probe's
2172    /// descriptor briefly takes the lowest free number, so a concurrent open
2173    /// by another guest thread may be given a higher number than in another
2174    /// run.
2175    ///
2176    /// `Err` is a probe Detcore could not finish cleanly (no stack scratch
2177    /// for its path, scratch it could not put back, a descriptor it could
2178    /// not close, the caller's capabilities unreadable), after the failure
2179    /// policy (`scratch_failure`): never an access answer.
2180    // TODO-HUMAN-REVIEW(#4213): Review the access check of another process's
2181    // open of its rendered /proc/<pid>/auxv by the kernel beneath's answer.
2182    pub(crate) async fn ptrace_read_allowed<G: Guest<Self>>(
2183        guest: &mut G,
2184        task: crate::procfs::BoundTask,
2185    ) -> Result<Result<(), Errno>, Error> {
2186        let named = task.thread.unwrap_or(task.process);
2187        let target = match Self::kernel_procfs_number(guest, named).await {
2188            Some(kernel) => Self::probe(guest, format!("/proc/{kernel}/auxv").as_bytes()).await?,
2189            None => Err(Errno::ESRCH),
2190        };
2191        if target.is_ok() {
2192            return Ok(Ok(()));
2193        }
2194        let serves = Self::probe(guest, b"/proc/thread-self/stat").await?;
2195        match auxv_access_answer(target, serves) {
2196            AuxvAccess::Answer(answer) => Ok(answer),
2197            AuxvAccess::CallerCapability => {
2198                match Self::inject_capget(guest, CapabilitySet::Effective).await? {
2199                    Some(effective) if effective & (1 << CAP_SYS_PTRACE) != 0 => Ok(Ok(())),
2200                    Some(_) => Ok(Err(Errno::EACCES)),
2201                    None => Err(Self::scratch_failure(
2202                        guest,
2203                        reverie::syscalls::Sysno::openat,
2204                        "the caller's capabilities, which decide another process's \
2205                         /proc/<pid>/auxv where the kernel beneath serves no /proc, could \
2206                         not be read"
2207                            .to_string(),
2208                    )
2209                    .await),
2210                }
2211            }
2212        }
2213    }
2214
2215    /// [`Self::kernel_procfs_open_answer`], with the failure policy applied
2216    /// to a probe Detcore could not finish cleanly.
2217    async fn probe<G: Guest<Self>>(guest: &mut G, path: &[u8]) -> Result<Result<(), Errno>, Error> {
2218        match Self::kernel_procfs_open_answer(guest, path).await {
2219            Ok(answer) => Ok(answer),
2220            Err(reason) => {
2221                Err(Self::scratch_failure(guest, reverie::syscalls::Sysno::openat, reason).await)
2222            }
2223        }
2224    }
2225
2226    /// The kernel beneath's answer to the caller opening `path` (a kernel
2227    /// `/proc` file) for reading: `Ok(Ok(()))` where it opens, `Ok(Err(errno))`
2228    /// where it fails with `errno`. An open Reverie reports did not run
2229    /// (ERESTARTSYS) is retried, so that errno never reaches the guest. The
2230    /// probe's descriptor is closed: a close Reverie reports did not run
2231    /// (ERESTARTSYS) is retried; EINTR is not, since Linux has released the
2232    /// descriptor before close returns it, and a retry could close a number
2233    /// another thread was given. The path lives in borrowed stack scratch
2234    /// whose original bytes go back on every path ([`Self::borrow_scratch`]).
2235    /// `Err` (no scratch for the path, scratch not put back, any other close
2236    /// error) is a probe that could not be finished cleanly.
2237    pub(super) async fn kernel_procfs_open_answer<G: Guest<Self>>(
2238        guest: &mut G,
2239        path: &[u8],
2240    ) -> Result<Result<(), Errno>, String> {
2241        const PATH_CAPACITY: usize = 32;
2242        let (scratch, original, stack_guard) =
2243            match Self::borrow_scratch::<G, PATH_CAPACITY>(guest).await? {
2244                Some(borrowed) => borrowed,
2245                None => {
2246                    return Err(format!(
2247                        "no guest stack scratch can hold the path of the access probe of {}",
2248                        String::from_utf8_lossy(path)
2249                    ));
2250                }
2251            };
2252        // Moved into the block by value: a borrowed address would make the
2253        // future not `Send`.
2254        let probe = &mut *guest;
2255        let answer: Result<Result<(), Errno>, String> = async move {
2256            let guest = probe;
2257            let bytes = c_string::<PATH_CAPACITY>(path);
2258            if let Err(errno) = guest.memory().write_exact(scratch, &bytes) {
2259                return Err(format!(
2260                    "the path of the access probe of {} could not be written to the guest \
2261                     stack scratch: {errno}",
2262                    String::from_utf8_lossy(path)
2263                ));
2264            }
2265            let opened = loop {
2266                match guest
2267                    .inject(
2268                        syscalls::Openat::new()
2269                            .with_dirfd(libc::AT_FDCWD)
2270                            .with_path(PathPtr::from_ptr(scratch.as_raw() as *const libc::c_char))
2271                            .with_flags(OFlag::O_RDONLY | OFlag::O_CLOEXEC),
2272                    )
2273                    .await
2274                {
2275                    Err(Errno::ERESTARTSYS) => continue,
2276                    opened => break opened,
2277                }
2278            };
2279            let fd = match opened {
2280                Ok(fd) => fd as RawFd,
2281                Err(errno) => return Ok(Err(errno)),
2282            };
2283            loop {
2284                match guest.inject(syscalls::Close::new().with_fd(fd)).await {
2285                    Ok(_) | Err(Errno::EINTR) => return Ok(Ok(())),
2286                    Err(Errno::ERESTARTSYS) => continue,
2287                    Err(errno) => {
2288                        return Err(format!(
2289                            "the descriptor {fd} of the access probe of {} could not be closed: \
2290                             {errno}",
2291                            String::from_utf8_lossy(path)
2292                        ));
2293                    }
2294                }
2295            }
2296        }
2297        .await;
2298        let restored = guest.memory().write_exact(scratch, &original);
2299        drop(stack_guard);
2300        if let Err(errno) = restored {
2301            return Err(format!(
2302                "the guest stack scratch under the access probe of {} could not be restored: \
2303                 {errno}",
2304                String::from_utf8_lossy(path)
2305            ));
2306        }
2307        answer
2308    }
2309
2310    /// The snapshot of the rendered file `procfs` an open takes, from
2311    /// Detcore's state, as its first read used to: a UUID from the thread's
2312    /// generator, Detcore's count of open ptys, the virtual CPU's table
2313    /// (`Detcore::virtual_cpuid`, which answers the guest's CPUID
2314    /// instruction), or a fact of the machine description.
2315    fn render_snapshot<G: Guest<Self>>(&self, guest: &mut G, procfs: &ProcfsFile) -> Vec<u8> {
2316        use rand::RngExt as _;
2317        let mut file = procfs.clone();
2318        let random_uuid = file
2319            .needs_random_uuid()
2320            .then(|| guest.thread_state_mut().thread_prng().random::<[u8; 16]>());
2321        let virtual_pty_count = guest
2322            .thread_state()
2323            .count_open_files_at_paths(&[Path::new("/dev/ptmx"), Path::new("/dev/pts/ptmx")]);
2324        let virtual_cpuinfo = file.is_cpuinfo().then(|| self.virtual_cpuid().cpuinfo());
2325        let template = file.rendering_template().to_vec();
2326        file.initialize(
2327            template,
2328            crate::procfs::ProcfsSnapshotContext {
2329                random_uuid,
2330                virtual_pty_count,
2331                virtual_cpuinfo,
2332                machine: crate::machine::MachineDescription::of(guest.config()),
2333                ..Default::default()
2334            },
2335        );
2336        file.rendered_snapshot()
2337            .expect("a rendered file has its snapshot once initialized")
2338            .to_vec()
2339    }
2340
2341    /// The uptime, in seconds, that the first read in this process of the
2342    /// rendered file `path` behind `fd`, one whose snapshot its first read
2343    /// takes (`ProcfsFile::snapshot_at_first_read`), renders: `uptime_seconds`,
2344    /// the uptime now, unless an earlier read of the same open file already
2345    /// took the snapshot and carried its uptime on the placeholder.
2346    ///
2347    /// Linux keeps that snapshot in the open file, for every process sharing
2348    /// it and across `exec`. Detcore keeps it in its state of the open file,
2349    /// which ptrace and KVM keep across `fork` and `exec` too; where the tool
2350    /// runs in each guest process (DBT, SaBRe, in-guest LiteInst), that state
2351    /// is the process's own, and a process that shares the open file, or the
2352    /// image after `exec`, starts without it. Every other byte of the
2353    /// snapshot is fixed for the run, so the uptime is all it needs, and the
2354    /// placeholder, one kernel object per open file, carries it in its
2355    /// modification time ([`carried_uptime_mtime`]). The guest never sees
2356    /// that time (every stat of a rendered file is Detcore's
2357    /// `rendered_stat`) and cannot set it (Detcore answers the guest's
2358    /// `utimensat` of a rendered file, by descriptor too).
2359    ///
2360    /// The carry is read and written only where nothing can change what the
2361    /// descriptor number names between Detcore's look and its write
2362    /// ([`may_carry_uptime`]): with threads sequentialized, Detcore holds the
2363    /// scheduler turn across both injected calls, no guest code runs inside
2364    /// the handler, and the descriptor must be the placeholder of `path` this
2365    /// tool process registered, by its kernel identity. Under
2366    /// `--no-sequentialize-threads` another thread could replace the number
2367    /// in between, and on SaBRe and in-guest LiteInst an accepted native
2368    /// signal handler could, so the write could land on another file: there
2369    /// is no carry there, and a process without Detcore's state of the open
2370    /// file (after `exec` or `fork` on SaBRe) takes its own snapshot at its
2371    /// next read, as before.
2372    ///
2373    /// If the write fails (the process no longer owns the placeholder after
2374    /// changing its user), the snapshot is still taken, and is not carried.
2375    pub(super) async fn first_read_uptime<G: Guest<Self>>(
2376        &self,
2377        guest: &mut G,
2378        fd: RawFd,
2379        path: &'static Path,
2380        uptime_seconds: u64,
2381    ) -> u64 {
2382        let stable = descriptor_stable_across_injections(
2383            guest.config().sequentialize_threads,
2384            guest.config().backend.runs_guest_code_between_injections,
2385        );
2386        if !stable {
2387            return uptime_seconds;
2388        }
2389        let Ok(stat) = self.inject_fstat(guest, fd).await else {
2390            return uptime_seconds;
2391        };
2392        if !may_carry_uptime(stable, placeholder_identity(stat.st_dev, stat.st_ino), path) {
2393            return uptime_seconds;
2394        }
2395        if let Some(carried) = carried_uptime(stat.st_mtime, stat.st_mtime_nsec) {
2396            return carried;
2397        }
2398        let times = [
2399            Timespec {
2400                tv_sec: 0,
2401                tv_nsec: libc::UTIME_OMIT,
2402            },
2403            carried_uptime_mtime(uptime_seconds),
2404        ];
2405        if let Err(errno) = Self::inject_futimens(guest, fd, times).await {
2406            warn!(
2407                "The first read of {} on fd {fd} could not carry its uptime on the \
2408                 placeholder ({errno}); a process without Detcore's state of the open \
2409                 file takes its own snapshot.",
2410                path.display()
2411            );
2412        }
2413        uptime_seconds
2414    }
2415
2416    /// `utimensat(fd, NULL, times, 0)`, its times in guest stack scratch, or in
2417    /// a private anonymous page mapped for this call only where the stack
2418    /// cannot hold them (as `inject_fstat` falls back,
2419    /// https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/3328).
2420    async fn inject_futimens<G: Guest<Self>>(
2421        guest: &mut G,
2422        fd: RawFd,
2423        times: [Timespec; 2],
2424    ) -> Result<(), Errno> {
2425        let call = |times| {
2426            syscalls::Utimensat::new()
2427                .with_dirfd(fd)
2428                .with_path(None)
2429                .with_times(Some(times))
2430                .with_flags(0)
2431        };
2432        let mut stack = guest.stack().await;
2433        let staged = stack.push(times);
2434        // Keep the guard until the call has read the times: on backends whose
2435        // scratch is a Tool-owned arena (DBT, SaBRe), dropping it frees them.
2436        if let Ok(stack_guard) = stack.commit() {
2437            let result = guest.inject(call(staged)).await;
2438            drop(stack_guard);
2439            if result != Err(Errno::EFAULT) {
2440                return result.map(drop);
2441            }
2442        }
2443        let len = std::mem::size_of::<[Timespec; 2]>();
2444        let mapped = guest
2445            .inject_with_retry(Syscall::Mmap(
2446                syscalls::Mmap::new()
2447                    .with_addr(None)
2448                    .with_len(len)
2449                    .with_prot(ProtFlags::PROT_READ | ProtFlags::PROT_WRITE)
2450                    .with_flags(MapFlags::MAP_PRIVATE | MapFlags::MAP_ANONYMOUS)
2451                    .with_fd(-1)
2452                    .with_offset(0),
2453            ))
2454            .await?;
2455        let page = usize::try_from(mapped).map_err(|_| Errno::EFAULT)?;
2456        let staged = Addr::<[Timespec; 2]>::from_raw(page).ok_or(Errno::EFAULT)?;
2457        let written = AddrMut::<[Timespec; 2]>::from_raw(page)
2458            .ok_or(Errno::EFAULT)
2459            .and_then(|address| guest.memory().write_value(address, &times));
2460        let result = match written {
2461            Ok(()) => guest.inject(call(staged)).await.map(drop),
2462            Err(errno) => Err(errno),
2463        };
2464        let _ = guest
2465            .inject_with_retry(Syscall::Munmap(
2466                syscalls::Munmap::new()
2467                    .with_addr(Some(staged.cast()))
2468                    .with_len(len),
2469            ))
2470            .await;
2471        result
2472    }
2473
2474    /// Inject a placeholder for `path` open with `flags`, at the lowest free
2475    /// descriptor number, as `open` would place the file.
2476    async fn inject_placeholder<G: Guest<Self>>(
2477        &self,
2478        guest: &mut G,
2479        path: &'static Path,
2480        snapshot: &[u8],
2481        flags: OFlag,
2482    ) -> Result<(RawFd, (u64, u64)), Error> {
2483        let name = placeholder_name(path, snapshot);
2484        let mut stack = guest.stack().await;
2485        let name = stack.push(c_string::<PLACEHOLDER_NAME_CAPACITY>(name.as_bytes()));
2486        let link = stack.reserve::<[u8; 32]>();
2487        let stack_guard = stack.commit()?;
2488        let name = name.as_raw();
2489        let create = move |flags| {
2490            syscalls::MemfdCreate::new()
2491                .with_name(PathPtr::from_ptr(name as *const libc::c_char))
2492                .with_flags(flags)
2493        };
2494        // Not executable, which a hardened `vm.memfd_noexec` requires and
2495        // kernels before 6.3 do not know; sealed, so that a spelling Detcore
2496        // does not recognise cannot change the empty file.
2497        let sealable = libc::MFD_CLOEXEC | libc::MFD_ALLOW_SEALING;
2498        let memfd = match guest.inject(create(sealable | libc::MFD_NOEXEC_SEAL)).await {
2499            Err(Errno::EINVAL) => guest.inject(create(sealable)).await?,
2500            created => created?,
2501        } as RawFd;
2502        // A backend without seals (KVM refuses `F_ADD_SEALS`) leaves the
2503        // placeholder unsealed; Detcore still answers every call it knows.
2504        let _ = guest
2505            .inject(
2506                syscalls::Fcntl::new()
2507                    .with_fd(memfd)
2508                    .with_cmd(syscalls::FcntlCmd::F_ADD_SEALS(PLACEHOLDER_SEALS)),
2509            )
2510            .await;
2511        // By the calling thread's own table (`/proc/thread-self/fd`): the
2512        // leader's (`/proc/self/fd`) lacks the memfd in a thread made without
2513        // CLONE_FILES, and the reopen then failed with ENOENT.
2514        guest.memory().write_exact(
2515            link.cast::<u8>(),
2516            &c_string::<32>(format!("/proc/thread-self/fd/{memfd}").as_bytes()),
2517        )?;
2518        // Reopening through the descriptor link gives a description with the
2519        // guest's flags, which then takes the memfd's number.
2520        let reopened = guest
2521            .inject(
2522                syscalls::Openat::new()
2523                    .with_dirfd(libc::AT_FDCWD)
2524                    .with_path(PathPtr::from_ptr(link.as_raw() as *const libc::c_char))
2525                    .with_flags(flags | OFlag::O_CLOEXEC),
2526            )
2527            .await;
2528        let moved = match reopened {
2529            Ok(reopened) => {
2530                let moved = guest
2531                    .inject(
2532                        syscalls::Dup3::new()
2533                            .with_oldfd(reopened as i32)
2534                            .with_newfd(memfd)
2535                            .with_flags(flags & OFlag::O_CLOEXEC),
2536                    )
2537                    .await;
2538                let _ = guest
2539                    .inject(syscalls::Close::new().with_fd(reopened as i32))
2540                    .await;
2541                moved
2542            }
2543            Err(errno) => Err(errno),
2544        };
2545        drop(stack_guard);
2546        // The guest gets the errno that kept the file from opening, as an open
2547        // that runs out of descriptors or memory reports it; nothing is left
2548        // open.
2549        if let Err(errno) = moved {
2550            let _ = guest.inject(syscalls::Close::new().with_fd(memfd)).await;
2551            return Err(errno.into());
2552        }
2553        let identity = match self.inject_fstat(guest, memfd).await {
2554            Ok(stat) => stat,
2555            Err(errno) => {
2556                let _ = guest.inject(syscalls::Close::new().with_fd(memfd)).await;
2557                return Err(errno.into());
2558            }
2559        };
2560        register_placeholder(identity.st_dev, identity.st_ino, path);
2561        Ok((memfd, (identity.st_dev, identity.st_ino)))
2562    }
2563
2564    /// The inode number of the directory entry `name`, which the host listed
2565    /// with inode `host_ino`, if it is a procfs file Detcore renders. A
2566    /// per-process file is numbered for the task holding its numbers now (the
2567    /// process table's incarnation, `Detcore::rendered_task_state`), as a stat
2568    /// of it would be, and only where Detcore renders procfs files (not after
2569    /// `chroot`, where the kernel's file is the file).
2570    pub(super) async fn rendered_listed_inode<G: Guest<Self>>(
2571        &self,
2572        guest: &mut G,
2573        inodes: &procfs_inode::DirectoryInodes,
2574        name: &[u8],
2575        host_ino: u64,
2576        files: RenderedFiles,
2577    ) -> Result<Option<u64>, Error> {
2578        if self.cfg.recordreplay_modes {
2579            return Ok(None);
2580        }
2581        if let Some(path) = inodes.entry_procfs_path(name)
2582            && let Some(mut file) = ProcfsFile::from_path(&path)
2583            && file.per_process_name().is_some()
2584        {
2585            if !self.renders_procfs(guest) || self.kernel_serves_every_comm(&file) {
2586                return Ok(None);
2587            }
2588            let Some(task) = file.bound_task() else {
2589                return Ok(None);
2590            };
2591            let answer = crate::tool_global::procfs_task(guest, task).await;
2592            if !answer.exists {
2593                return Ok(None);
2594            }
2595            let without_mm = answer.without_mm;
2596            self.bind_task_identity(guest, &mut file, answer).await?;
2597            // A process whose `auxv` the kernel serves lists the kernel's
2598            // entry, as a path to it opens the kernel's file; so does another
2599            // process's `cmdline`.
2600            if self.kept_by_kernel(guest, &file, without_mm).await {
2601                return Ok(None);
2602            }
2603            return Ok(file.rendered_path(files).map(procfs_inode::rendered_inode));
2604        }
2605        // By kernel identity only for an entry named as a rendered file is,
2606        // which costs a stat for each rendered file of that name (at most
2607        // two: `version`), and only then.
2608        Ok(inodes.rendered_entry_inode(name, files).or_else(|| {
2609            let named = rendered_paths(files).filter(|path| rendered_name(path) == name);
2610            rendered_path_among(inodes.device(), host_ino, named).map(procfs_inode::rendered_inode)
2611        }))
2612    }
2613
2614    /// Whether `file` is a process's `auxv` that the kernel serves: Detcore
2615    /// recorded no auxiliary vector for the process's image (the backend
2616    /// reports no initial stack pointer, or the vector there is implausible;
2617    /// `Detcore::record_process_auxv`), so every spelling of it, and its
2618    /// listing, is the kernel's file.
2619    ///
2620    /// Never a zombie's (`without_mm`): its file is served on the rendered
2621    /// path, whose access check refuses it (EACCES, global root's), whether
2622    /// or not its vector is still kept, so the host-timed moment the global
2623    /// tool forgets an exited process's vector changes nothing the guest
2624    /// sees.
2625    ///
2626    /// And whether `file` is a process's `cmdline` that the kernel serves:
2627    /// another process's, whose memory Detcore cannot read (Linux reads the
2628    /// target's memory at each read), or the caller's own where Detcore
2629    /// recorded no argument area for its image (the same backends and stacks
2630    /// as for `auxv`).
2631    ///
2632    /// And whether `file` is a process's `stat` that the kernel serves:
2633    /// another process's (package K slice D4c-1 renders the reader's own
2634    /// process's only), or, where the name's source is Detcore's record
2635    /// (`Self::comm_source`), the caller's own before a name is recorded
2636    /// (`procfs::task_stat::renders_task_stat`).
2637    ///
2638    /// And whether `file` is a task's `comm` that the kernel serves: every
2639    /// one where the name's source is the kernel
2640    /// ([`Self::kernel_serves_every_comm`]), and elsewhere another process's,
2641    /// or the caller's own before a name is recorded
2642    /// (`procfs::task_stat::renders_task_comm`, package K slice A+).
2643    async fn kept_by_kernel<G: Guest<Self>>(
2644        &self,
2645        guest: &mut G,
2646        file: &ProcfsFile,
2647        without_mm: bool,
2648    ) -> bool {
2649        let Some(task) = file.bound_task() else {
2650            return false;
2651        };
2652        // A task's `comm` (package K slice A+): Detcore renders only the
2653        // reader's own process's, from its record, where the record is the
2654        // name's source (`procfs::task_stat::renders_task_comm`).
2655        if file.is_task_comm() {
2656            if self.kernel_serves_every_comm(file) {
2657                return true;
2658            }
2659            let facts = crate::tool_global::task_stat_facts(guest, task).await;
2660            let own = self.own_detpid(guest).as_raw();
2661            return !crate::procfs::task_stat::renders_task_comm(&facts, own, self.comm_source());
2662        }
2663        if file.is_task_stat() {
2664            let facts = crate::tool_global::task_stat_facts(guest, task).await;
2665            let own = self.own_detpid(guest).as_raw();
2666            let rendered =
2667                crate::procfs::task_stat::renders_task_stat(&facts, own, self.comm_source());
2668            // The guest-visible parent of a process whose real parent is
2669            // outside the guest's tree (the run's root, and a CLONE_PARENT
2670            // child of such a process), which no other record of Detcore's
2671            // holds, is its own getppid answer, recorded here, in its own
2672            // turn, before any descriptor of its rendered stat exists: a
2673            // descriptor that reaches another process (across fork or
2674            // SCM_RIGHTS) renders it (`Scheduler::guest_visible_parent`).
2675            // Every rendered stat of a process is opened, or listed, by one of
2676            // its own threads, so this runs before any other process can read
2677            // one. The global tool ignores it for a process whose model knows
2678            // its parent.
2679            if rendered
2680                && facts.parent.is_none()
2681                && let Ok(parent) = guest.inject(syscalls::Getppid::new()).await
2682            {
2683                crate::tool_global::record_external_parent(
2684                    guest,
2685                    detcore_model::pid::DetPid::from_raw(own),
2686                    parent as i32,
2687                )
2688                .await;
2689            }
2690            return !rendered;
2691        }
2692        let process = detcore_model::pid::DetPid::from_raw(task.process);
2693        if file.is_process_cmdline() {
2694            let (process, area) = crate::tool_global::process_cmdline(guest, process).await;
2695            return process != self.own_detpid(guest) || area.is_none();
2696        }
2697        if !file.is_process_auxv() || without_mm {
2698            return false;
2699        }
2700        crate::tool_global::process_auxv(guest, process)
2701            .await
2702            .is_none()
2703    }
2704
2705    /// Where a rendered `stat`'s name comes from on this backend
2706    /// (`procfs::task_stat::CommSource::of_backend`): the kernel beneath's
2707    /// own where its guest threads are host tasks the tool can name (the
2708    /// declared `tool_can_name_guest_descriptors` capability: ptrace, DBT,
2709    /// SaBRe, LiteInst), Detcore's record elsewhere (KVM).
2710    pub(super) fn comm_source(&self) -> crate::procfs::task_stat::CommSource {
2711        crate::procfs::task_stat::CommSource::of_backend(
2712            crate::procfs_inode::tool_can_name_guest_descriptors(&self.cfg.backend),
2713        )
2714    }
2715
2716    /// Whether `file` is a task's `comm` and the name's source on this
2717    /// backend is the kernel's own ([`Self::comm_source`]): then every
2718    /// spelling of every task's `comm`, its lookup, metadata and listing
2719    /// included, is the kernel's, unclassified, exactly as before Detcore
2720    /// rendered any (package K slice A+), decided before any request to the
2721    /// global tool, so those backends pay nothing for the file.
2722    pub(super) fn kernel_serves_every_comm(&self, file: &ProcfsFile) -> bool {
2723        file.is_task_comm() && self.comm_source() == crate::procfs::task_stat::CommSource::Kernel
2724    }
2725
2726    /// Resolve `path`, an absolute spelling with a `..`, where Detcore renders
2727    /// `comm` (its record is the name's source, package K slice A+):
2728    /// [`walk_comm_alias`], then each task the walk passed must exist in
2729    /// Detcore's process table, in order, as Linux's lookup requires of the
2730    /// components a `..` later undoes (ENOENT otherwise); a walk that fails
2731    /// is Linux's error there (ENOENT, ENOTDIR). Only global-tool requests,
2732    /// no injected call. An undetermined walk is returned unchecked: only a
2733    /// write open refuses it ([`Self::refuse_undetermined_comm_write`]).
2734    pub(super) async fn resolve_comm_alias<G: Guest<Self>>(
2735        &self,
2736        guest: &mut G,
2737        path: &Path,
2738    ) -> Result<CommAlias, Error> {
2739        if self.comm_source() != crate::procfs::task_stat::CommSource::Record {
2740            return Ok(CommAlias::NotApplicable);
2741        }
2742        let Some(own) = self.procfs_caller_ids(guest) else {
2743            return Ok(CommAlias::NotApplicable);
2744        };
2745        let (checks, outcome) = match walk_comm_alias(path, own) {
2746            CommAliasWalk::NotApplicable => return Ok(CommAlias::NotApplicable),
2747            CommAliasWalk::Undetermined(_) => return Ok(CommAlias::Undetermined),
2748            CommAliasWalk::Comm { checks, task } => (checks, Ok(task)),
2749            CommAliasWalk::Fails { checks, errno } => (checks, Err(errno)),
2750        };
2751        self.check_alias_tasks(guest, checks).await?;
2752        let task = outcome?;
2753        Ok(CommAlias::Resolved(std::path::PathBuf::from(
2754            match task.thread {
2755                Some(thread) => format!("/proc/{}/task/{thread}/comm", task.process),
2756                None => format!("/proc/{}/comm", task.process),
2757            },
2758        )))
2759    }
2760
2761    /// ENOENT unless every task in `checks` exists in Detcore's process
2762    /// table (`tool_global::procfs_task`, the lookup Linux makes of
2763    /// `/proc/<n>` and `task/<tid>`), checked in order.
2764    async fn check_alias_tasks<G: Guest<Self>>(
2765        &self,
2766        guest: &mut G,
2767        checks: Vec<crate::procfs::BoundTask>,
2768    ) -> Result<(), Error> {
2769        for task in checks {
2770            if !crate::tool_global::procfs_task(guest, task).await.exists {
2771                return Err(Errno::ENOENT.into());
2772            }
2773        }
2774        Ok(())
2775    }
2776
2777    /// Refuse an open with `flags` that asks to write of `path`, an absolute
2778    /// `/proc` spelling with a `..` and a last component `comm`, whose walk
2779    /// Detcore cannot finish ([`CommAlias::Undetermined`]): it may name a
2780    /// `comm` Detcore renders, whose write open is refused
2781    /// ([`asks_to_write_comm`]), and forwarding it would hand the kernel
2782    /// beneath a writable open of it (package K slice A+;
2783    /// <https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4263>). The tasks the walk
2784    /// passed are checked first (ENOENT, as Linux's lookup); then the
2785    /// failure policy's named refusal. Nothing else is refused.
2786    pub(super) async fn refuse_undetermined_comm_write<G: Guest<Self>>(
2787        &self,
2788        guest: &mut G,
2789        path: &Path,
2790        flags: OFlag,
2791    ) -> Result<(), Error> {
2792        if !open_asks_to_write(flags)
2793            || !self.renders_procfs(guest)
2794            || self.comm_source() != crate::procfs::task_stat::CommSource::Record
2795        {
2796            return Ok(());
2797        }
2798        let Some(own) = self.procfs_caller_ids(guest) else {
2799            return Ok(());
2800        };
2801        // The walk alone (no request): a spelling it resolves, or fails, is
2802        // checked once, by `Self::rendered_procfs_at`.
2803        if let CommAliasWalk::Undetermined(checks) = walk_comm_alias(path, own) {
2804            self.check_alias_tasks(guest, checks).await?;
2805            let reason = format!(
2806                "a writable open of {} passes a /proc component Detcore does not model before \
2807                 its `..` and may name a task's comm, which Detcore renders and does not write \
2808                 (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4263)",
2809                path.display()
2810            );
2811            return Err(
2812                Self::scratch_failure(guest, reverie::syscalls::Sysno::openat, reason).await,
2813            );
2814        }
2815        Ok(())
2816    }
2817
2818    /// The parent Detcore's model records for another process's rendered
2819    /// `stat` (`parent`), numbered as that process's own `getppid` answers
2820    /// it, which Detcore passes to the kernel: where the kernel numbers
2821    /// processes otherwise than Detcore (DBT,
2822    /// `Self::translates_procfs_numbers`), the kernel's number for that
2823    /// guest process (`tool_global::procfs_ids`); a number of no guest
2824    /// process (the namespace's init, 1, whose number both numberings share,
2825    /// and 0 for a parent outside the PID namespace, the only outside
2826    /// parents a guest has) as it is. So a descriptor that reached another
2827    /// process shows the parent the described process's own `stat` shows,
2828    /// the reader's `getppid` answer, on every backend.
2829    async fn getppid_numbered_parent<G: Guest<Self>>(guest: &mut G, parent: i32) -> i32 {
2830        if parent <= 1 || !Self::registered_kernel_ids(guest) {
2831            return parent;
2832        }
2833        crate::tool_global::procfs_ids(guest, true, vec![parent])
2834            .await
2835            .into_iter()
2836            .next()
2837            .flatten()
2838            .unwrap_or(parent)
2839    }
2840
2841    /// Whether this guest thread's backend registered its kernel ids (DBT,
2842    /// and an in-guest native exec, whose numberings agree), so that a
2843    /// number Detcore gives a task may not be the kernel's: whatever the
2844    /// guest's root or PID namespace, which `Self::translates_procfs_numbers`
2845    /// also weighs because it translates the guest's own paths. A rendered
2846    /// `stat` reaches the container's procfs by the tool's own spelling, so
2847    /// it translates by this alone. `tool_global::procfs_ids` returns each
2848    /// number unchanged where the numberings agree.
2849    fn registered_kernel_ids<G: Guest<Self>>(guest: &G) -> bool {
2850        guest.thread_state().physical_tid.is_some()
2851    }
2852
2853    /// The kernel beneath's name (`comm`) of the task a rendered `stat`
2854    /// describes (the thread below `task/`, else the task the top-level
2855    /// number names), read by the tool itself from that task's own procfs
2856    /// directory, `/proc/<pid>/task/<tid>/comm` (`/proc/<pid>/comm` for a
2857    /// top-level number), by the kernel's numbers for them
2858    /// (`tool_global::procfs_ids` wherever the backend registered kernel ids,
2859    /// [`Self::registered_kernel_ids`], whatever the guest's root or PID
2860    /// namespace), with raw syscalls; never through the reader's own
2861    /// `/proc/self`, so a descriptor that reached another process (across
2862    /// fork or `SCM_RIGHTS`) reads the described task's name. Only where
2863    /// [`Self::comm_source`] is the kernel. It runs in the reader's turn, and
2864    /// the name is the guest's own history's: every way Linux renames a task
2865    /// (an exec, by Linux's own rule for the host's kernel version, a clone,
2866    /// `PR_SET_NAME`, a write to a `comm` file by any spelling) is a guest
2867    /// system call, sequentialized.
2868    ///
2869    /// Where the tool shares the guest's root (DBT, SaBRe, LiteInst) and the
2870    /// guest has changed it, nothing is read: the path would be looked up
2871    /// through the guest's own root, where the guest decides what
2872    /// /proc/<pid>/comm is (an ordinary file, a link into a bind-mounted
2873    /// procfs naming another task, a bind-mounted subdirectory), and no
2874    /// check on the opened file can tell that it is the described task's.
2875    /// That read is the failure policy's named refusal (declared). A tracer
2876    /// (ptrace) reads its own `/proc`, which no guest `chroot` changes.
2877    /// Otherwise the file must be on a procfs ([`comm_file_is_on_procfs`]),
2878    /// which refuses an ordinary file mounted over the path.
2879    /// `Err` names why there is no name.
2880    async fn kernel_task_comm<G: Guest<Self>>(
2881        &self,
2882        guest: &mut G,
2883        task: crate::procfs::BoundTask,
2884    ) -> Result<crate::procfs::task_stat::TaskComm, &'static str> {
2885        let changed_root = guest
2886            .thread_state()
2887            .changed_root
2888            .load(std::sync::atomic::Ordering::SeqCst);
2889        if !comm_read_is_trusted(
2890            guest.config().backend.tool_shares_guest_descriptor_table,
2891            changed_root,
2892        ) {
2893            return Err(
2894                "the guest changed its root, and the tool, which shares it, would look the \
2895                 task's comm up through it",
2896            );
2897        }
2898        let unreadable = "the tool could not read the kernel's comm of the task";
2899        let mut ids = vec![task.process];
2900        ids.extend(task.thread);
2901        let kernel = if Self::registered_kernel_ids(guest) {
2902            crate::tool_global::procfs_ids(guest, true, ids)
2903                .await
2904                .into_iter()
2905                .collect::<Option<Vec<_>>>()
2906                .ok_or(unreadable)?
2907        } else {
2908            ids
2909        };
2910        let path = match kernel[..] {
2911            [process] => format!("/proc/{process}/comm"),
2912            [process, thread] => format!("/proc/{process}/task/{thread}/comm"),
2913            _ => return Err(unreadable),
2914        };
2915        let file =
2916            crate::util::RawFd::open(Path::new(&path), libc::O_RDONLY).map_err(|_| unreadable)?;
2917        if !comm_file_is_on_procfs(&file) {
2918            return Err("the task's comm path is not on a procfs");
2919        }
2920        let mut bytes = [0_u8; 2 * crate::procfs::task_stat::TASK_COMM_LEN];
2921        let read = file.read_at(&mut bytes, 0).map_err(|_| unreadable)?;
2922        Ok(crate::procfs::task_stat::TaskComm::from_comm_file(
2923            &bytes[..read],
2924        ))
2925    }
2926
2927    /// The facts a snapshot of the rendered `stat` open as `fd` renders
2928    /// (`procfs::task_stat::TaskStat`), taken at its first read and again
2929    /// where Linux's `seq_file` would run `do_task_stat` again
2930    /// (`ProcfsFile::drop_stale_task_snapshot`). The same for a rendered
2931    /// `comm` (package K slice A+), which shows only the name, chosen by the
2932    /// same rule, where `comm_show` would run again. `None` for any other
2933    /// file.
2934    ///
2935    /// - The task must still be the one the file was bound to (ESRCH
2936    ///   otherwise), as `do_task_stat`'s `get_proc_task` requires.
2937    /// - The name, from [`Self::comm_source`]
2938    ///   (`procfs::task_stat::stat_comm`): the kernel's own
2939    ///   ([`Self::kernel_task_comm`]) where the backend's guest threads are
2940    ///   host tasks, and a failed read there is the failure policy's named
2941    ///   refusal, never Detcore's record, which a host resource would then
2942    ///   choose and which a write to a `comm` file leaves stale; Detcore's
2943    ///   record elsewhere (KVM), and with none, the same refusal.
2944    /// - The parent: the reader's own `getppid` answer (`ppid`) for its own
2945    ///   process; for another process's file (a descriptor that reached the
2946    ///   reader across `fork` or `SCM_RIGHTS`), the guest-visible parent
2947    ///   Detcore's model records (`procfs::task_stat::TaskStatFacts::parent`),
2948    ///   numbered as that process's `getppid` answers it
2949    ///   ([`Self::getppid_numbered_parent`]).
2950    /// - `rsslim`: the described process's virtual `RLIMIT_RSS`, whoever
2951    ///   reads it (`procfs::task_stat::TaskStatFacts::rsslim`).
2952    pub(super) async fn rendered_task_stat<G: Guest<Self>>(
2953        &self,
2954        guest: &mut G,
2955        fd: RawFd,
2956        ppid: i32,
2957    ) -> Result<Option<crate::procfs::task_stat::TaskStat>, Error> {
2958        let Some(path) = guest
2959            .thread_state()
2960            .with_detfd(fd, |detfd| detfd.procfs_rendered_path())?
2961        else {
2962            return Ok(None);
2963        };
2964        let Some(file) = ProcfsFile::from_rendered_path(path)
2965            .filter(|file| file.is_task_stat() || file.is_task_comm())
2966        else {
2967            return Ok(None);
2968        };
2969        let task = file.bound_task().ok_or(Errno::ESRCH)?;
2970        match self.rendered_task_state(guest, path).await {
2971            Some((_, true)) => {}
2972            _ => return Err(Errno::ESRCH.into()),
2973        }
2974        let facts = crate::tool_global::task_stat_facts(guest, task).await;
2975        let source = self.comm_source();
2976        let kernel = if source == crate::procfs::task_stat::CommSource::Kernel {
2977            self.kernel_task_comm(guest, task).await
2978        } else {
2979            Err("the backend's tasks have no kernel comm the tool can read")
2980        };
2981        let comm = match crate::procfs::task_stat::stat_comm(source, kernel, facts.comm) {
2982            Ok(comm) => comm,
2983            Err(why) => {
2984                let file_name = if file.is_task_comm() { "comm" } else { "stat" };
2985                let reason = format!(
2986                    "the /proc/<pid>/{file_name} of task {task:?} has no name to render: {why}"
2987                );
2988                return Err(Self::scratch_failure(guest, Sysno::read, reason).await);
2989            }
2990        };
2991        let described = task.thread.unwrap_or(task.process);
2992        let own = facts.process == Some(self.own_detpid(guest).as_raw());
2993        // A `comm` shows the name alone.
2994        let ppid = if own || file.is_task_comm() {
2995            ppid
2996        } else {
2997            Self::getppid_numbered_parent(guest, facts.parent.unwrap_or(0)).await
2998        };
2999        Ok(Some(crate::procfs::task_stat::TaskStat {
3000            pid: described,
3001            comm,
3002            facts,
3003            reader_is_task: described == guest.thread_state().dettid.as_raw(),
3004            ppid,
3005            rsslim: facts.rsslim,
3006        }))
3007    }
3008
3009    /// One plain read of the rendered `/proc/<pid>/cmdline` open as `fd`, of
3010    /// `count` bytes at position `pos` into the caller's buffer at
3011    /// `destination`, as Linux's `proc_pid_cmdline_read` reads it
3012    /// (fs/proc/base.c:393-408): it finds the descriptor's task again (ESRCH
3013    /// where it is gone, or another task now holds its numbers), reads
3014    /// nothing where the task has no memory map (a zombie, or the top-level
3015    /// file of a thread group whose leader has exited: `get_task_mm`), and
3016    /// otherwise reads the process's memory now, at the read's position,
3017    /// page by page, copying out after each page (`procfs::read_cmdline`).
3018    /// Returns the bytes copied.
3019    ///
3020    /// Detcore reads only the calling process's memory. Where the file is
3021    /// another process's (a descriptor its owner opened that reached the
3022    /// caller across `fork` or `SCM_RIGHTS`), the read goes to the kernel
3023    /// beneath's file of that task ([`Self::kernel_cmdline_read`]), as before
3024    /// Detcore rendered it, and never to a copy.
3025    pub(crate) async fn read_rendered_cmdline<G: Guest<Self>>(
3026        &self,
3027        guest: &mut G,
3028        fd: RawFd,
3029        destination: usize,
3030        count: usize,
3031        pos: u64,
3032    ) -> Result<usize, Error> {
3033        let path = guest
3034            .thread_state()
3035            .with_detfd(fd, |detfd| detfd.procfs_rendered_path())?
3036            .ok_or(Errno::EBADF)?;
3037        let task = ProcfsFile::from_rendered_path(path)
3038            .and_then(|file| file.bound_task())
3039            .ok_or(Errno::ESRCH)?;
3040        match self.rendered_task_state(guest, path).await {
3041            Some((_, false)) | None => return Err(Errno::ESRCH.into()),
3042            Some((answer, true)) if answer.without_mm => return Ok(0),
3043            Some(_) => {}
3044        }
3045        let (process, area) = crate::tool_global::process_cmdline(
3046            guest,
3047            detcore_model::pid::DetPid::from_raw(task.process),
3048        )
3049        .await;
3050        // A read of zero bytes has found its task, which is all Linux's
3051        // does before get_mm_cmdline returns 0 (base.c:325-328): no memory,
3052        // and no injection.
3053        if count == 0 {
3054            return Ok(0);
3055        }
3056        // Another process's file, or the caller's own where Detcore recorded
3057        // no argument area for its current image (a stack layout
3058        // initial_stack_cmdline_area refused, or one it could not read, on a
3059        // backend that reports the initial stack pointer; a descriptor kept
3060        // across an exec into such an image), is the kernel beneath's.
3061        let Some(area) = area.filter(|_| process == self.own_detpid(guest)) else {
3062            return Box::pin(self.kernel_cmdline_read(guest, task, area, destination, count, pos))
3063                .await;
3064        };
3065        let mut memory = guest.memory();
3066        let mut adapter = GuestCmdlineMemory {
3067            memory: &mut memory,
3068            destination,
3069            failure: None,
3070        };
3071        let read = crate::procfs::read_cmdline(&area, pos, count, &mut adapter);
3072        if let Some(failure) = adapter.failure {
3073            return Err(failure.into());
3074        }
3075        Ok(read?)
3076    }
3077
3078    /// One read of `count` bytes at `pos` of the kernel beneath's
3079    /// `/proc/<pid>/cmdline` (or `/proc/<pid>/task/<tid>/cmdline`) of `task`,
3080    /// into the caller's buffer at `destination`: the caller's own open of
3081    /// the kernel's file by the kernel's numbers
3082    /// (`Self::kernel_procfs_number`), a `pread64` into the caller's buffer,
3083    /// and the close ([`Self::inject_cmdline_pread`]). On ptrace the kernel
3084    /// reads the task's memory as Linux does; where the kernel beneath serves
3085    /// no `/proc` for the caller (KVM), its answer to the open is the read's
3086    /// (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4564). The path lives in
3087    /// borrowed stack scratch whose bytes go back on every path, or, where
3088    /// that scratch would overlap the caller's buffer (whose bytes its
3089    /// restoration would erase) or the target's recorded argument and
3090    /// environment area `source` (which the caller shares when the target
3091    /// is a `CLONE_VM` relative, and whose bytes the kernel would read with
3092    /// the path written over them), in a transient private page; where that
3093    /// page overlaps either too, or a step Detcore cannot finish cleanly, the
3094    /// read goes to the failure policy.
3095    ///
3096    /// This reopens a pathname in the caller's own root, mount namespace,
3097    /// descriptor table and seccomp filter, where Linux's read of the open
3098    /// descriptor needs none of them: a caller that changed its root or mount
3099    /// namespace so that `/proc` is not the kernel's procfs reads ENOENT or
3100    /// another file, a full descriptor table gives EMFILE, and a seccomp
3101    /// filter that refuses the open gives its answer
3102    /// (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4568).
3103    async fn kernel_cmdline_read<G: Guest<Self>>(
3104        &self,
3105        guest: &mut G,
3106        task: crate::procfs::BoundTask,
3107        source: Option<crate::procfs::CmdlineArea>,
3108        destination: usize,
3109        count: usize,
3110        pos: u64,
3111    ) -> Result<usize, Error> {
3112        const PATH_CAPACITY: usize = 64;
3113        let process = Self::kernel_procfs_number(guest, task.process)
3114            .await
3115            .ok_or(Errno::ESRCH)?;
3116        let path = match task.thread {
3117            Some(thread) => {
3118                let thread = Self::kernel_procfs_number(guest, thread)
3119                    .await
3120                    .ok_or(Errno::ESRCH)?;
3121                format!("/proc/{process}/task/{thread}/cmdline")
3122            }
3123            None => format!("/proc/{process}/cmdline"),
3124        };
3125        let borrowed = match Self::borrow_scratch::<G, PATH_CAPACITY>(guest).await {
3126            Ok(Some(borrowed)) => borrowed,
3127            Ok(None) => {
3128                return Err(Self::scratch_failure(
3129                    guest,
3130                    Sysno::read,
3131                    format!("no guest stack scratch can hold the path {path}"),
3132                )
3133                .await);
3134            }
3135            Err(reason) => return Err(Self::scratch_failure(guest, Sysno::read, reason).await),
3136        };
3137        let (scratch, original, stack_guard) = borrowed;
3138        let overlaps = |at: usize, length: usize| {
3139            cmdline_path_overlaps(at, length, destination, count, source)
3140        };
3141        if !overlaps(scratch.as_raw(), PATH_CAPACITY) {
3142            let answer =
3143                Self::inject_cmdline_pread(guest, scratch, &path, destination, count, pos).await;
3144            let restored = guest.memory().write_exact(scratch, &original);
3145            drop(stack_guard);
3146            if let Err(errno) = restored {
3147                return Err(Self::scratch_failure(
3148                    guest,
3149                    Sysno::read,
3150                    format!(
3151                        "the guest stack scratch under a kernel cmdline read could not be \
3152                         restored: {errno}"
3153                    ),
3154                )
3155                .await);
3156            }
3157            return match answer {
3158                Ok(read) => Ok(read?),
3159                Err(reason) => Err(Self::scratch_failure(guest, Sysno::read, reason).await),
3160            };
3161        }
3162        // The stack scratch overlaps the caller's buffer (a buffer below the
3163        // stack pointer), whose bytes the scratch's restoration would erase,
3164        // or the target's argument area (a CLONE_VM child whose stack pointer
3165        // lies in the parent's arguments), whose bytes the path would replace
3166        // while the kernel reads them. Put the stack back untouched and hold
3167        // the path in a transient private page instead, a new mapping that no
3168        // mapped buffer or area can lie in.
3169        let restored = guest.memory().write_exact(scratch, &original);
3170        drop(stack_guard);
3171        if let Err(errno) = restored {
3172            return Err(Self::scratch_failure(
3173                guest,
3174                Sysno::read,
3175                format!("the guest stack scratch could not be put back unused: {errno}"),
3176            )
3177            .await);
3178        }
3179        const PAGE: usize = 4096;
3180        let Some(page) = super::procfs_levels::map_transient(guest, PAGE).await else {
3181            return Err(Self::scratch_failure(
3182                guest,
3183                Sysno::read,
3184                format!("no transient page can hold the path {path}"),
3185            )
3186            .await);
3187        };
3188        if overlaps(page.as_raw(), PAGE) {
3189            super::procfs_levels::unmap_transient(guest, page, PAGE).await;
3190            return Err(Self::scratch_failure(
3191                guest,
3192                Sysno::read,
3193                format!(
3194                    "the transient page for the path {path} overlaps the caller's buffer or \
3195                     the target's argument area"
3196                ),
3197            )
3198            .await);
3199        }
3200        let answer = Self::inject_cmdline_pread(guest, page, &path, destination, count, pos).await;
3201        super::procfs_levels::unmap_transient(guest, page, PAGE).await;
3202        match answer {
3203            Ok(read) => Ok(read?),
3204            Err(reason) => Err(Self::scratch_failure(guest, Sysno::read, reason).await),
3205        }
3206    }
3207
3208    /// The caller's open of the kernel's `path`, written to `scratch`, a
3209    /// `pread64` of `count` bytes at `pos` into the caller's buffer at
3210    /// `destination`, and the close ([`Self::kernel_cmdline_read`]). An
3211    /// injected call Reverie reports did not run (ERESTARTSYS) is retried.
3212    /// `Ok(Err(errno))` is the kernel's answer to the open or the read;
3213    /// `Err` is a step Detcore could not finish cleanly.
3214    async fn inject_cmdline_pread<G: Guest<Self>>(
3215        guest: &mut G,
3216        scratch: AddrMut<'static, u8>,
3217        path: &str,
3218        destination: usize,
3219        count: usize,
3220        pos: u64,
3221    ) -> Result<Result<usize, Errno>, String> {
3222        const PATH_CAPACITY: usize = 64;
3223        if let Err(errno) = guest
3224            .memory()
3225            .write_exact(scratch, &c_string::<PATH_CAPACITY>(path.as_bytes()))
3226        {
3227            return Err(format!(
3228                "the path {path} could not be written to the guest scratch: {errno}"
3229            ));
3230        }
3231        let opened = loop {
3232            match guest
3233                .inject(
3234                    syscalls::Openat::new()
3235                        .with_dirfd(libc::AT_FDCWD)
3236                        .with_path(PathPtr::from_ptr(scratch.as_raw() as *const libc::c_char))
3237                        .with_flags(OFlag::O_RDONLY | OFlag::O_CLOEXEC),
3238                )
3239                .await
3240            {
3241                Err(Errno::ERESTARTSYS) => continue,
3242                opened => break opened,
3243            }
3244        };
3245        let fd = match opened {
3246            Ok(fd) => fd as RawFd,
3247            Err(errno) => return Ok(Err(errno)),
3248        };
3249        let read = loop {
3250            match guest
3251                .inject(
3252                    syscalls::Pread64::new()
3253                        .with_fd(fd)
3254                        .with_buf(AddrMut::from_raw(destination))
3255                        .with_len(count)
3256                        .with_offset(pos as libc::loff_t),
3257                )
3258                .await
3259            {
3260                Err(Errno::ERESTARTSYS) => continue,
3261                read => break read.map(|copied| copied as usize),
3262            }
3263        };
3264        loop {
3265            match guest.inject(syscalls::Close::new().with_fd(fd)).await {
3266                Ok(_) | Err(Errno::EINTR) => return Ok(read),
3267                Err(Errno::ERESTARTSYS) => continue,
3268                Err(errno) => {
3269                    return Err(format!(
3270                        "the descriptor {fd} of {path} could not be closed: {errno}"
3271                    ));
3272                }
3273            }
3274        }
3275    }
3276
3277    /// Bind the per-process file `file` to the identity of the task the
3278    /// process table's `answer` describes (`ProcfsFile::set_task_incarnation`),
3279    /// which numbers its path and inode. A task generation beyond what an
3280    /// inode number tells apart (`procfs_inode::MAX_INCARNATION`: the 256th
3281    /// task to hold one number in a run) is refused by name through the
3282    /// unsupported-operation policy: a fail-closed run stops with the
3283    /// policy's exit and the reason, and a run started with
3284    /// `--allow-unsupported-syscalls` gets EOVERFLOW. Never another task's
3285    /// number, and never a panic.
3286    pub(super) async fn bind_task_identity<G: Guest<Self>>(
3287        &self,
3288        guest: &mut G,
3289        file: &mut ProcfsFile,
3290        answer: crate::procfs::listing::ProcfsTaskAnswer,
3291    ) -> Result<(), Error> {
3292        let incarnation = answer.incarnation();
3293        if procfs_inode::incarnation_fits(incarnation) {
3294            file.set_task_incarnation(incarnation);
3295            return Ok(());
3296        }
3297        let task = file.bound_task();
3298        let reason = format!(
3299            "the per-process /proc file of task {task:?} would belong to its numbers' \
3300             incarnation {incarnation:?}, beyond the {} task generations an inode number \
3301             of such a file tells apart (procfs_inode::MAX_INCARNATION)",
3302            procfs_inode::MAX_INCARNATION
3303        );
3304        let sysno = reverie::syscalls::Sysno::new(guest.regs().await.orig_rax as usize)
3305            .unwrap_or(reverie::syscalls::Sysno::openat);
3306        error!("Detcore refuses {sysno}: {reason}");
3307        crate::syscalls::helpers::refuse_unserviceable_operation_with(
3308            guest,
3309            self.cfg.panic_on_unsupported_syscalls,
3310            sysno,
3311            Errno::EOVERFLOW,
3312            Some(&reason),
3313        )
3314        .await
3315        .map(|_| ())
3316    }
3317
3318    /// Where the tool runs in each guest process, take a placeholder's cursor
3319    /// from its kernel position, before a read or seek uses it. Processes
3320    /// that share the open file across `fork` share only that position, which
3321    /// Detcore keeps at each one's cursor after every read and seek
3322    /// (`sync_rendered_procfs_position`). Where one tool process serves every
3323    /// guest, the cursor itself is shared.
3324    ///
3325    /// DECLARED for a rendered `stat` (package K slice D4c-1), whose
3326    /// description is never promoted to Detcore's global state: where the
3327    /// tool runs in each guest process (DBT, SaBRe, LiteInst), each process
3328    /// that holds it after `fork` keeps its own copy of its snapshot and
3329    /// sequence (`ProcfsFile::read_pos`), and only this kernel cursor is
3330    /// shared. A read by a process whose copy holds no snapshot, or whose
3331    /// sequence is not at the shared cursor (another process read or seeked
3332    /// since), renders the record anew at the shared cursor, in the reader's
3333    /// turn, where Linux continues the one buffered record (and is at its end
3334    /// once another process consumed it). A stat descriptor sent with
3335    /// `SCM_RIGHTS` to another process that holds no alias of the same open
3336    /// file Detcore models is rebuilt there from its placeholder (the registry
3337    /// of placeholders is each tool process's own) and renders anew at its
3338    /// next read, even while the sender still holds it; a receiver that
3339    /// already holds an alias (inherited at `fork`, say) joins that local
3340    /// description (`FileMetadata::adopt_placeholder`) and shares its copy's
3341    /// state. Every outcome follows from the guest's own sequentialized
3342    /// history.
3343    pub(super) async fn adopt_rendered_procfs_position<G: Guest<Self>>(
3344        &self,
3345        guest: &mut G,
3346        fd: RawFd,
3347    ) -> Result<(), Error> {
3348        if !self.cfg.backend.tool_shares_guest_descriptor_table {
3349            return Ok(());
3350        }
3351        let kept = guest
3352            .thread_state()
3353            .with_detfd(fd, |detfd| detfd.procfs_keeps_kernel_position())
3354            .unwrap_or(false);
3355        if !kept {
3356            return Ok(());
3357        }
3358        // An `O_PATH` descriptor has no position (EBADF): nothing to adopt.
3359        if let Ok(offset) = guest
3360            .inject(
3361                syscalls::Lseek::new()
3362                    .with_fd(fd)
3363                    .with_offset(0)
3364                    .with_whence(syscalls::Whence::SEEK_CUR),
3365            )
3366            .await
3367        {
3368            guest
3369                .thread_state()
3370                .with_detfd(fd, |detfd| detfd.set_procfs_offset(offset as usize))?;
3371        }
