3372        Ok(())
3373    }
3374
3375    /// Keep a placeholder's kernel position at Detcore's cursor, which a
3376    /// backend that rediscovers descriptors after `exec`, or another process
3377    /// sharing the open file, reads back (`adopt_rendered_procfs_position`).
3378    pub(super) async fn sync_rendered_procfs_position<G: Guest<Self>>(
3379        &self,
3380        guest: &mut G,
3381        fd: RawFd,
3382    ) -> Result<(), Error> {
3383        let (offset, placeholder) = guest.thread_state().with_detfd(fd, |detfd| {
3384            let offset = detfd
3385                .procfs_keeps_kernel_position()
3386                .then(|| detfd.procfs_position().map(|(offset, _)| offset))
3387                .flatten();
3388            (offset, detfd.procfs_rendered_path().is_some())
3389        })?;
3390        if let Some(offset) = offset {
3391            let moved = guest
3392                .inject(
3393                    syscalls::Lseek::new()
3394                        .with_fd(fd)
3395                        .with_offset(offset as libc::off_t)
3396                        .with_whence(syscalls::Whence::SEEK_SET),
3397                )
3398                .await;
3399            // A placeholder takes any position. The kernel's own file may
3400            // refuse one past its contents, which differ from Detcore's;
3401            // Detcore's cursor still answers the guest.
3402            if placeholder {
3403                moved?;
3404            }
3405        }
3406        Ok(())
3407    }
3408
3409    /// The metadata of the rendered procfs file `target` names, if it names
3410    /// one.
3411    pub(super) async fn rendered_procfs_stat<G: Guest<Self>>(
3412        &self,
3413        guest: &mut G,
3414        target: StatTarget<'_>,
3415    ) -> Result<Option<DetStat>, Error> {
3416        let path = match target {
3417            StatTarget::Descriptor(fd) => {
3418                match guest
3419                    .thread_state()
3420                    .with_detfd(fd, |detfd| detfd.procfs_rendered_path())
3421                    .ok()
3422                    .flatten()
3423                {
3424                    Some(path) => Some(path),
3425                    // With metadata virtualized, `determinize_stat` already
3426                    // knows a placeholder by its identity.
3427                    None if !guest.config().virtualize_metadata => {
3428                        self.placeholder_descriptor(guest, fd).await
3429                    }
3430                    None => None,
3431                }
3432            }
3433            StatTarget::Path {
3434                dirfd,
3435                path: Some(pointer),
3436                follow,
3437            } => {
3438                let path = pointer.read(&guest.memory())?;
3439                // Without `AT_EMPTY_PATH` (which names a descriptor), an
3440                // empty path names nothing: the kernel's ENOENT.
3441                if path.as_os_str().is_empty() {
3442                    return Ok(None);
3443                }
3444                let observed = self.procfs_spelling_at(guest, dirfd, &path).await?;
3445                match self.rendered_procfs_at(guest, &observed).await? {
3446                    // A link's own metadata is not its target's: `lstat` of
3447                    // `/proc/self/fd/N` describes the link, which the kernel has.
3448                    Some(target) if follow || !target.via_link => {
3449                        target.procfs.rendered_path(self.rendered_files(guest))
3450                    }
3451                    Some(_) => None,
3452                    None if !guest.config().virtualize_metadata => {
3453                        self.placeholder_reached(guest, dirfd, Some(pointer), follow)
3454                            .await
3455                    }
3456                    None => None,
3457                }
3458            }
3459            StatTarget::Path { path: None, .. } | StatTarget::Cwd | StatTarget::Overwritten => None,
3460        };
3461        let Some(path) = path else {
3462            return Ok(None);
3463        };
3464        Ok(Some(self.rendered_metadata(guest, path).await?))
3465    }
3466
3467    /// Write the guest's procfs statistics, as `statfs` of a procfs file
3468    /// Detcore renders reports them, to `buffer`.
3469    pub(super) fn write_procfs_statfs<G: Guest<Self>>(
3470        &self,
3471        guest: &mut G,
3472        buffer: Option<AddrMut<'_, libc::statfs>>,
3473    ) -> Result<(), Error> {
3474        let (statfs, _) = GuestProcfs::statfs()?;
3475        let buffer = buffer.ok_or(Errno::EFAULT)?;
3476        guest.memory().write_value(buffer, &statfs)?;
3477        self.canonicalize_statfs_buf(guest, Some(buffer))
3478    }
3479
3480    /// Answer `readlink` of `path` if it names a procfs file Detcore renders:
3481    /// the file itself is no symbolic link, and this process's descriptor
3482    /// link to it names its path, as Linux's names the file's.
3483    pub(crate) async fn readlink_rendered_procfs<G: Guest<Self>>(
3484        &self,
3485        guest: &mut G,
3486        path: &Path,
3487        buffer: Option<AddrMut<'_, libc::c_char>>,
3488        buffer_len: usize,
3489    ) -> Result<Option<i64>, Error> {
3490        let Some(target) = self.rendered_procfs_at(guest, path).await? else {
3491            return Ok(None);
3492        };
3493        let size = crate::syscalls::namespace::readlink_size(buffer_len)?;
3494        if !target.via_link {
3495            return Err(Errno::EINVAL.into());
3496        }
3497        // `/proc/mounts`, a link the kernel's procfs has: its target is
3498        // `self/mounts`, which the kernel reads.
3499        let Some(link) = target.procfs.opened_path() else {
3500            return Ok(None);
3501        };
3502        write_link(&mut guest.memory(), link, buffer, size).map(Some)
3503    }
3504
3505    /// Rewrite a descriptor link that the kernel wrote to `buffer`, `result`
3506    /// bytes, if it names a placeholder: another process's link to a
3507    /// rendered file names the file's path, as Linux's does. (A buffer too
3508    /// small for the placeholder's link keeps the kernel's truncated bytes.)
3509    pub(crate) async fn rewrite_placeholder_link<G: Guest<Self>>(
3510        &self,
3511        guest: &mut G,
3512        link: (RawFd, Option<PathPtr<'_>>),
3513        buffer: Option<AddrMut<'_, libc::c_char>>,
3514        buffer_len: usize,
3515        result: i64,
3516        saved: Option<&[u8]>,
3517    ) -> Result<i64, Error> {
3518        let (Some(address), Ok(length)) = (buffer, usize::try_from(result)) else {
3519            return Ok(result);
3520        };
3521        // Nothing to rewrite where no placeholder can be reached: one tool
3522        // process serves every guest and knows none.
3523        if self.cfg.recordreplay_modes
3524            || length == 0
3525            || (!placeholders_exist() && !self.cfg.backend.tool_shares_guest_descriptor_table)
3526        {
3527            return Ok(result);
3528        }
3529        let mut bytes = vec![0; length.min(buffer_len)];
3530        guest
3531            .memory()
3532            .read_exact(address.cast::<u8>(), &mut bytes)?;
3533        match placeholder_link_path(&bytes, self.rendered_files(guest)) {
3534            Some(path) if self.placeholder_link_confirmed(guest, link).await => {
3535                let written = write_link(&mut guest.memory(), path, buffer, buffer_len)?;
3536                crate::syscalls::namespace::restore_link_tail(
3537                    guest,
3538                    address,
3539                    written as usize,
3540                    bytes.len(),
3541                    saved,
3542                )?;
3543                Ok(written)
3544            }
3545            _ => Ok(result),
3546        }
3547    }
3548
3549    /// Linux's answer to cloning descriptor `source`'s data into a rendered
3550    /// file: no such descriptor, or one on procfs itself (where the
3551    /// read-only destination is checked next), is EBADF; another file system
3552    /// is EXDEV.
3553    fn clone_ioctl_answer<G: Guest<Self>>(guest: &mut G, source: RawFd) -> Result<i64, Errno> {
3554        let on_procfs = guest
3555            .thread_state()
3556            .with_detfd(source, |detfd| {
3557                detfd.procfs_rendered_path().is_some()
3558                    || detfd.path().is_some_and(|path| path.starts_with("/proc"))
3559            })
3560            .map_err(|_| Errno::EBADF)?;
3561        Err(if on_procfs {
3562            Errno::EBADF
3563        } else {
3564            Errno::EXDEV
3565        })
3566    }
3567
3568    /// Whether the descriptor link the guest named at `link` (its `dirfd` and
3569    /// path), whose target text names a placeholder, reaches one Detcore
3570    /// made: by the kernel identity of what it reaches. Where the tool runs in
3571    /// each guest process and so may not have made it (another process's
3572    /// placeholder), its name is taken as it stands.
3573    pub(crate) async fn placeholder_link_confirmed<G: Guest<Self>>(
3574        &self,
3575        guest: &mut G,
3576        link: (RawFd, Option<PathPtr<'_>>),
3577    ) -> bool {
3578        if self.cfg.backend.tool_shares_guest_descriptor_table {
3579            return true;
3580        }
3581        let (dirfd, Some(path)) = link else {
3582            return false;
3583        };
3584        match Self::inject_statat(guest, dirfd, path, [Some(path), None], AtFlags::empty()).await {
3585            Ok(stat) => placeholder_identity(stat.st_dev, stat.st_ino).is_some(),
3586            Err(_) => false,
3587        }
3588    }
3589
3590    /// Linux's answer to a mapping of a procfs file Detcore renders, which the
3591    /// placeholder standing in for it (a memfd) would accept. In `do_mmap`'s
3592    /// order: the arguments and the address; `MAP_FIXED_NOREPLACE` over a
3593    /// mapping is `EEXIST`, found by asking the kernel for the same range
3594    /// with nothing behind it; then the file's checks
3595    /// (`rendered_mmap_file_checks`). A `seq_file`'s mapping reaches
3596    /// `mmap_region`, which with `MAP_FIXED` unmaps whatever the range held
3597    /// before `proc_reg_mmap` fails, so the range is unmapped here too.
3598    ///
3599    /// The probe and the unmap are injected while the thread holds its turn,
3600    /// so with threads sequentialized (the default) no sibling runs between
3601    /// them. Under `--no-sequentialize-threads` a sibling could map into the
3602    /// probed range between the probe and its unmap and lose its mapping,
3603    /// where Linux makes the whole call under the mm's lock; there neither is
3604    /// made, so an overlap is not found and the range is not unmapped.
3605    pub(crate) async fn answer_rendered_procfs_mapping<G: Guest<Self>>(
3606        &self,
3607        guest: &mut G,
3608        call: &Syscall,
3609    ) -> Option<Result<i64, Error>> {
3610        let Syscall::Mmap(c) = call else {
3611            return None;
3612        };
3613        if c.flags().contains(MapFlags::MAP_ANONYMOUS) {
3614            return None;
3615        }
3616        let (path, status_flags) = guest
3617            .thread_state()
3618            .with_detfd(c.fd(), |detfd| {
3619                (detfd.status_flags() & libc::O_PATH == 0)
3620                    .then(|| detfd.procfs_rendered_path())
3621                    .flatten()
3622                    .map(|path| (path, detfd.status_flags()))
3623            })
3624            .ok()
3625            .flatten()?;
3626        // A per-process file has no `mmap` method, as a sysctl file has none.
3627        let seq_file = crate::procfs::rendered_path_is_seq_file(path)
3628            && !crate::procfs::rendered_path_is_per_process(path);
3629        let access_mode = status_flags & libc::O_ACCMODE;
3630        // Exactly: access mode 3 can neither read nor write.
3631        let mode = (
3632            access_mode == libc::O_RDONLY || access_mode == libc::O_RDWR,
3633            access_mode == libc::O_WRONLY || access_mode == libc::O_RDWR,
3634        );
3635        let noexec = match GuestProcfs::statfs() {
3636            Ok((_, flags)) => flags & libc::ST_NOEXEC != 0,
3637            Err(errno) => return Some(Err(errno.into())),
3638        };
3639        let addr = c.addr().map_or(0, |addr| addr.as_raw());
3640        let flags = c.flags();
3641        let len = match rendered_mmap_address_checks(addr, c.len(), c.offset(), flags) {
3642            Ok(len) => len,
3643            Err(errno) => return Some(Err(errno.into())),
3644        };
3645        let sequentialized = guest.config().sequentialize_threads;
3646        if sequentialized && flags.contains(MapFlags::MAP_FIXED_NOREPLACE) {
3647            let probe = guest
3648                .inject_with_retry(Syscall::Mmap(
3649                    syscalls::Mmap::new()
3650                        .with_addr(c.addr())
3651                        .with_len(len)
3652                        .with_prot(ProtFlags::PROT_NONE)
3653                        .with_flags(
3654                            MapFlags::MAP_PRIVATE
3655                                | MapFlags::MAP_ANONYMOUS
3656                                | MapFlags::MAP_FIXED_NOREPLACE,
3657                        )
3658                        .with_fd(-1)
3659                        .with_offset(0),
3660                ))
3661                .await;
3662            match probe {
3663                // The backend refused the range itself: EEXIST for an overlap,
3664                // or EPERM where DBT answers an overlap so for every mapping
3665                // (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4429; an anonymous
3666                // PROT_NONE probe meets no other EPERM, the address having
3667                // passed `security_mmap_addr` above). That is its answer for
3668                // this range too.
3669                Err(errno @ (Errno::EEXIST | Errno::EPERM)) => return Some(Err(errno.into())),
3670                // A probe refused for another reason says nothing of an
3671                // overlap: its limits (`map_count` before the overlap check,
3672                // `RLIMIT_AS` after the file's) are not this mapping's order.
3673                // Nothing is unmapped for MAP_FIXED_NOREPLACE, so going on to
3674                // the file's checks cannot remove a mapping.
3675                Err(_) => {}
3676                // Unmap the probe where it was placed. A backend that takes
3677                // MAP_FIXED_NOREPLACE as a hint (KVM's executor) places it
3678                // elsewhere when the range is occupied: that is the overlap.
3679                // If that unmap fails, the PROT_NONE probe stays and the guest
3680                // sees the unmap's error, which Linux never gives here; the
3681                // unmap of a range the kernel just mapped does not fail in
3682                // practice.
3683                Ok(placed) => {
3684                    let placed_addr = Addr::<libc::c_void>::from_raw(placed as usize);
3685                    if let Err(errno) = guest
3686                        .inject_with_retry(Syscall::Munmap(
3687                            syscalls::Munmap::new().with_addr(placed_addr).with_len(len),
3688                        ))
3689                        .await
3690                    {
3691                        return Some(Err(errno.into()));
3692                    }
3693                    if placed as usize != addr {
3694                        return Some(Err(Errno::EEXIST.into()));
3695                    }
3696                }
3697            }
3698        }
3699        let errno =
3700            rendered_mmap_file_checks(len, c.offset(), c.prot(), flags, noexec, seq_file, mode);
3701        // MAP_FIXED_NOREPLACE implies MAP_FIXED in Linux, but its range is
3702        // vacant here, so there is nothing to unmap.
3703        let unmaps = sequentialized
3704            && errno == Errno::EIO
3705            && flags.contains(MapFlags::MAP_FIXED)
3706            && !flags.contains(MapFlags::MAP_FIXED_NOREPLACE);
3707        if unmaps
3708            && let Err(unmapped) = guest
3709                .inject_with_retry(Syscall::Munmap(
3710                    syscalls::Munmap::new().with_addr(c.addr()).with_len(len),
3711                ))
3712                .await
3713        {
3714            return Some(Err(unmapped.into()));
3715        }
3716        if unmaps {
3717            // As `handle_munmap` does, so Detcore's record of shared
3718            // mappings (futex keys, the heap) forgets the range.
3719            guest.thread_state().unmap_memory(addr, len);
3720        }
3721        Some(Err(errno.into()))
3722    }
3723
3724    /// Linux's answer to a call that writes through the descriptor it names
3725    /// (`write`, `writev`, `pwritev2` at the file position, `ftruncate`,
3726    /// `fallocate`, and `sendfile` or `splice` into it) when that descriptor
3727    /// is a placeholder opened for writing, or with access mode 3, which only
3728    /// a per-process file under `CAP_DAC_OVERRIDE` is (`rendered_open_flags`).
3729    /// Detcore answers every such call, so none reaches the memfd. Linux opens such a
3730    /// file without `FMODE_CAN_WRITE` (it has no write method), so a write is
3731    /// `EINVAL` after the access mode is checked; `proc_setattr` accepts any
3732    /// size and keeps none; and it has no `fallocate` method. A positioned
3733    /// write is the `seq_file`'s `ESPIPE` (`answer_rendered_procfs_operation`).
3734    /// Measured on Linux 7.1 against `/proc/self/cgroup` as its owner with
3735    /// `CAP_DAC_OVERRIDE`. `sendfile` and `splice` into it answer in Linux's
3736    /// order, which looks at the source descriptor and the offsets first
3737    /// ([`Self::answer_rendered_procfs_transfer`]).
3738    async fn answer_rendered_procfs_write<G: Guest<Self>>(
3739        &self,
3740        guest: &mut G,
3741        sysno: Sysno,
3742        args: &reverie::syscalls::SyscallArgs,
3743    ) -> Option<Result<i64, Errno>> {
3744        let fd = match written_descriptor_position(sysno)? {
3745            // Only `pwritev2` at the file position writes as `writev` does.
3746            _ if sysno == Sysno::pwritev2 && args.arg3 as i64 != -1 => return None,
3747            0 => args.arg0,
3748            _ => args.arg2,
3749        } as RawFd;
3750        let status = guest
3751            .thread_state()
3752            .with_detfd(fd, |detfd| {
3753                (detfd.procfs_rendered_path().is_some() && detfd.status_flags() & libc::O_PATH == 0)
3754                    .then(|| detfd.status_flags())
3755            })
3756            .ok()
3757            .flatten();
3758        let writable = match status.map(|status| status & libc::O_ACCMODE) {
3759            None | Some(libc::O_RDONLY) => return None,
3760            Some(mode) => mode == libc::O_WRONLY || mode == libc::O_RDWR,
3761        };
3762        if matches!(sysno, Sysno::sendfile | Sysno::splice) {
3763            // `O_APPEND`, which `F_SETFL` changes, is the kernel's answer for
3764            // the description, not Detcore's copy, which another process
3765            // sharing it (a native fork's child under DBT) may have changed.
3766            // The access mode is fixed at the open.
3767            let kernel = guest
3768                .inject(
3769                    syscalls::Fcntl::new()
3770                        .with_fd(fd)
3771                        .with_cmd(syscalls::FcntlCmd::F_GETFL),
3772                )
3773                .await
3774                .ok()
3775                .map(|status| status as i32);
3776            let appends = kernel
3777                .or(status)
3778                .is_some_and(|status| status & libc::O_APPEND != 0);
3779            return Some(
3780                self.answer_rendered_procfs_transfer(guest, sysno, args, writable, appends)
3781                    .await,
3782            );
3783        }
3784        // Access mode 3 (O_ACCMODE) asks for both permissions at the open and
3785        // gives the description neither: it can neither read nor write
3786        // (`FMODE_READ` and `FMODE_WRITE` unset), so a write is EBADF, and
3787        // `ftruncate` the EINVAL of a description not open for writing.
3788        if !writable {
3789            return Some(match sysno {
3790                Sysno::ftruncate => Err(Errno::EINVAL),
3791                _ => Err(Errno::EBADF),
3792            });
3793        }
3794        Some(match sysno {
3795            Sysno::ftruncate if (args.arg1 as i64) < 0 => Err(Errno::EINVAL),
3796            Sysno::ftruncate => Ok(0),
3797            Sysno::fallocate if (args.arg2 as i64) < 0 || (args.arg3 as i64) <= 0 => {
3798                Err(Errno::EINVAL)
3799            }
3800            Sysno::fallocate => Err(Errno::EOPNOTSUPP),
3801            _ => Err(Errno::EINVAL),
3802        })
3803    }
3804
3805    /// Linux's answer to `sendfile` or `splice` into a placeholder opened
3806    /// for writing (`writable`) or with access mode 3, in the order Linux
3807    /// checks (measured on Linux 7.1 as root against `/proc/self/cgroup`
3808    /// opened `O_RDWR`). The source descriptor's state is the kernel's
3809    /// answer (`F_GETFL`, `fstat`), not Detcore's record of it, which a
3810    /// received descriptor need not have.
3811    ///
3812    /// `sendfile(out, in, offset, count)`: an unreadable `offset` is EFAULT;
3813    /// a source that is not open, is `O_PATH`, or cannot read is EBADF; an
3814    /// offset for a pipe or socket source is ESPIPE; a negative offset is
3815    /// EINVAL; then a destination that cannot write is EBADF and an
3816    /// `O_APPEND` one EINVAL; a count of 0 transfers nothing (0); anything
3817    /// else is the EINVAL of a file with no `splice_write`.
3818    ///
3819    /// `splice(in, off_in, out, off_out, len, flags)`: a length of 0 is 0
3820    /// before anything is looked at; unknown flags are EINVAL; a source that
3821    /// is not open or is `O_PATH` is EBADF; an `off_in` for a pipe source is
3822    /// ESPIPE; an unreadable `off_in` or `off_out` is EFAULT; a source that
3823    /// cannot read, or a destination that cannot write, is EBADF; anything
3824    /// else is EINVAL (no `splice_write`, or no pipe on either side).
3825    async fn answer_rendered_procfs_transfer<G: Guest<Self>>(
3826        &self,
3827        guest: &mut G,
3828        sysno: Sysno,
3829        args: &reverie::syscalls::SyscallArgs,
3830        writable: bool,
3831        appends: bool,
3832    ) -> Result<i64, Errno> {
3833        const SPLICE_F_ALL: usize = 0xf;
3834        fn offset_at<M: MemoryAccess>(memory: &M, address: usize) -> Result<i64, Errno> {
3835            let address = reverie::syscalls::Addr::<i64>::from_raw(address).ok_or(Errno::EFAULT)?;
3836            memory.read_value(address).map_err(|_| Errno::EFAULT)
3837        }
3838        let (source, length) = match sysno {
3839            Sysno::sendfile => (args.arg1 as RawFd, args.arg3),
3840            _ => (args.arg0 as RawFd, args.arg4),
3841        };
3842        if sysno == Sysno::splice {
3843            if length == 0 {
3844                return Ok(0);
3845            }
3846            if args.arg5 & !SPLICE_F_ALL != 0 {
3847                return Err(Errno::EINVAL);
3848            }
3849        }
3850        let offset = if sysno == Sysno::sendfile && args.arg2 != 0 {
3851            Some(offset_at(&guest.memory(), args.arg2)?)
3852        } else {
3853            None
3854        };
3855        let source_status = guest
3856            .inject(
3857                syscalls::Fcntl::new()
3858                    .with_fd(source)
3859                    .with_cmd(syscalls::FcntlCmd::F_GETFL),
3860            )
3861            .await
3862            .map_err(|_| Errno::EBADF)? as i32;
3863        if source_status & libc::O_PATH != 0 {
3864            return Err(Errno::EBADF);
3865        }
3866        let source_reads = matches!(
3867            source_status & libc::O_ACCMODE,
3868            libc::O_RDONLY | libc::O_RDWR
3869        );
3870        // The source's file type, which decides an offset's ESPIPE: a pipe
3871        // or FIFO has no position for either call, and a socket none for
3872        // `sendfile` (no `FMODE_PREAD`).
3873        let source_type =
3874            |stat: Result<libc::stat, Errno>| stat.map_or(0, |stat| stat.st_mode & libc::S_IFMT);
3875        if sysno == Sysno::sendfile {
3876            if !source_reads {
3877                return Err(Errno::EBADF);
3878            }
3879            if let Some(offset) = offset {
3880                let kind = source_type(self.inject_fstat(guest, source).await);
3881                if matches!(kind, libc::S_IFIFO | libc::S_IFSOCK) {
3882                    return Err(Errno::ESPIPE);
3883                }
3884                if offset < 0 {
3885                    return Err(Errno::EINVAL);
3886                }
3887            }
3888            if !writable {
3889                return Err(Errno::EBADF);
3890            }
3891            if appends {
3892                return Err(Errno::EINVAL);
3893            }
3894            return if length == 0 {
3895                Ok(0)
3896            } else {
3897                Err(Errno::EINVAL)
3898            };
3899        }
3900        // `splice`.
3901        let (off_in, off_out) = (args.arg1, args.arg3);
3902        if off_in != 0 && source_type(self.inject_fstat(guest, source).await) == libc::S_IFIFO {
3903            return Err(Errno::ESPIPE);
3904        }
3905        if off_in != 0 {
3906            offset_at(&guest.memory(), off_in)?;
3907        }
3908        if off_out != 0 {
3909            offset_at(&guest.memory(), off_out)?;
3910        }
3911        if !source_reads || !writable {
3912            return Err(Errno::EBADF);
3913        }
3914        Err(Errno::EINVAL)
3915    }
3916
3917    /// Record the auxiliary vector the calling process's new image finds on
3918    /// its initial stack, before its first instruction, for its
3919    /// `/proc/<pid>/auxv` (`ProcessAuxvTable`): the pairs past `argc`, the
3920    /// argument and environment pointers, read through guest memory at the
3921    /// stack pointer the image starts with, as the backend reports it
3922    /// (`Guest::initial_stack_pointer`; `procfs::initial_stack_auxv`, which
3923    /// refuses an implausible vector). They are what the guest's own
3924    /// `getauxval` reads: a backend that gives the guest the canonical values
3925    /// of the CPU and kernel entries (ptrace's `canonicalize_new_image`, KVM's
3926    /// loader) has written them there before this callback, and Detcore takes
3927    /// them as they are, never from the backend's `Guest::auxv`, which is
3928    /// sorted and, on ptrace, the kernel's own file.
3929    ///
3930    /// A backend that reports no initial stack pointer (DBT, SaBRe, LiteInst),
3931    /// or a vector that fails the check, records nothing and drops the
3932    /// process's stale vector: the image's file is left to the kernel beneath
3933    /// (the host kernel's file on ptrace and DBT, the provider's EACCES
3934    /// backstop on KVM), and the run warns of each reason once, by name
3935    /// (`tool_global::auxv_from_kernel`).
3936    ///
3937    /// The same stack gives the image's argument and environment areas
3938    /// (`procfs::initial_stack_cmdline_area`), which the process's rendered
3939    /// `/proc/<pid>/cmdline` reads from its memory
3940    /// ([`Self::read_rendered_cmdline`]); without them, the same backends
3941    /// leave that file to the kernel beneath too. Both go to the global tool
3942    /// in the one request this callback already makes.
3943    // AUTONOMOUS-BOT-IMPLEMENTED
3944    // TODO-HUMAN-REVIEW(#4213): Review the auxiliary vector Detcore takes from
3945    // each new image's initial stack, which its rendered /proc/<pid>/auxv
3946    // serves in place of the kernel's saved_auxv.
3947    pub(crate) async fn record_process_auxv<G: Guest<Self>>(&self, guest: &mut G) {
3948        use crate::tool_global::AuxvFromKernel;
3949        // Only a stack pointer the backend observed at the image's first
3950        // instruction: post-exec registers need not retain it on every
3951        // backend. DBT's first-entry admission still does not supply this
3952        // image-owned hook. Without one, or with an implausible vector there,
3953        // the file is left to the kernel beneath:
3954        // the host kernel's file on ptrace and DBT, the provider's refusal on
3955        // KVM.
3956        let (found, cmdline) = match guest.initial_stack_pointer() {
3957            Some(stack) => {
3958                let memory = guest.memory();
3959                let read_word = |at: u64| {
3960                    let address =
3961                        reverie::syscalls::Addr::<u64>::from_raw(usize::try_from(at).ok()?)?;
3962                    memory.read_value(address).ok()
3963                };
3964                (
3965                    crate::procfs::initial_stack_auxv(stack, read_word)
3966                        .ok_or(AuxvFromKernel::Implausible),
3967                    crate::procfs::initial_stack_cmdline_area(stack, read_word, |at, buffer| {
3968                        read_prefix(&memory, at, buffer)
3969                    }),
3970                )
3971            }
3972            None => (Err(AuxvFromKernel::NoInitialStackPointer), None),
3973        };
3974        let bytes = match found {
3975            Ok(bytes) => bytes,
3976            Err(why) => {
3977                // The process's vector from before the exec (its own earlier
3978                // image's, or its creator's) is stale: the global tool drops
3979                // it (`ProcessAuxvTable::clear`). The run says once, by name,
3980                // what the guest sees instead; the global tool's per-run flag
3981                // decides the first, so a second run in one process warns too.
3982                if crate::tool_global::auxv_from_kernel(guest, why, cmdline).await {
3983                    match why {
3984                        AuxvFromKernel::NoInitialStackPointer => warn!(
3985                            "auxv: backend gives no initial stack pointer; guest auxv is the \
3986                             kernel beneath's"
3987                        ),
3988                        AuxvFromKernel::Implausible => warn!(
3989                            "auxv: the initial stack did not hold a plausible auxiliary vector; \
3990                             guest auxv is the kernel beneath's"
3991                        ),
3992                    }
3993                }
3994                return;
3995            }
3996        };
3997        crate::tool_global::record_process_auxv(guest, bytes, cmdline).await;
3998    }
3999
4000    /// Adopt, before `call` runs, each descriptor it names that Detcore has
4001    /// no record of and that is a placeholder this tool process made: a
4002    /// rendered procfs descriptor that reached this table by a route Detcore
4003    /// does not model, `SCM_RIGHTS` (where the tool does not discover
4004    /// descriptors as they are used: ptrace and KVM; an in-guest backend
4005    /// discovers it first, `discover_fd_from_current_process`). Then every
4006    /// call through it (reads, the cursor, a mapping, the write family, the
4007    /// task's reap) is answered as for a descriptor Detcore opened, and none
4008    /// reaches the memfd.
4009    ///
4010    /// The descriptor shares the sender's `struct file`, so it is an alias of
4011    /// the sender's open file description while Detcore still models that
4012    /// description (`PLACEHOLDER_OPENS`): one cursor, one snapshot, one set
4013    /// of status flags, exactly as Linux shares them. Once no modeled
4014    /// descriptor refers to it (the sender closed its own), the description
4015    /// is rebuilt from what the kernel keeps of it, as an in-guest backend
4016    /// rebuilds a placeholder it rediscovers (`adopt_placeholder`): the file
4017    /// and task its identity names, the status flags (`F_GETFL`), the cursor
4018    /// Detcore keeps at the kernel position (`sync_rendered_procfs_position`),
4019    /// and the snapshot its name carries; a file whose snapshot is taken at
4020    /// its first read takes it then (`initialize_procfs_snapshot`).
4021    ///
4022    /// Only once this tool process has made a placeholder, and only for a
4023    /// descriptor Detcore has no record of: one injected `fstat`, for each
4024    /// such descriptor a listed call names, every time (no cache: a
4025    /// descriptor number's identity can only be learned by asking).
4026    // TODO-HUMAN-REVIEW(#4213): Review the adoption of rendered descriptors
4027    // received over SCM_RIGHTS: the alias of a modeled description, the
4028    // rebuild of one no longer modeled, and the refusal without
4029    // sequentialized threads.
4030    pub(crate) async fn adopt_received_placeholders<G: Guest<Self>>(
4031        &self,
4032        guest: &mut G,
4033        call: &Syscall,
4034    ) -> Result<(), Error> {
4035        let (sysno, args) = call.into_parts();
4036        // The number first: it takes no lock, and most calls name no
4037        // descriptor (`descriptor_arguments` would name none).
4038        if !names_a_descriptor(sysno) || !placeholders_exist() || !self.renders_procfs(guest) {
4039            return Ok(());
4040        }
4041        for fd in descriptor_arguments(&guest.memory(), sysno, &args) {
4042            if fd < 0 || guest.thread_state().with_detfd(fd, |_| ()).is_ok() {
4043                continue;
4044            }
4045            info!(
4046                "Descriptor {fd}, which Detcore has no record of, is checked for a rendered \
4047                 procfs placeholder."
4048            );
4049            let Ok(stat) = self.inject_fstat(guest, fd).await else {
4050                continue;
4051            };
4052            // A placeholder is an empty regular file with no link, as the
4053            // in-guest discovery requires too (`discover_fd_from_current_process`).
4054            // Its identity is its memfd's (dev, ino) on the kernel's internal
4055            // shmem mount, which numbers inodes from the superblock's 64-bit
4056            // `next_ino` in per-CPU batches, without the 32-bit wrap of a
4057            // user-visible tmpfs (`shmem_reserve_inode` for an
4058            // `SB_KERNMOUNT`, Linux 5.9 and later): ASSUMED here, a number
4059            // that named a placeholder this run is not given to another
4060            // memfd, so the registry, which is never pruned, names no other
4061            // file.
4062            let may_be_placeholder = stat.st_mode & libc::S_IFMT == libc::S_IFREG
4063                && stat.st_nlink == 0
4064                && stat.st_size == 0;
4065            if !may_be_placeholder {
4066                continue;
4067            }
4068            let identity = (stat.st_dev, stat.st_ino);
4069            let Some(path) = placeholder_identity(identity.0, identity.1) else {
4070                continue;
4071            };
4072            // Without sequentialized threads (a declared relaxation) another
4073            // thread may close the descriptor, or `dup2` another file over
4074            // it, between the `fstat` above and the adoption, which would
4075            // then install a stale description. Refused by name instead,
4076            // through the unsupported-operation policy: a fail-closed run
4077            // stops with the reason, and --allow-unsupported-syscalls gives
4078            // the call EOPNOTSUPP. Never a silent memfd effect.
4079            if !guest.config().sequentialize_threads {
4080                let reason = format!(
4081                    "descriptor {fd}, a rendered procfs file Detcore has no record of \
4082                     (received over SCM_RIGHTS), cannot be adopted without sequentialized \
4083                     threads (--no-sequentialize-threads)"
4084                );
4085                // Named where the run's own output shows it: a backend whose
4086                // tool runs in Hermit's process prints no stored reason.
4087                error!("Detcore refuses {sysno}: {reason}");
4088                return crate::syscalls::helpers::refuse_unserviceable_operation_with(
4089                    guest,
4090                    self.cfg.panic_on_unsupported_syscalls,
4091                    sysno,
4092                    Errno::EOPNOTSUPP,
4093                    Some(&reason),
4094                )
4095                .await
4096                .map(|_| ());
4097            }
4098            self.adopt_received_placeholder(guest, fd, path, identity)
4099                .await?;
4100        }
4101        Ok(())
4102    }
4103
4104    /// Make descriptor `fd`, which Detcore has no record of, the placeholder
4105    /// `identity` for the rendered file `path` it is
4106    /// ([`Self::adopt_received_placeholders`]).
4107    async fn adopt_received_placeholder<G: Guest<Self>>(
4108        &self,
4109        guest: &mut G,
4110        fd: RawFd,
4111        path: &'static Path,
4112        identity: (u64, u64),
4113    ) -> Result<(), Error> {
4114        let descriptor_flags = guest
4115            .inject(
4116                syscalls::Fcntl::new()
4117                    .with_fd(fd)
4118                    .with_cmd(syscalls::FcntlCmd::F_GETFD),
4119            )
4120            .await?;
4121        let cloexec = descriptor_flags & i64::from(libc::FD_CLOEXEC) != 0;
4122        let registered = placeholder_open(identity);
4123        if let Some(alias) = registered
4124            .as_ref()
4125            .and_then(|(description, _)| description.descriptor(fd, cloexec))
4126        {
4127            guest.thread_state().install_alias(alias);
4128            return Ok(());
4129        }
4130        let status = guest
4131            .inject(
4132                syscalls::Fcntl::new()
4133                    .with_fd(fd)
4134                    .with_cmd(syscalls::FcntlCmd::F_GETFL),
4135            )
4136            .await? as i32;
4137        // An `O_PATH` descriptor has no position (EBADF).
4138        let offset = guest
4139            .inject(
4140                syscalls::Lseek::new()
4141                    .with_fd(fd)
4142                    .with_offset(0)
4143                    .with_whence(syscalls::Whence::SEEK_CUR),
4144            )
4145            .await
4146            .map_or(0, |offset| offset as usize);
4147        // OBLIGATION for every rendered file added after `cgroup`: this
4148        // rebuilt description has the snapshot the placeholder's name
4149        // carries, and no other. A file whose snapshot its first read takes
4150        // takes it again at its next read here; at position 0 that is
4151        // Linux's `show` running again, exact, but at a position above 0
4152        // Linux reads on from the buffer the first read filled in another
4153        // process. That is exact only while every snapshot of the file is
4154        // the same, as `cgroup`'s template is. A per-process file whose bytes
4155        // change (`status`, `stat`) must carry its first read's snapshot on
4156        // its placeholder (as `/proc/uptime` carries its first read's uptime,
4157        // `first_read_uptime`) before it is rendered, or its rebuilt
4158        // descriptor reads other bytes than the sender's buffer held.
4159        // DECLARED for a process's `stat` (package K slice D4c-1), which
4160        // carries none: its record is rendered again wherever Linux's
4161        // seq_file traverses, so no one snapshot can be carried, and neither
4162        // the placeholder's name, fixed when it is made, nor its modification
4163        // time, 64 bits, can hold one. A rebuilt `stat` renders its record
4164        // again at its next read, at the shared cursor, where Linux continues
4165        // the buffered one: where one tool process serves every guest
4166        // (ptrace, KVM), a descriptor whose every modeled alias closed before
4167        // a receiver's first call naming it
4168        // (tests/misc/proc_stat_lifecycle.rs
4169        // `a_queued_stat_descriptor_whose_sender_closed_it_renders_again_under_detcore`);
4170        // where the tool runs in each guest process, a descriptor another
4171        // process receives when it holds no alias of the open file Detcore
4172        // models, and the image after `exec`
4173        // (`Detcore::adopt_rendered_procfs_position`).
4174        let snapshot = registered.map(|(_, snapshot)| snapshot).unwrap_or_default();
4175        let metadata = self.rendered_metadata(guest, path).await?;
4176        let mut flags = OFlag::from_bits_truncate(status);
4177        flags.set(OFlag::O_CLOEXEC, cloexec);
4178        guest
4179            .thread_state()
4180            .add_fd(fd, flags, FdType::Regular, Some(metadata))?;
4181        let procfs = ProcfsFile::from_rendered_path(path)
4182            .expect("a placeholder's path is a rendered procfs file")
4183            .into_rendered();
4184        let procfs = if snapshot.is_empty() {
4185            procfs
4186        } else {
4187            procfs.with_rendered_snapshot(snapshot.clone())
4188        };
4189        let handle = guest.thread_state().with_detfd(fd, |detfd| {
4190            detfd.set_path(path);
4191            detfd.set_procfs(procfs.clone());
4192            detfd.set_procfs_offset(offset);
4193            detfd.open_file_handle()
4194        })?;
4195        register_placeholder_open(identity, handle, snapshot);
4196        Ok(())
4197    }
4198
4199    /// Linux's answer to `splice` from a rendered `/proc/<pid>/cmdline`
4200    /// (`fd_in`, `args.arg0`), which must not read the empty placeholder
4201    /// standing in for it, in the order Linux 6.17 checks (fs/splice.c): a
4202    /// length of 0 is 0 and unknown flags are EINVAL (`SYSCALL_DEFINE6(splice)`,
4203    /// splice.c:1622-1626); a source or destination that is not open or is
4204    /// `O_PATH` is EBADF (splice.c:1628-1634, `fdget` skips `O_PATH`); a
4205    /// destination pipe that splice can use (not a notification pipe:
4206    /// [`Self::splice_pipe`]) with an `off_out` is ESPIPE (`__do_splice`,
4207    /// splice.c:1416-1418); an unreadable `off_out` or `off_in` is EFAULT
4208    /// (splice.c:1422-1431); a source that cannot read or a destination that
4209    /// cannot write is EBADF (`do_splice`, splice.c:1310-1312); and then,
4210    /// because `proc_pid_cmdline_ops` has no `splice_read`, EINVAL: at
4211    /// splice.c:1383 for a destination that is not a pipe, and from
4212    /// `do_splice_read` through `splice_file_to_pipe` (splice.c:1376) for a
4213    /// pipe. The destination's state is the kernel's answer (`F_GETFL`,
4214    /// `fstat`, the watch-queue probe), as for a transfer into a
4215    /// placeholder.
4216    ///
4217    /// Not modelled, declared: before that last EINVAL a destination pipe
4218    /// with no reader gives SIGPIPE and EPIPE, and a full one EAGAIN when
4219    /// nonblocking or a wait for space (`wait_for_space`, splice.c:1261). By
4220    /// default Detcore refuses `splice` with EINVAL for every other file; this
4221    /// answer stands with and without `--allow-unsupported-syscalls`. `None`
4222    /// for any other source.
4223    async fn answer_cmdline_splice<G: Guest<Self>>(
4224        &self,
4225        guest: &mut G,
4226        args: &reverie::syscalls::SyscallArgs,
4227    ) -> Option<Result<i64, Error>> {
4228        let status = guest
4229            .thread_state()
4230            .with_detfd(args.arg0 as RawFd, |detfd| {
4231                detfd
4232                    .procfs_reads_memory_at_every_read()
4233                    .then(|| detfd.status_flags())
4234            })
4235            .ok()
4236            .flatten()?;
4237        Some(self.cmdline_splice_answer(guest, args, status).await)
4238    }
4239
4240    /// [`Self::answer_cmdline_splice`]'s answer for a source whose status
4241    /// flags are `status`. The destination's state comes from the kernel by
4242    /// injected probes, each retried while Reverie reports it did not run
4243    /// (ERESTARTSYS) or it was interrupted (EINTR): EBADF only where the
4244    /// kernel says the descriptor is not open, and any other probe failure
4245    /// the named refusal of the failure policy, never an errno Detcore made
4246    /// up.
4247    async fn cmdline_splice_answer<G: Guest<Self>>(
4248        &self,
4249        guest: &mut G,
4250        args: &reverie::syscalls::SyscallArgs,
4251        status: i32,
4252    ) -> Result<i64, Error> {
4253        const SPLICE_F_ALL: usize = 0xf;
4254        fn readable_offset<M: MemoryAccess>(memory: &M, address: usize) -> Result<(), Errno> {
4255            let address = reverie::syscalls::Addr::<i64>::from_raw(address).ok_or(Errno::EFAULT)?;
4256            memory
4257                .read_value(address)
4258                .map(|_| ())
4259                .map_err(|_| Errno::EFAULT)
4260        }
4261        let (off_in, out, off_out) = (args.arg1, args.arg2 as RawFd, args.arg3);
4262        if args.arg4 == 0 {
4263            return Ok(0);
4264        }
4265        if args.arg5 & !SPLICE_F_ALL != 0 {
4266            return Err(Errno::EINVAL.into());
4267        }
4268        if status & libc::O_PATH != 0 {
4269            return Err(Errno::EBADF.into());
4270        }
4271        let out_status = loop {
4272            match guest
4273                .inject(
4274                    syscalls::Fcntl::new()
4275                        .with_fd(out)
4276                        .with_cmd(syscalls::FcntlCmd::F_GETFL),
4277                )
4278                .await
4279            {
4280                Err(Errno::ERESTARTSYS | Errno::EINTR) => continue,
4281                Ok(flags) => break flags as i32,
4282                Err(Errno::EBADF) => return Err(Errno::EBADF.into()),
4283                Err(errno) => {
4284                    return Err(Self::scratch_failure(
4285                        guest,
4286                        Sysno::splice,
4287                        format!(
4288                            "the status flags of splice's destination {out} could not be read: \
4289                             {errno}"
4290                        ),
4291                    )
4292                    .await);
4293                }
4294            }
4295        };
4296        if out_status & libc::O_PATH != 0 {
4297            return Err(Errno::EBADF.into());
4298        }
4299        if off_out != 0 && self.splice_pipe(guest, out).await? {
4300            return Err(Errno::ESPIPE.into());
4301        }
4302        if off_out != 0 {
4303            readable_offset(&guest.memory(), off_out)?;
4304        }
4305        if off_in != 0 {
4306            readable_offset(&guest.memory(), off_in)?;
4307        }
4308        let reads = matches!(status & libc::O_ACCMODE, libc::O_RDONLY | libc::O_RDWR);
4309        let writes = matches!(out_status & libc::O_ACCMODE, libc::O_WRONLY | libc::O_RDWR);
4310        if !reads || !writes {
4311            return Err(Errno::EBADF.into());
4312        }
4313        Err(Errno::EINVAL.into())
4314    }
4315
4316    /// Whether the open descriptor `fd` is a pipe that splice can use,
4317    /// Linux's `get_pipe_info(file, true)` (fs/pipe.c:1413-1424): a pipe or
4318    /// FIFO (the kernel's `fstat`), but not a notification pipe
4319    /// (`pipe2(O_NOTIFICATION_PIPE)`, which has a watch queue). The watch
4320    /// queue is told by `ioctl(IOC_WATCH_QUEUE_SET_SIZE, 0)`, which changes
4321    /// nothing whatever it answers (`watch_queue_set_size`,
4322    /// kernel/watch_queue.c:237-252): ENODEV for a pipe without one, EBUSY or
4323    /// EINVAL for one with one, and ENOTTY from a kernel without watch
4324    /// queues, where no pipe has one. Each probe is retried as in
4325    /// [`Self::cmdline_splice_answer`]; any other failure, which leaves the
4326    /// pipe unclassified, is the named refusal of the failure policy.
4327    async fn splice_pipe<G: Guest<Self>>(&self, guest: &mut G, fd: RawFd) -> Result<bool, Error> {
4328        const IOC_WATCH_QUEUE_SET_SIZE: usize = 0x5760; // _IO('W', 0x60)
4329        let stat = loop {
4330            match self.inject_fstat(guest, fd).await {
4331                Err(Errno::ERESTARTSYS | Errno::EINTR) => continue,
4332                stat => break stat,
4333            }
4334        };
4335        let stat = match stat {
4336            Ok(stat) => stat,
4337            Err(errno) => {
4338                return Err(Self::scratch_failure(
4339                    guest,
4340                    Sysno::splice,
4341                    format!(
4342                        "the file type of splice's destination {fd} could not be read: {errno}"
4343                    ),
4344                )
4345                .await);
4346            }
4347        };
4348        if stat.st_mode & libc::S_IFMT != libc::S_IFIFO {
4349            return Ok(false);
4350        }
4351        let probe = loop {
4352            let call = Syscall::from_raw(
4353                Sysno::ioctl,
4354                reverie::syscalls::SyscallArgs::new(
4355                    fd as usize,
4356                    IOC_WATCH_QUEUE_SET_SIZE,
4357                    0,
4358                    0,
4359                    0,
4360                    0,
4361                ),
4362            );
4363            match guest.inject(call).await {
4364                Err(Errno::ERESTARTSYS | Errno::EINTR) => continue,
4365                probe => break probe,
4366            }
4367        };
4368        match splice_pipe_from_watch_queue_probe(probe) {
4369            Some(usable) => Ok(usable),
4370            None => Err(Self::scratch_failure(
4371                guest,
4372                Sysno::splice,
4373                format!(
4374                    "splice's destination pipe {fd} could not be classified as a notification \
4375                     pipe or not: the watch-queue probe answered {probe:?}"
4376                ),
4377            )
4378            .await),
4379        }
4380    }
4381
4382    /// Answer `call` if it must not reach the placeholder behind a rendered
4383    /// procfs descriptor: what a procfs file refuses, in the order Linux
4384    /// checks. A read-only placeholder's description already makes the kernel
4385    /// refuse writes and size changes, and an `O_PATH` one every call but
4386    /// `fstat`, which Detcore answers. A per-process file opened for writing
4387    /// (`rendered_open_flags`) has a writable placeholder, so Detcore answers
4388    /// every call that would write it ([`Self::answer_rendered_procfs_write`]):
4389    /// none reaches the memfd, which KVM, without seals, would let change.
4390    pub(crate) async fn answer_rendered_procfs_operation<G: Guest<Self>>(
4391        &self,
4392        guest: &mut G,
4393        call: &Syscall,
4394    ) -> Option<Result<i64, Error>> {
4395        let (sysno, args) = call.into_parts();
4396        if let Some(answer) = self.answer_rendered_procfs_write(guest, sysno, &args).await {
4397            return Some(answer.map_err(Error::from));
4398        }
4399        if sysno == Sysno::splice
4400            && let Some(answer) = self.answer_cmdline_splice(guest, &args).await
4401        {
4402            return Some(answer);
4403        }
4404        if !operates_on_descriptor(sysno) {
4405            return None;
4406        }
4407        let fd = args.arg0 as i32;
4408        let (path, status_flags) = guest
4409            .thread_state()
4410            .with_detfd(fd, |detfd| {
4411                detfd
4412                    .procfs_rendered_path()
4413                    .map(|path| (path, detfd.status_flags()))
4414            })
4415            .ok()
4416            .flatten()?;
4417        if status_flags & libc::O_PATH != 0 {
4418            return None;
4419        }
4420        let seq_file = crate::procfs::rendered_path_is_seq_file(path);
4421        let writable = matches!(
4422            status_flags & libc::O_ACCMODE,
4423            libc::O_WRONLY | libc::O_RDWR
4424        );
4425        let per_process = crate::procfs::rendered_path_is_per_process(path);
4426        // Who owns the file matters only to the calls below that ask for the
4427        // owner or for write access, and only an attribute change asks for
4428        // the caller's capabilities; no other call pays for either.
4429        let command = args.arg1 as libc::c_int;
4430        let needs_access = matches!(sysno, Sysno::fsetxattr | Sysno::fremovexattr)
4431            || (sysno == Sysno::fcntl
4432                && (matches!(command, libc::F_SETLEASE | 1036)
4433                    || (command == libc::F_SETFL
4434                        && args.arg2 as libc::c_int & libc::O_NOATIME != 0)));
4435        let access = if needs_access {
4436            match self
4437                .rendered_file_access(
4438                    guest,
4439                    path,
4440                    matches!(sysno, Sysno::fsetxattr | Sysno::fremovexattr),
4441                    CapabilitySet::Effective,
4442                )
4443                .await
4444            {
4445                Ok(access) => access,
4446                Err(error) => return Some(Err(error)),
4447            }
4448        } else {
4449            FileAccess::GLOBAL_ROOT
4450        };
4451        let answer = match call {
4452            // A sysctl file's placeholder refuses a positioned write as the
4453            // sysctl does: it is read-only (EBADF), or the position is
4454            // negative (EINVAL).
4455            // A process's `auxv` keeps its positioned writes (`FMODE_PWRITE`):
4456            // a negative position is EINVAL first (`pwritev2` at -1, which
4457            // writes at the file position, is `writev`'s EBADF on a
4458            // read-only description; a writable one is answered by
4459            // `answer_rendered_procfs_write`); then a description that cannot
4460            // write is EBADF, and one that can the EINVAL of a file with no
4461            // write method. Answered here, as its placeholder may be writable.
4462            Syscall::Pwrite64(_) | Syscall::Pwritev(_) | Syscall::Pwritev2(_)
4463                if !seq_file && per_process =>
4464            {
4465                let position = args.arg3 as i64;
4466                if sysno == Sysno::pwritev2 && position == -1 {
4467                    Err(Errno::EBADF)
4468                } else if position < 0 || writable {
4469                    Err(Errno::EINVAL)
4470                } else {
4471                    Err(Errno::EBADF)
4472                }
4473            }
4474            Syscall::Pwrite64(_) | Syscall::Pwritev(_) | Syscall::Pwritev2(_) if !seq_file => {
4475                return None;
4476            }
4477            Syscall::Pwrite64(_) | Syscall::Pwritev(_) | Syscall::Pwritev2(_) => {
4478                return rendered_seq_file_pwrite_answer(sysno, args.arg3 as i64)
4479                    .map(|answer| answer.map_err(Error::from));
4480            }
4481            Syscall::Fsync(_) | Syscall::Fdatasync(_) => Err(Errno::EINVAL),
4482            Syscall::Fchmod(_) => Err(Errno::EPERM),
4483            Syscall::Fgetxattr(c) => match c.name().map(|name| name.read(&guest.memory())) {
4484                // The description was opened by a caller that could read the
4485                // file, so read permission holds.
4486                Some(Ok(name)) => xattr_get_answer(name.as_bytes(), true),
4487                Some(Err(errno)) => Err(errno),
4488                None => Err(Errno::EFAULT),
4489            },
4490            Syscall::Flistxattr(_) => Ok(0),
4491            Syscall::Fsetxattr(c)
4492                if c.flags() & !(libc::XATTR_CREATE | libc::XATTR_REPLACE) != 0 =>
4493            {
4494                Err(Errno::EINVAL)
4495            }
4496            Syscall::Fsetxattr(c) => match c.name().map(|name| name.read(&guest.memory())) {
4497                Some(Ok(name)) => xattr_change_answer(
4498                    name.as_bytes(),
4499                    Some(c.size()),
4500                    read_value(&guest.memory(), args.arg2 as u64, c.size()).as_deref(),
4501                    access,
4502                ),
4503                Some(Err(errno)) => Err(errno),
4504                None => Err(Errno::EFAULT),
4505            },
4506            Syscall::Fremovexattr(c) => match c.name().map(|name| name.read(&guest.memory())) {
4507                Some(Ok(name)) => xattr_change_answer(name.as_bytes(), None, None, access),
4508                Some(Err(errno)) => Err(errno),
4509                None => Err(Errno::EFAULT),
4510            },
4511            Syscall::Ioctl(_) => match args.arg1 as u32 as usize {
4512                // The generic file ioctls Linux answers before the file's own:
4513                // FIBMAP and FIFREEZE/FITHAW need capabilities, FS_IOC_FIEMAP
4514                // a file system that maps extents, and FICLONE/FICLONERANGE a
4515                // source on the same file system.
4516                1 | 0xc004_5877 | 0xc004_5878 => Err(Errno::EPERM),
4517                0xc020_660b => Err(Errno::EOPNOTSUPP),
4518                0x4004_9409 => Self::clone_ioctl_answer(guest, args.arg2 as i32),
4519                0x4020_940d => {
4520                    // The whole `struct file_clone_range` is read first.
4521                    match reverie::syscalls::Addr::<[u64; 4]>::from_raw(args.arg2)
4522                        .map(|address| guest.memory().read_value(address))
4523                    {
4524                        Some(Ok(range)) => Self::clone_ioctl_answer(guest, range[0] as i32),
4525                        _ => Err(Errno::EFAULT),
4526                    }
4527                }
4528                // FIONCLEX, FIOCLEX, FIOASYNC and FIONBIO, which the kernel
4529                // answers alike for both files.
4530                0x5450..=0x5452 | 0x5421 => return None,
4531                // FIONREAD: the file's size, 0, less the position, as an int.
4532                // Where the tool runs in each guest process, the kernel answers
4533                // from the empty placeholder's position, which Detcore keeps
4534                // current and which processes sharing the open file share.
4535                // Elsewhere Detcore answers from its own cursor, shared there
4536                // too (KVM's executor has no answer for a memfd).
4537                0x541b if self.cfg.backend.tool_shares_guest_descriptor_table => return None,
4538                0x541b => match AddrMut::<libc::c_int>::from_raw(args.arg2) {
4539                    Some(address) => {
4540                        let position = guest
4541                            .thread_state()
4542                            .with_detfd(args.arg0 as RawFd, |detfd| {
4543                                detfd.procfs_position().map_or(0, |(offset, _)| offset)
4544                            })
4545                            .unwrap_or(0);
4546                        let unread = 0_i64.wrapping_sub(position as i64) as libc::c_int;
4547                        guest.memory().write_value(address, &unread).map(|()| 0)
4548                    }
4549                    None => Err(Errno::EFAULT),
4550                },
4551                // FIOQSIZE: the bytes the file uses, none for procfs.
4552                0x5460 => match AddrMut::<libc::loff_t>::from_raw(args.arg2) {
4553                    Some(address) => guest.memory().write_value(address, &0).map(|()| 0),
4554                    None => Err(Errno::EFAULT),
4555                },
4556                // FIGETBSZ: procfs's block size.
4557                2 => match AddrMut::<libc::c_int>::from_raw(args.arg2) {
4558                    Some(address) => guest
4559                        .memory()
4560                        .write_value(address, &(procfs_inode::PROC_BLOCK_SIZE as libc::c_int))
4561                        .map(|()| 0),
4562                    None => Err(Errno::EFAULT),
4563                },
4564                _ => Err(Errno::ENOTTY),
4565            },
4566            Syscall::Fcntl(_) => match args.arg1 as libc::c_int {
4567                // Adding seals needs a writable descriptor, before Linux
4568                // looks for seals the file cannot have.
4569                libc::F_ADD_SEALS if !writable => Err(Errno::EPERM),
4570                libc::F_ADD_SEALS | libc::F_GET_SEALS => Err(Errno::EINVAL),
4571                // A lease needs the file's owner, so a system-wide file never
4572                // has one; its owner may ask, and procfs takes none.
4573                libc::F_SETLEASE if access.owner => Err(Errno::EINVAL),
4574                libc::F_SETLEASE => Err(Errno::EACCES),
4575                // F_SET_RW_HINT needs the file's owner, then reads and checks
4576                // the hint (`rw_hint_valid`), which procfs keeps on the inode.
4577                // Detcore keeps it, for every descriptor and reopening of a
4578                // per-process file (`RenderedFileState`).
4579                1036 if access.owner => {
4580                    match Addr::<u64>::from_raw(args.arg2)
4581                        .map(|hint| guest.memory().read_value(hint))
4582                    {
4583                        Some(Ok(hint)) if hint <= 5 => {
4584                            crate::tool_global::rendered_file_state(
4585                                guest,
4586                                procfs_inode::rendered_inode(path),
4587                                crate::procfs::listing::RenderedFileChange::WriteHint(hint),
4588                            )
4589                            .await;
4590                            Ok(0)
4591                        }
4592                        Some(Ok(_)) => Err(Errno::EINVAL),
4593                        _ => Err(Errno::EFAULT),
4594                    }
4595                }
4596                1036 => Err(Errno::EPERM),
4597                // F_GET_RW_HINT: the hint the inode keeps, which any
4598                // descriptor reads. A system-wide file's is never set, and its
4599                // placeholder answers 0 as the file does.
4600                1035 if per_process => {
4601                    let state = crate::tool_global::rendered_file_state(
4602                        guest,
4603                        procfs_inode::rendered_inode(path),
4604                        crate::procfs::listing::RenderedFileChange::Read,
4605                    )
4606                    .await;
4607                    match AddrMut::<u64>::from_raw(args.arg2) {
4608                        Some(address) => guest
4609                            .memory()
4610                            .write_value(address, &state.write_hint)
4611                            .map(|()| 0),
4612                        None => Err(Errno::EFAULT),
4613                    }
4614                }
4615                libc::F_GETLEASE => Ok(i64::from(libc::F_UNLCK)),
4616                // `O_NOATIME` needs the file's owner (the owner's reaches the
4617                // placeholder, which the guest owns too), and procfs files
4618                // cannot do direct I/O.
4619                libc::F_SETFL
4620                    if args.arg2 as libc::c_int & libc::O_NOATIME != 0 && !access.owner =>
4621                {
4622                    Err(Errno::EPERM)
4623                }
4624                libc::F_SETFL if args.arg2 as libc::c_int & libc::O_DIRECT != 0 => {
4625                    Err(Errno::EINVAL)
4626                }
4627                _ => return None,
4628            },
4629            _ => return None,
4630        };
4631        Some(answer.map_err(Into::into))
4632    }
4633}
4634
4635/// What a path call asks of the file it names.
4636#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
4637enum PathRequest {
4638    /// `access`: `mode`, with `faccessat2`'s `flags`.
4639    Access {
4640        mode: u64,
4641        flags: u64,
4642    },
4643    /// `getxattr`, whose attribute name the answer reads.
4644    GetXattr,
4645    ListXattr,
4646    /// `setxattr` with the value's size, the value at guest address `value`,
4647    /// and its flags; or `removexattr`.
4648    ChangeXattr {
4649        size: Option<usize>,
4650        value: u64,
4651        flags: u64,
4652    },
4653    Chmod {
4654        flags: u64,
4655    },
4656    Truncate {
4657        length: i64,
4658    },
4659    /// Setting times: `None` for the current time, as when `times` is NULL.
4660    Times {
4661        explicit: Option<bool>,
4662    },
4663    Chdir,
4664    /// Arguments Linux refuses before it looks at the file.
4665    Invalid,
4666    /// Arguments Linux cannot read.
4667    Fault,
4668}
4669
4670/// Linux's answer to `request` for a rendered procfs file (0444, or 0644 for
4671/// a fixed tunable: only global root may write either), owned and overridden
4672/// as `access` says, given the attribute `name` when it changes one.
4673fn path_request_answer(
4674    request: PathRequest,
4675    name: Option<&[u8]>,
4676    value: Option<&[u8]>,
4677    access: FileAccess,
4678) -> Result<i64, Errno> {
4679    match request {
4680        PathRequest::Access { mode, flags } => {
4681            if access_arguments_invalid(mode, flags) {
4682                Err(Errno::EINVAL)
4683            } else if mode & libc::X_OK as u64 != 0
4684                || (mode & libc::W_OK as u64 != 0 && !access.may_write())
4685                || (mode & libc::R_OK as u64 != 0 && !access.readable)
4686            {
4687                // No execute bit, which even CAP_DAC_OVERRIDE needs one of.
4688                Err(Errno::EACCES)
4689            } else {
4690                Ok(0)
4691            }
4692        }
4693        PathRequest::GetXattr => xattr_get_answer(name.ok_or(Errno::EFAULT)?, access.readable),
4694        PathRequest::ListXattr => Ok(0),
4695        PathRequest::ChangeXattr { size, flags, .. } => {
4696            if flags & !(libc::XATTR_CREATE | libc::XATTR_REPLACE) as u64 != 0 {
4697                return Err(Errno::EINVAL);
4698            }
4699            xattr_change_answer(name.ok_or(Errno::EFAULT)?, size, value, access)
4700        }
4701        PathRequest::Chmod { flags } => {
4702            if flags & !(libc::AT_SYMLINK_NOFOLLOW | libc::AT_EMPTY_PATH) as u64 != 0 {
4703                Err(Errno::EINVAL)
4704            } else {
4705                Err(Errno::EPERM)
4706            }
4707        }
4708        PathRequest::Truncate { length } if length < 0 => Err(Errno::EINVAL),
4709        // procfs's `proc_setattr` accepts a size and keeps none.
4710        PathRequest::Truncate { .. } if access.may_write() => Ok(0),
4711        PathRequest::Truncate { .. } => Err(Errno::EACCES),
4712        // Leaving both times alone changes nothing; the current time needs
4713        // write permission or the owner, and any other time the owner.
4714        // (`proc_setattr` keeps the owner's times on the inode until Linux
4715        // drops it; Detcore's metadata of the file keeps the epoch.)
4716        PathRequest::Times { explicit: None } if access.owner || access.may_write() => Ok(0),
4717        PathRequest::Times { explicit: None } => Err(Errno::EACCES),
4718        PathRequest::Times {
4719            explicit: Some(false),
4720        } => Ok(0),
4721        PathRequest::Times {
4722            explicit: Some(true),
4723        } if access.owner => Ok(0),
4724        PathRequest::Times {
4725            explicit: Some(true),
4726        } => Err(Errno::EPERM),
4727        PathRequest::Chdir => Err(Errno::ENOTDIR),
4728        PathRequest::Invalid => Err(Errno::EINVAL),
4729        PathRequest::Fault => Err(Errno::EFAULT),
4730    }
4731}
4732
4733/// How `utimensat`'s `times` asks to change a file's times: `None` for the
4734/// current time (NULL, or both `UTIME_NOW`), `Some(false)` to change nothing
4735/// (both `UTIME_OMIT`), `Some(true)` for any other time, including one
4736/// `UTIME_NOW` with one `UTIME_OMIT`, which needs the owner.
4737fn utimens_request(times: Option<[libc::timespec; 2]>) -> Option<bool> {
4738    let times = times?;
4739    if times.iter().all(|time| time.tv_nsec == libc::UTIME_OMIT) {
4740        Some(false)
4741    } else if times.iter().all(|time| time.tv_nsec == libc::UTIME_NOW) {
4742        None
4743    } else {
4744        Some(true)
4745    }
4746}
4747
4748/// Whether Linux refuses an access probe's mode or flags. It checks them
4749/// before it looks up the path (`do_faccessat`), with both already narrowed
4750/// to `int`.
4751fn access_arguments_invalid(mode: u64, flags: u64) -> bool {
4752    flags & !(libc::AT_EACCESS | libc::AT_SYMLINK_NOFOLLOW | libc::AT_EMPTY_PATH) as u64 != 0
4753        || mode & !7 != 0
4754}
4755
4756impl<T: RecordOrReplay> Detcore<T> {
4757    /// Answer a path call (or an `AT_EMPTY_PATH`/NULL-path call on a
4758    /// descriptor) if it names a procfs file Detcore renders, as Linux
4759    /// answers for the procfs file: the backend has no such file, and the
4760    /// placeholder must not be changed or shown. A spelling Detcore does not
4761    /// resolve lexically that may be a descriptor link is resolved by the
4762    /// object it reaches.
4763    pub(crate) async fn answer_rendered_procfs_path_call<G: Guest<Self>>(
4764        &self,
4765        guest: &mut G,
4766        call: &Syscall,
4767    ) -> Result<Option<i64>, Error> {
4768        // A rendered descriptor stays the file after a chroot, so only the
4769        // path spellings below depend on the root.
4770        if self.cfg.recordreplay_modes {
4771            return Ok(None);
4772        }
4773        let (sysno, args) = call.into_parts();
4774        let Some(kind) = path_call_kind(sysno) else {
4775            return Ok(None);
4776        };
4777        let [a0, a1, a2, a3, a4] =
4778            [args.arg0, args.arg1, args.arg2, args.arg3, args.arg4].map(|arg| arg as u64);
4779        let at = libc::AT_FDCWD as u64;
4780        // (dirfd, path, flags, request, attribute name pointer)
4781        // For `futimens` on an `O_PATH` descriptor: whether the call is done
4782        // before the descriptor is looked up (both times omitted), or refused
4783        // first (unknown flags).
4784        let mut decided_before_descriptor = false;
4785        let (dirfd, path, flags, request, name) = match kind {
4786            // Linux declares mode and flags `int`: it ignores the upper bits
4787            // of the raw registers.
4788            PathCall::Access => (
4789                at,
4790                a0,
4791                0,
4792                PathRequest::Access {
4793                    mode: u64::from(a1 as u32),
4794                    flags: 0,
4795                },
4796                0,
4797            ),
4798            PathCall::Faccessat => (
4799                a0,
4800                a1,
4801                0,
4802                PathRequest::Access {
4803                    mode: u64::from(a2 as u32),
4804                    flags: 0,
4805                },
4806                0,
4807            ),
4808            PathCall::Faccessat2 => (
4809                a0,
4810                a1,
4811                u64::from(a3 as u32),
4812                PathRequest::Access {
4813                    mode: u64::from(a2 as u32),
4814                    flags: u64::from(a3 as u32),
4815                },
4816                0,
4817            ),
4818            PathCall::Getxattr => (at, a0, 0, PathRequest::GetXattr, a1),
4819            PathCall::Lgetxattr => (at, a0, nofollow(), PathRequest::GetXattr, a1),
4820            PathCall::Listxattr => (at, a0, 0, PathRequest::ListXattr, 0),
4821            PathCall::Llistxattr => (at, a0, nofollow(), PathRequest::ListXattr, 0),
4822            PathCall::Setxattr => (
4823                at,
4824                a0,
4825                if sysno == Sysno::lsetxattr {
4826                    nofollow()
4827                } else {
4828                    0
4829                },
4830                PathRequest::ChangeXattr {
4831                    size: Some(a3 as usize),
4832                    value: a2,
4833                    // Linux declares the flags `int`.
4834                    flags: u64::from(a4 as u32),
4835                },
4836                a1,
4837            ),
4838            PathCall::Removexattr => (
4839                at,
4840                a0,
4841                if sysno == Sysno::lremovexattr {
4842                    nofollow()
4843                } else {
4844                    0
4845                },
4846                PathRequest::ChangeXattr {
4847                    size: None,
4848                    value: 0,
4849                    flags: 0,
4850                },
4851                a1,
4852            ),
4853            PathCall::Chmod => (at, a0, 0, PathRequest::Chmod { flags: 0 }, 0),
4854            PathCall::Fchmodat => (a0, a1, 0, PathRequest::Chmod { flags: 0 }, 0),
4855            PathCall::Fchmodat2 => (a0, a1, a3, PathRequest::Chmod { flags: a3 }, 0),
4856            // Ownership changes keep Detcore's policy for every file
4857            // (`handle_ownership_change_noop`).
4858            PathCall::Truncate => (at, a0, 0, PathRequest::Truncate { length: a1 as i64 }, 0),
4859            PathCall::Utimensat => {
4860                // Unreadable times are the kernel's EFAULT to report.
4861                let Ok(times) = read_pair::<libc::timespec, _>(&guest.memory(), a2) else {
4862                    return Ok(None);
4863                };
4864                // Both times omitted is success before anything is looked at;
4865                // unknown flags, or any flag with a NULL path, are refused
4866                // before the file is looked up; out-of-range times after it
4867                // (measured on Linux 7.1). Detcore answers the first two for
4868                // every file where it renders procfs files: Linux's answer
4869                // does not depend on the file.
4870                let omitted = times
4871                    .is_some_and(|times| times.iter().all(|time| time.tv_nsec == libc::UTIME_OMIT));
4872                // A NULL path with flags is refused only for a descriptor:
4873                // with AT_FDCWD, Linux looks the NULL path up (EFAULT).
4874                let flags_valid = a3 & !((libc::AT_SYMLINK_NOFOLLOW | libc::AT_EMPTY_PATH) as u64)
4875                    == 0
4876                    && (a1 != 0 || a3 == 0 || a0 as i32 == libc::AT_FDCWD);
4877                if self.renders_procfs(guest) {
4878                    if omitted {
4879                        return Ok(Some(0));
4880                    }
4881                    if !flags_valid {
4882                        return Err(Errno::EINVAL.into());
4883                    }
4884                    // A NULL path with AT_FDCWD is looked up, and faults (KVM's
4885                    // executor answers otherwise).
4886                    if a1 == 0 && a0 as i32 == libc::AT_FDCWD {
4887                        return Err(Errno::EFAULT.into());
4888                    }
4889                }
4890                decided_before_descriptor = omitted || !flags_valid;
4891                let valid = omitted
4892                    || (flags_valid
4893                        && times.is_none_or(|times| times.iter().all(valid_utime_nsec)));
4894                (
4895                    a0,
4896                    a1,
4897                    a3,
4898                    if valid {
4899                        PathRequest::Times {
4900                            explicit: utimens_request(times),
4901                        }
4902                    } else {
4903                        PathRequest::Invalid
4904                    },
4905                    0,
4906                )
4907            }
4908            PathCall::Utime => (
4909                at,
4910                a0,
4911                0,
4912                legacy_times_request(&guest.memory(), a1, false),
4913                0,
4914            ),
4915            PathCall::Utimes => (
4916                at,
4917                a0,
4918                0,
4919                legacy_times_request(&guest.memory(), a1, true),
4920                0,
4921            ),
4922            // `futimesat` is ENOSYS under Detcore for every file.
4923            PathCall::Chdir => (at, a0, 0, PathRequest::Chdir, 0),
4924        };
4925        // Linux refuses invalid access arguments before it looks up the path
4926        // or the descriptor, for every file; leave that order to the backend.
4927        if let PathRequest::Access { mode, flags } = request
4928            && access_arguments_invalid(mode, flags)
4929        {
4930            return Ok(None);
4931        }
4932        // Likewise invalid setxattr flags, before the name, the value or the
4933        // path, for every file (`setxattr_copy`). KVM's executor reads the
4934        // name first, so answer here rather than leave it to the backend.
4935        if let PathRequest::ChangeXattr { flags, .. } = request
4936            && flags & !(libc::XATTR_CREATE | libc::XATTR_REPLACE) as u64 != 0
4937            && self.renders_procfs(guest)
4938        {
4939            return Err(Errno::EINVAL.into());
4940        }
4941        let dirfd = dirfd as i32;
4942        let empty_path_allowed = flags & libc::AT_EMPTY_PATH as u64 != 0;
4943        // An unreadable path is the kernel's EFAULT to report.
4944        let path_text = match PathPtr::from_ptr(path as *const libc::c_char) {
4945            Some(path) => match path.read(&guest.memory()) {
4946                Ok(path) => Some(path),
4947                Err(_) => return Ok(None),
4948            },
4949            None => None,
4950        };
4951        // A descriptor named by an empty path (`AT_EMPTY_PATH`), or by none
4952        // (`futimens`).
4953        let by_descriptor = match &path_text {
4954            None => sysno == Sysno::utimensat,
4955            Some(path) => path.as_os_str().is_empty() && empty_path_allowed,
4956        };
4957        // Linux then copies and checks the attribute name, the size and the
4958        // value, still before it looks up the path (`setxattr_copy`,
4959        // `import_xattr_name`), so a path error cannot hide them. Detcore
4960        // checks them first where it may answer: a spelling that could name
4961        // a rendered file. Any other path goes to the kernel untouched.
4962        let session_files = self.rendered_files(guest);
4963        let renders_comm = self.comm_source() == crate::procfs::task_stat::CommSource::Record;
4964        let xattr_arguments = if self.renders_procfs(guest)
4965            && (by_descriptor
4966                || path_text
4967                    .as_deref()
4968                    .is_some_and(|text| could_name_rendered(text, session_files, renders_comm)))
4969        {
4970            checked_xattr_arguments(&guest.memory(), request, name)?
4971        } else {
4972            None
4973        };
4974        let rendered = if by_descriptor {
4975            let known = guest
4976                .thread_state()
4977                .with_detfd(dirfd, |detfd| {
