4978                    detfd
4979                        .procfs_rendered_path()
4980                        .map(|path| (path, detfd.status_flags() & libc::O_PATH != 0))
4981                })
4982                .ok()
4983                .flatten();
4984            // A placeholder Detcore did not record as one (an alias the kernel
4985            // opened) is known by its kernel identity, as a stat of it is: a
4986            // time set on it would change no file of the guest's and could be
4987            // read as an uptime a first read carried (`first_read_uptime`).
4988            let rendered = match known {
4989                Some(known) => Some(known),
4990                None if self.renders_procfs(guest) => {
4991                    match self.placeholder_descriptor(guest, dirfd).await {
4992                        Some(path) => {
4993                            let status = guest
4994                                .inject(
4995                                    syscalls::Fcntl::new()
4996                                        .with_fd(dirfd)
4997                                        .with_cmd(syscalls::FcntlCmd::F_GETFL),
4998                                )
4999                                .await
5000                                .unwrap_or(0);
5001                            Some((path, status & i64::from(libc::O_PATH) != 0))
5002                        }
5003                        None => None,
5004                    }
5005                }
5006                None => None,
5007            };
5008            // `futimens` needs a descriptor opened for I/O: an `O_PATH` one
5009            // is EBADF, after the flags are checked and before the times are
5010            // (measured on Linux 7.1).
5011            if let Some((_, true)) = rendered
5012                && path_text.is_none()
5013                && !decided_before_descriptor
5014            {
5015                return Err(Errno::EBADF.into());
5016            }
5017            rendered.map(|(path, _)| path)
5018        } else {
5019            let Some(text) = path_text else {
5020                return Ok(None);
5021            };
5022            if text.as_os_str().is_empty() || !self.renders_procfs(guest) {
5023                return Ok(None);
5024            }
5025            // The spellings Detcore resolves itself, which end in a rendered
5026            // file's name or a descriptor number.
5027            let files = self.rendered_files(guest);
5028            let lexical = if could_name_rendered(&text, files, renders_comm) {
5029                let observed = self.procfs_spelling_at(guest, dirfd, &text).await?;
5030                match self.rendered_procfs_at(guest, &observed).await? {
5031                    // A call that does not follow a descriptor link acts on
5032                    // the link itself, which the kernel has.
5033                    Some(target) if target.via_link && flags & nofollow() != 0 => return Ok(None),
5034                    Some(target) => target.procfs.rendered_path(files),
5035                    None => None,
5036                }
5037            } else {
5038                None
5039            };
5040            match lexical {
5041                Some(rendered) => Some(rendered),
5042                // Any other spelling (a symbolic link to a descriptor link,
5043                // `/dev/stdin`): what the kernel reaches, by its identity.
5044                // Only while this process holds a rendered descriptor, so a
5045                // process that holds none pays nothing.
5046                None => {
5047                    self.placeholder_reached(
5048                        guest,
5049                        dirfd,
5050                        PathPtr::from_ptr(path as *const libc::c_char),
5051                        flags & nofollow() == 0,
5052                    )
5053                    .await
5054                }
5055            }
5056        };
5057        if rendered.is_none() {
5058            return Ok(None);
5059        }
5060        // A spelling that reached a rendered file another way (a link) has
5061        // its attribute arguments checked here, after the path.
5062        let xattr_arguments = match xattr_arguments {
5063            Some(arguments) => Some(arguments),
5064            None => checked_xattr_arguments(&guest.memory(), request, name)?,
5065        };
5066        let (name, value) = match xattr_arguments {
5067            Some((name, value)) => (Some(name), value),
5068            None => (None, None),
5069        };
5070        let rendered = rendered.expect("checked above");
5071        // A request for write access (an access probe for it, a truncation,
5072        // an attribute change) asks for the caller's capabilities; `access`
5073        // and `faccessat` without `AT_EACCESS` check the real IDs' set.
5074        let needs_capabilities = match request {
5075            PathRequest::Access { mode, .. } => mode & libc::W_OK as u64 != 0,
5076            PathRequest::Truncate { .. } | PathRequest::ChangeXattr { .. } => true,
5077            _ => false,
5078        };
5079        let set = match request {
5080            PathRequest::Access { flags, .. } if flags & libc::AT_EACCESS as u64 == 0 => {
5081                CapabilitySet::Permitted
5082            }
5083            _ => CapabilitySet::Effective,
5084        };
5085        let access = self
5086            .rendered_file_access(guest, rendered, needs_capabilities, set)
5087            .await?;
5088        let answer = path_request_answer(request, name.as_deref(), value.as_deref(), access);
5089        // A time set on a per-process file stays on its inode (`proc_setattr`
5090        // keeps what `setattr_copy` copies), which every alias of the file
5091        // shows: Detcore keeps the modification time in its global tool. The
5092        // access and change times are the epoch for every rendered file, as
5093        // Detcore's metadata shows them for every file it virtualizes.
5094        if answer == Ok(0)
5095            && matches!(request, PathRequest::Times { .. })
5096            && access.owner
5097            && crate::procfs::per_process_name(rendered).is_some()
5098            && let Some((seconds, nanoseconds)) =
5099                self.requested_mtime(guest, sysno, [a0, a1, a2]).await
5100        {
5101            crate::tool_global::rendered_file_state(
5102                guest,
5103                procfs_inode::rendered_inode(rendered),
5104                crate::procfs::listing::RenderedFileChange::Mtime(seconds, nanoseconds),
5105            )
5106            .await;
5107        }
5108        answer.map(Some).map_err(Into::into)
5109    }
5110
5111    /// The modification time a successful `utimensat`, `utime` or `utimes`
5112    /// with arguments `args` sets: the given time, or the virtual time now for
5113    /// a NULL `times` or `UTIME_NOW`; `None` where it leaves it alone
5114    /// (`UTIME_OMIT`) or its times cannot be read.
5115    async fn requested_mtime<G: Guest<Self>>(
5116        &self,
5117        guest: &mut G,
5118        sysno: Sysno,
5119        [_, a1, a2]: [u64; 3],
5120    ) -> Option<(i64, i64)> {
5121        let times = if sysno == Sysno::utimensat { a2 } else { a1 };
5122        let explicit = if times == 0 {
5123            None
5124        } else if sysno == Sysno::utimensat {
5125            let [_, mtime] = read_pair::<libc::timespec, _>(&guest.memory(), times)
5126                .ok()
5127                .flatten()?;
5128            match mtime.tv_nsec {
5129                libc::UTIME_OMIT => return None,
5130                libc::UTIME_NOW => None,
5131                _ => Some((mtime.tv_sec, mtime.tv_nsec)),
5132            }
5133        } else if sysno == Sysno::utime {
5134            let [_, modtime] = read_pair::<libc::time_t, _>(&guest.memory(), times)
5135                .ok()
5136                .flatten()?;
5137            Some((modtime, 0))
5138        } else {
5139            let [_, mtime] = read_pair::<libc::timeval, _>(&guest.memory(), times)
5140                .ok()
5141                .flatten()?;
5142            Some((mtime.tv_sec, mtime.tv_usec * 1000))
5143        };
5144        match explicit {
5145            Some(time) => Some(time),
5146            None => {
5147                let now: Timespec = crate::tool_global::thread_observe_time(guest).await.into();
5148                Some((now.tv_sec, now.tv_nsec))
5149            }
5150        }
5151    }
5152}
5153
5154/// The descriptors `sysno` with arguments `args` names, which a placeholder
5155/// may be (`Detcore::adopt_received_placeholders`): the descriptor of every
5156/// call that reads, writes, seeks, maps, describes, changes, duplicates or
5157/// transfers an open file, and the directory descriptor of a path call whose
5158/// path is empty or NULL, which names that descriptor itself. Not `close`,
5159/// which needs no model of what it closes.
5160pub(super) fn descriptor_arguments<M: MemoryAccess>(
5161    memory: &M,
5162    sysno: Sysno,
5163    args: &reverie::syscalls::SyscallArgs,
5164) -> Vec<RawFd> {
5165    let names_itself = |path: usize| -> bool {
5166        match PathPtr::from_ptr(path as *const libc::c_char) {
5167            None => true,
5168            Some(pointer) => pointer
5169                .read(memory)
5170                .is_ok_and(|path: std::path::PathBuf| path.as_os_str().is_empty()),
5171        }
5172    };
5173    let values = [
5174        args.arg0, args.arg1, args.arg2, args.arg3, args.arg4, args.arg5,
5175    ];
5176    let fd = |position: usize| values[position] as i32 as RawFd;
5177    match descriptor_positions(sysno) {
5178        Some(DescriptorPositions::Always(positions)) => {
5179            positions.iter().map(|&position| fd(position)).collect()
5180        }
5181        Some(DescriptorPositions::WhenPathNamesItself { dirfd, path })
5182            if names_itself(values[path]) =>
5183        {
5184            vec![fd(dirfd)]
5185        }
5186        _ => Vec::new(),
5187    }
5188}
5189
5190/// Where a call's descriptor arguments ([`descriptor_arguments`]) are.
5191#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug)]
5192enum DescriptorPositions {
5193    /// The descriptors at these argument positions, always.
5194    Always(&'static [usize]),
5195    /// The directory descriptor at `dirfd`, when the path at `path` is empty
5196    /// or NULL and so names that descriptor itself.
5197    WhenPathNamesItself { dirfd: usize, path: usize },
5198}
5199
5200/// The argument positions of the descriptors a call numbered `sysno` may
5201/// name ([`descriptor_arguments`]); `None` for a call that names none.
5202fn descriptor_positions(sysno: Sysno) -> Option<DescriptorPositions> {
5203    Some(match sysno {
5204        Sysno::read
5205        | Sysno::readv
5206        | Sysno::pread64
5207        | Sysno::preadv
5208        | Sysno::preadv2
5209        | Sysno::write
5210        | Sysno::writev
5211        | Sysno::pwrite64
5212        | Sysno::pwritev
5213        | Sysno::pwritev2
5214        | Sysno::lseek
5215        | Sysno::fstat
5216        | Sysno::fstatfs
5217        | Sysno::fcntl
5218        | Sysno::ioctl
5219        | Sysno::ftruncate
5220        | Sysno::fallocate
5221        | Sysno::fsync
5222        | Sysno::fdatasync
5223        | Sysno::fadvise64
5224        | Sysno::readahead
5225        | Sysno::sync_file_range
5226        | Sysno::fchmod
5227        | Sysno::fchown
5228        | Sysno::fgetxattr
5229        | Sysno::fsetxattr
5230        | Sysno::flistxattr
5231        | Sysno::fremovexattr
5232        | Sysno::flock
5233        | Sysno::dup
5234        | Sysno::dup2
5235        | Sysno::dup3
5236        | Sysno::getdents
5237        | Sysno::getdents64
5238        | Sysno::fchdir => DescriptorPositions::Always(&[0]),
5239        Sysno::mmap => DescriptorPositions::Always(&[4]),
5240        Sysno::sendfile | Sysno::tee => DescriptorPositions::Always(&[0, 1]),
5241        Sysno::splice | Sysno::copy_file_range => DescriptorPositions::Always(&[0, 2]),
5242        Sysno::epoll_ctl => DescriptorPositions::Always(&[2]),
5243        Sysno::newfstatat
5244        | Sysno::statx
5245        | Sysno::utimensat
5246        | Sysno::faccessat2
5247        | Sysno::fchownat
5248        | Sysno::fchmodat
5249        | Sysno::readlinkat => DescriptorPositions::WhenPathNamesItself { dirfd: 0, path: 1 },
5250        _ => return None,
5251    })
5252}
5253
5254/// Whether a call numbered `sysno` may name a descriptor
5255/// ([`descriptor_arguments`], whatever its arguments): the calls
5256/// [`Detcore::adopt_received_placeholders`] can act on. Part of
5257/// `may_name_file_or_descriptor`.
5258pub(crate) fn names_a_descriptor(sysno: Sysno) -> bool {
5259    descriptor_positions(sysno).is_some()
5260}
5261
5262/// The argument position of the descriptor a call numbered `sysno` writes
5263/// through, as [`Detcore::answer_rendered_procfs_write`] answers it: `write`,
5264/// `writev`, `ftruncate`, `fallocate`, `pwritev2` (only at the file
5265/// position, which its offset decides) and `sendfile` write their argument
5266/// 0, and `splice` its argument 2. `None` for any other call.
5267fn written_descriptor_position(sysno: Sysno) -> Option<usize> {
5268    match sysno {
5269        Sysno::write
5270        | Sysno::writev
5271        | Sysno::ftruncate
5272        | Sysno::fallocate
5273        | Sysno::pwritev2
5274        | Sysno::sendfile => Some(0),
5275        Sysno::splice => Some(2),
5276        _ => None,
5277    }
5278}
5279
5280/// Whether a call numbered `sysno` is one whose argument-0 descriptor
5281/// [`Detcore::answer_rendered_procfs_operation`] answers for a rendered
5282/// procfs file, after the writes ([`written_descriptor_position`]).
5283fn operates_on_descriptor(sysno: Sysno) -> bool {
5284    matches!(
5285        sysno,
5286        Sysno::pwrite64
5287            | Sysno::pwritev
5288            | Sysno::pwritev2
5289            | Sysno::fsync
5290            | Sysno::fdatasync
5291            | Sysno::fchmod
5292            | Sysno::fgetxattr
5293            | Sysno::flistxattr
5294            | Sysno::fsetxattr
5295            | Sysno::fremovexattr
5296            | Sysno::ioctl
5297            | Sysno::fcntl
5298    )
5299}
5300
5301/// Whether [`Detcore::answer_rendered_procfs_operation`] can answer a call
5302/// numbered `sysno`: a write through a descriptor
5303/// ([`written_descriptor_position`], `splice` included, which a `cmdline`
5304/// source also answers) or an operation on one ([`operates_on_descriptor`]).
5305/// Part of `may_name_file_or_descriptor`.
5306pub(crate) fn may_answer_operation(sysno: Sysno) -> bool {
5307    written_descriptor_position(sysno).is_some() || operates_on_descriptor(sysno)
5308}
5309
5310/// Whether [`Detcore::answer_rendered_procfs_mapping`] can answer a call
5311/// numbered `sysno`: only `mmap` (`Syscall::Mmap`). Part of
5312/// `may_name_file_or_descriptor`.
5313pub(crate) fn may_answer_mapping(sysno: Sysno) -> bool {
5314    sysno == Sysno::mmap
5315}
5316
5317/// The calls [`Detcore::answer_rendered_procfs_path_call`] answers, one per
5318/// arm of its table, which matches on this exhaustively: a call can reach
5319/// the table only through [`path_call_kind`], and a new arm needs a variant
5320/// here.
5321#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug)]
5322enum PathCall {
5323    Access,
5324    Faccessat,
5325    Faccessat2,
5326    Getxattr,
5327    Lgetxattr,
5328    Listxattr,
5329    Llistxattr,
5330    /// `setxattr` and `lsetxattr`.
5331    Setxattr,
5332    /// `removexattr` and `lremovexattr`.
5333    Removexattr,
5334    Chmod,
5335    Fchmodat,
5336    Fchmodat2,
5337    Truncate,
5338    Utimensat,
5339    Utime,
5340    Utimes,
5341    Chdir,
5342}
5343
5344/// The arm of [`Detcore::answer_rendered_procfs_path_call`]'s table for a
5345/// call numbered `sysno`, or `None` for a call the table has no arm for.
5346fn path_call_kind(sysno: Sysno) -> Option<PathCall> {
5347    Some(match sysno {
5348        Sysno::access => PathCall::Access,
5349        Sysno::faccessat => PathCall::Faccessat,
5350        Sysno::faccessat2 => PathCall::Faccessat2,
5351        Sysno::getxattr => PathCall::Getxattr,
5352        Sysno::lgetxattr => PathCall::Lgetxattr,
5353        Sysno::listxattr => PathCall::Listxattr,
5354        Sysno::llistxattr => PathCall::Llistxattr,
5355        Sysno::setxattr | Sysno::lsetxattr => PathCall::Setxattr,
5356        Sysno::removexattr | Sysno::lremovexattr => PathCall::Removexattr,
5357        Sysno::chmod => PathCall::Chmod,
5358        Sysno::fchmodat => PathCall::Fchmodat,
5359        Sysno::fchmodat2 => PathCall::Fchmodat2,
5360        Sysno::truncate => PathCall::Truncate,
5361        Sysno::utimensat => PathCall::Utimensat,
5362        Sysno::utime => PathCall::Utime,
5363        Sysno::utimes => PathCall::Utimes,
5364        Sysno::chdir => PathCall::Chdir,
5365        _ => return None,
5366    })
5367}
5368
5369/// Whether [`Detcore::answer_rendered_procfs_path_call`] can answer a call
5370/// numbered `sysno`: whether its table has an arm for it
5371/// ([`path_call_kind`], the only way into the table). Part of
5372/// `may_name_file_or_descriptor`.
5373pub(crate) fn may_answer_path_call(sysno: Sysno) -> bool {
5374    path_call_kind(sysno).is_some()
5375}
5376
5377/// An extended attribute's name, and the value a change sets.
5378type XattrArguments = (Vec<u8>, Option<Vec<u8>>);
5379
5380/// An extended-attribute call's name and value, checked as Linux checks them
5381/// before the path (`setxattr_copy`, `import_xattr_name`): the name (EFAULT,
5382/// or ERANGE when empty or longer than `XATTR_NAME_MAX`), then the size
5383/// (E2BIG), then the value (EFAULT). None for any other request.
5384fn checked_xattr_arguments<M: MemoryAccess>(
5385    memory: &M,
5386    request: PathRequest,
5387    name: u64,
5388) -> Result<Option<XattrArguments>, Errno> {
5389    if !matches!(
5390        request,
5391        PathRequest::GetXattr | PathRequest::ChangeXattr { .. }
5392    ) {
5393        return Ok(None);
5394    }
5395    let name = read_xattr_name(memory, name)?;
5396    if name.is_empty() || name.len() > XATTR_NAME_MAX {
5397        return Err(Errno::ERANGE);
5398    }
5399    let value = match request {
5400        PathRequest::ChangeXattr {
5401            size: Some(size),
5402            value,
5403            ..
5404        } => {
5405            if size > XATTR_SIZE_MAX {
5406                return Err(Errno::E2BIG);
5407            }
5408            let read = read_value(memory, value, size);
5409            if size > 0 && read.is_none() {
5410                return Err(Errno::EFAULT);
5411            }
5412            read
5413        }
5414        _ => None,
5415    };
5416    Ok(Some((name, value)))
5417}
5418
5419/// An extended attribute's name at guest address `address`, imported as
5420/// Linux does (`import_xattr_name`): at most `XATTR_NAME_MAX + 1` bytes are
5421/// read, so a name with no NUL within them is too long (ERANGE) even when
5422/// the bytes after them cannot be read, and only a fault within them is
5423/// EFAULT. The answer checks the length.
5424///
5425/// Each read stays within one page and uses the guest's own access rights
5426/// (`read_exact_with_user_access`): an exact read that faults only when a
5427/// needed byte does, as the kernel's copy faults.
5428fn read_xattr_name<M: MemoryAccess>(memory: &M, address: u64) -> Result<Vec<u8>, Errno> {
5429    const PAGE: u64 = 4096;
5430    let mut name = Vec::new();
5431    let limit = XATTR_NAME_MAX + 1;
5432    while name.len() < limit {
5433        let start = address
5434            .checked_add(name.len() as u64)
5435            .ok_or(Errno::EFAULT)?;
5436        let page_left = (PAGE - start % PAGE) as usize;
5437        let mut chunk = vec![0u8; page_left.min(limit - name.len())];
5438        let chunk_address =
5439            reverie::syscalls::Addr::<u8>::from_raw(start as usize).ok_or(Errno::EFAULT)?;
5440        memory
5441            .read_exact_with_user_access(chunk_address, &mut chunk)
5442            .map_err(|_| Errno::EFAULT)?;
5443        match chunk.iter().position(|byte| *byte == 0) {
5444            Some(end) => {
5445                name.extend_from_slice(&chunk[..end]);
5446                return Ok(name);
5447            }
5448            None => name.extend_from_slice(&chunk),
5449        }
5450    }
5451    Ok(name)
5452}
5453
5454/// `AT_SYMLINK_NOFOLLOW`, as the path-call table records `l*` calls.
5455const fn nofollow() -> u64 {
5456    libc::AT_SYMLINK_NOFOLLOW as u64
5457}
5458
5459/// Whether `path` could be a spelling Detcore resolves to a rendered file
5460/// itself: its last component is a rendered file's name or a descriptor
5461/// number. A task's `comm` is one only where Detcore may render it
5462/// (`renders_comm`: its record is the name's source, package K slice A+);
5463/// elsewhere the kernel serves every `comm` and its spelling goes to the
5464/// kernel untouched, as before.
5465fn could_name_rendered(path: &Path, files: RenderedFiles, renders_comm: bool) -> bool {
5466    let Some(std::path::Component::Normal(last)) = path.components().next_back() else {
5467        return false;
5468    };
5469    let last = last.as_bytes();
5470    rendered_paths(files).any(|rendered| rendered_name(rendered) == last)
5471        || ProcfsFile::PER_PROCESS_NAMES
5472            .iter()
5473            .filter(|name| renders_comm || **name != "comm")
5474            .any(|name| name.as_bytes() == last)
5475        || (!last.is_empty() && last.iter().all(u8::is_ascii_digit))
5476}
5477
5478/// The guest's bytes at `at`, up to the first one it cannot read, through
5479/// the vectored, protection-respecting copy (`super::read_guest_prefix`):
5480/// how many it read. Any failure other than a fault reads nothing.
5481fn read_prefix<M: MemoryAccess>(memory: &M, at: u64, buffer: &mut [u8]) -> usize {
5482    usize::try_from(at)
5483        .ok()
5484        .and_then(AddrMut::<u8>::from_raw)
5485        .map_or(0, |address| {
5486            super::read_guest_prefix(memory, address, buffer).unwrap_or(0)
5487        })
5488}
5489
5490/// The calling process's memory as a read of its own rendered
5491/// `/proc/<pid>/cmdline` reads and copies it (`procfs::CmdlineMemory`):
5492/// reads through the vectored, protection-respecting copy, as Linux's
5493/// `access_remote_vm` without `FOLL_FORCE` (never `PTRACE_PEEKDATA`, which
5494/// reaches pages the guest made unreadable and reads whole words), and
5495/// copies to the caller's buffer at `destination` through the user-access
5496/// write, up to its first byte the guest cannot write. A failure other than
5497/// a fault (the backend's) is kept in `failure` and ends the read.
5498struct GuestCmdlineMemory<'m, M> {
5499    memory: &'m mut M,
5500    destination: usize,
5501    failure: Option<Errno>,
5502}
5503
5504impl<M: MemoryAccess> crate::procfs::CmdlineMemory for GuestCmdlineMemory<'_, M> {
5505    fn read(&mut self, at: u64, buffer: &mut [u8]) -> usize {
5506        if self.failure.is_some() {
5507            return 0;
5508        }
5509        let Some(address) = usize::try_from(at).ok().and_then(AddrMut::<u8>::from_raw) else {
5510            return 0;
5511        };
5512        match super::read_guest_prefix(&*self.memory, address, buffer) {
5513            Ok(read) => read,
5514            Err(errno) => {
5515                self.failure = Some(errno);
5516                0
5517            }
5518        }
5519    }
5520
5521    fn copy_out(&mut self, offset: usize, bytes: &[u8]) -> usize {
5522        let mut copied = 0;
5523        while copied < bytes.len() && self.failure.is_none() {
5524            let Some(address) = self
5525                .destination
5526                .checked_add(offset + copied)
5527                .and_then(AddrMut::<u8>::from_raw)
5528            else {
5529                break;
5530            };
5531            match self
5532                .memory
5533                .write_with_user_access(address, &bytes[copied..])
5534            {
5535                Ok(0) | Err(Errno::EFAULT) => break,
5536                Ok(written) => copied += written,
5537                Err(errno) => self.failure = Some(errno),
5538            }
5539        }
5540        copied
5541    }
5542}
5543
5544/// An attribute value of `size` bytes at guest address `address` (at most
5545/// `XATTR_SIZE_MAX`, which the answer checks first), if the guest can read it.
5546fn read_value<M: MemoryAccess>(memory: &M, address: u64, size: usize) -> Option<Vec<u8>> {
5547    let size = size.min(XATTR_SIZE_MAX);
5548    if size == 0 {
5549        return Some(Vec::new());
5550    }
5551    let address = reverie::syscalls::Addr::<u8>::from_raw(address as usize)?;
5552    let mut value = vec![0; size];
5553    // Exact, with the guest's own access rights, as the kernel copies it.
5554    memory
5555        .read_exact_with_user_access(address, &mut value)
5556        .ok()?;
5557    Some(value)
5558}
5559
5560/// What `utime` (a `struct utimbuf`) or `utimes`/`futimesat` (two `struct
5561/// timeval`, when `timevals`) asks of a file's times at guest address
5562/// `times`: NULL is the current time; the times are read, and for `timeval`s
5563/// their microseconds checked, before the file is.
5564fn legacy_times_request<M: MemoryAccess>(memory: &M, times: u64, timevals: bool) -> PathRequest {
5565    if times == 0 {
5566        return PathRequest::Times { explicit: None };
5567    }
5568    if !timevals {
5569        // A `struct utimbuf`: two `time_t`, with nothing to check.
5570        return match read_pair::<libc::time_t, M>(memory, times) {
5571            Err(_) => PathRequest::Fault,
5572            Ok(_) => PathRequest::Times {
5573                explicit: Some(true),
5574            },
5575        };
5576    }
5577    match read_pair::<libc::timeval, M>(memory, times) {
5578        Err(_) => PathRequest::Fault,
5579        Ok(Some(values))
5580            if values
5581                .iter()
5582                .any(|value| !(0..1_000_000).contains(&value.tv_usec)) =>
5583        {
5584            PathRequest::Invalid
5585        }
5586        Ok(_) => PathRequest::Times {
5587            explicit: Some(true),
5588        },
5589    }
5590}
5591
5592/// Whether a `utimensat` time's nanoseconds are in range or name a special
5593/// value.
5594fn valid_utime_nsec(time: &libc::timespec) -> bool {
5595    (0..1_000_000_000).contains(&time.tv_nsec)
5596        || time.tv_nsec == libc::UTIME_NOW
5597        || time.tv_nsec == libc::UTIME_OMIT
5598}
5599
5600/// Two values of `U` at guest address `address`, or `None` for NULL.
5601fn read_pair<U: Copy, M: MemoryAccess>(memory: &M, address: u64) -> Result<Option<[U; 2]>, Error> {
5602    match reverie::syscalls::Addr::<[U; 2]>::from_raw(address as usize) {
5603        Some(address) => Ok(Some(memory.read_value(address)?)),
5604        None => Ok(None),
5605    }
5606}
5607
5608/// What `ioctl(fd, IOC_WATCH_QUEUE_SET_SIZE, 0)` on a pipe or FIFO says about
5609/// whether splice can use it (`Detcore::splice_pipe`): `Some(true)` for a pipe
5610/// without a watch queue (ENODEV, or ENOTTY from a kernel without watch
5611/// queues), `Some(false)` for a notification pipe (EBUSY once sized, EINVAL
5612/// for the size 0 before), which `get_pipe_info(file, true)` refuses, and
5613/// `None` for any other answer, which classifies nothing.
5614fn splice_pipe_from_watch_queue_probe(probe: Result<i64, Errno>) -> Option<bool> {
5615    match probe {
5616        Err(Errno::ENODEV | Errno::ENOTTY) => Some(true),
5617        Err(Errno::EBUSY | Errno::EINVAL) => Some(false),
5618        _ => None,
5619    }
5620}
5621
5622/// Whether a path written at `at`, `length` bytes, for a kernel read of
5623/// another process's `/proc/<pid>/cmdline` (`Detcore::kernel_cmdline_read`)
5624/// overlaps a range it must not lie in: the caller's buffer, `count` bytes at
5625/// `destination`, whose bytes restoring the scratch would erase; or the
5626/// target's argument and environment area `source`, which get_mm_cmdline
5627/// reads from `arg_start` through `env_end` (fs/proc/base.c:319-328), and
5628/// which a caller sharing the target's memory (`CLONE_VM`) would have
5629/// overwritten with the path while the kernel reads it. Empty ranges overlap
5630/// nothing.
5631fn cmdline_path_overlaps(
5632    at: usize,
5633    length: usize,
5634    destination: usize,
5635    count: usize,
5636    source: Option<crate::procfs::CmdlineArea>,
5637) -> bool {
5638    let end = at.saturating_add(length);
5639    let hits = |start: usize, stop: usize| start < stop && at < stop && start < end;
5640    hits(destination, destination.saturating_add(count))
5641        || source.is_some_and(|area| {
5642            hits(
5643                area.arg_start as usize,
5644                area.env_end.max(area.arg_end) as usize,
5645            )
5646        })
5647}

5751
5752    /// The 255th task generation of a number binds a file; the 256th, which
5753    /// no inode number tells apart, is refused by name through the
5754    /// unsupported-operation policy: with --allow-unsupported-syscalls the
5755    /// call is EOVERFLOW, and a fail-closed run stops naming the call (the
5756    /// `exit_on_unsupported_syscall` form of the stop: the run's tool error,
5757    /// `UnsupportedSyscallError`). No file is bound to the 256th.
5758    #[tokio::test]
5759    async fn the_256th_task_generation_is_refused_by_name() {
5760        use reverie::Tool as _;
5761        let answer = |process| crate::procfs::listing::ProcfsTaskAnswer {
5762            exists: true,
5763            without_mm: false,
5764            process_incarnation: process,
5765            thread_incarnation: None,
5766            mm: None,
5767            runtime_private_memory: None,
5768        };
5769        for (fail_closed, incarnation, expected) in [
5770            (true, procfs_inode::MAX_INCARNATION, "bound"),
5771            (false, procfs_inode::MAX_INCARNATION + 1, "EOVERFLOW"),
5772            (true, procfs_inode::MAX_INCARNATION + 1, "stop"),
5773        ] {
5774            let config = crate::config::Config {
5775                panic_on_unsupported_syscalls: fail_closed,
5776                exit_on_unsupported_syscall: true,
5777                shutdown_on_unsupported_syscall: false,
5778                ..crate::config::Config::default()
5779            };
5780            let tool = Detcore::new(reverie::Tid::from_raw(3), &config);
5781            let mut guest = RefusalGuest {
5782                config: &config,
5783                thread: crate::ThreadState::new(crate::types::DetTid::from_raw(3), &config, ()),
5784            };
5785            let mut file = ProcfsFile::from_path(Path::new("/proc/stat")).unwrap();
5786            let result = tool
5787                .bind_task_identity(&mut guest, &mut file, answer(incarnation))
5788                .await;
5789            let bound = file.task_incarnation();
5790            match (expected, result) {
5791                ("bound", Ok(())) => assert_eq!(bound, Some(answer(incarnation).incarnation())),
5792                ("EOVERFLOW", Err(Error::Errno(Errno::EOVERFLOW))) => assert_eq!(bound, None),
5793                ("stop", Err(Error::Tool(error))) => assert_eq!(
5794                    error
5795                        .downcast_ref::<crate::UnsupportedSyscallError>()
5796                        .expect("the policy's named refusal")
5797                        .0,
5798                    Sysno::openat
5799                ),
5800                (expected, result) => panic!("{incarnation}: expected {expected}, got {result:?}"),
5801            }
5802            if expected != "bound" {
5803                assert_eq!(bound, None, "a refused generation is bound to no file");
5804            }
5805        }
5806    }
5807
5808    /// A read of the caller's own `cmdline` reads guest memory through the
5809    /// vectored, protection-respecting copy (`GuestCmdlineMemory`), as
5810    /// `access_remote_vm` without `FOLL_FORCE`: here real memory of this
5811    /// process, through `LocalMemory`. The setproctitle test reads exactly
5812    /// the one byte at `arg_end - 1`, which a following unmapped page does not
5813    /// hide (a word-sized `PTRACE_PEEKDATA` there would fail); a PROT_NONE
5814    /// page of the argument area reads nothing, as on Linux, and a read at a
5815    /// position past it reads the next page.
5816    #[test]
5817    fn the_watch_queue_probe_tells_a_notification_pipe() {
5818        // A pipe without a watch queue, and any pipe of a kernel without them.
5819        assert_eq!(
5820            splice_pipe_from_watch_queue_probe(Err(Errno::ENODEV)),
5821            Some(true)
5822        );
5823        assert_eq!(
5824            splice_pipe_from_watch_queue_probe(Err(Errno::ENOTTY)),
5825            Some(true)
5826        );
5827        // A notification pipe, sized or not.
5828        assert_eq!(
5829            splice_pipe_from_watch_queue_probe(Err(Errno::EBUSY)),
5830            Some(false)
5831        );
5832        assert_eq!(
5833            splice_pipe_from_watch_queue_probe(Err(Errno::EINVAL)),
5834            Some(false)
5835        );
5836        // Anything else classifies nothing; a success cannot happen for the
5837        // size 0 and is no answer either.
5838        assert_eq!(splice_pipe_from_watch_queue_probe(Ok(0)), None);
5839        assert_eq!(splice_pipe_from_watch_queue_probe(Err(Errno::EPERM)), None);
5840        assert_eq!(splice_pipe_from_watch_queue_probe(Err(Errno::EBADF)), None);
5841    }
5842
5843    #[test]
5844    fn a_cmdline_path_avoids_the_buffer_and_the_source_area() {
5845        let area = crate::procfs::CmdlineArea {
5846            arg_start: 0x1000,
5847            arg_end: 0x1040,
5848            env_start: 0x1040,
5849            env_end: 0x1080,
5850        };
5851        // The caller's buffer: touching ends do not overlap; a count of 0
5852        // overlaps nothing.
5853        assert!(cmdline_path_overlaps(0x500, 64, 0x520, 8, None));
5854        assert!(!cmdline_path_overlaps(0x500, 64, 0x540, 8, None));
5855        assert!(!cmdline_path_overlaps(0x500, 64, 0x4f8, 8, None));
5856        assert!(cmdline_path_overlaps(0x500, 64, 0x4f8, 9, None));
5857        assert!(!cmdline_path_overlaps(0x500, 64, 0x520, 0, None));
5858        // The target's area, arguments through environment (a CLONE_VM
5859        // child's stack pointer inside its parent's arguments).
5860        assert!(cmdline_path_overlaps(0x1000, 64, 0x9000, 8, Some(area)));
5861        assert!(cmdline_path_overlaps(0x1070, 64, 0x9000, 8, Some(area)));
5862        assert!(cmdline_path_overlaps(0xfc1, 64, 0x9000, 8, Some(area)));
5863        assert!(!cmdline_path_overlaps(0xfc0, 64, 0x9000, 8, Some(area)));
5864        assert!(!cmdline_path_overlaps(0x1080, 64, 0x9000, 8, Some(area)));
5865        // An environment area that does not follow the arguments still
5866        // leaves arg_start..arg_end protected.
5867        let detached = crate::procfs::CmdlineArea { env_end: 0, ..area };
5868        assert!(cmdline_path_overlaps(0x1020, 8, 0x9000, 8, Some(detached)));
5869        assert!(!cmdline_path_overlaps(0x1040, 8, 0x9000, 8, Some(detached)));
5870    }
5871
5872    #[test]
5873    fn a_cmdline_read_respects_page_protection() {
5874        const PAGE: usize = 4096;
5875        // SAFETY: a fresh private anonymous mapping, unmapped below.
5876        let base = unsafe {
5877            libc::mmap(
5878                std::ptr::null_mut(),
5879                3 * PAGE,
5880                libc::PROT_READ | libc::PROT_WRITE,
5881                libc::MAP_PRIVATE | libc::MAP_ANONYMOUS,
5882                -1,
5883                0,
5884            )
5885        };
5886        assert_ne!(base, libc::MAP_FAILED);
5887        let base = base as usize;
5888        let page1 = base + PAGE;
5889        let page2 = base + 2 * PAGE;
5890        let titled = b"title\0xyz";
5891        let other = b"abc\0";
5892        // SAFETY: both ranges lie in the mapping, before its protection
5893        // changes.
5894        unsafe {
5895            std::ptr::copy_nonoverlapping(
5896                titled.as_ptr(),
5897                (page2 - titled.len()) as *mut u8,
5898                titled.len(),
5899            );
5900            std::ptr::copy_nonoverlapping(other.as_ptr(), page1 as *mut u8, other.len());
5901            assert_eq!(libc::munmap(page2 as *mut libc::c_void, PAGE), 0);
5902            assert_eq!(
5903                libc::mprotect(base as *mut libc::c_void, PAGE, libc::PROT_NONE),
5904                0
5905            );
5906        }
5907        let read = |area: &crate::procfs::CmdlineArea, pos: u64, count: usize| {
5908            let mut out = vec![0_u8; count];
5909            let mut memory = reverie::syscalls::LocalMemory::new();
5910            let mut adapter = GuestCmdlineMemory {
5911                memory: &mut memory,
5912                destination: out.as_mut_ptr() as usize,
5913                failure: None,
5914            };
5915            let copied = crate::procfs::read_cmdline(area, pos, count, &mut adapter).unwrap();
5916            assert_eq!(adapter.failure, None);
5917            out.truncate(copied);
5918            out
5919        };
5920        let area = |start: usize, end: usize| crate::procfs::CmdlineArea {
5921            arg_start: start as u64,
5922            arg_end: end as u64,
5923            env_start: end as u64,
5924            env_end: end as u64,
5925        };
5926        // The argument area ends at the last byte before an unmapped page,
5927        // which is not NUL: a title.
5928        assert_eq!(read(&area(page2 - titled.len(), page2), 0, 64), b"title\0");
5929        // The argument area lies in a PROT_NONE page: nothing.
5930        assert_eq!(read(&area(base + 100, base + 110), 0, 64), b"");
5931        // It starts in the PROT_NONE page and ends in the next: a read from
5932        // the start reads nothing, one at a position in the next page reads
5933        // it.
5934        let spanning = area(page1 - 6, page1 + other.len());
5935        assert_eq!(read(&spanning, 0, 64), b"");
5936        assert_eq!(read(&spanning, 6, 64), b"abc\0");
5937        // SAFETY: the two pages still mapped, unmapped once.
5938        unsafe {
5939            assert_eq!(libc::munmap(base as *mut libc::c_void, 2 * PAGE), 0);
5940        }
5941    }
5942
5943    /// The snapshot a placeholder carries across `exec` is the one its open
5944    /// took, and a later image continues from it.
5945    #[test]
5946    fn a_rendered_snapshot_survives_exec() {
5947        let open = || {
5948            ProcfsFile::from_path(Path::new("/proc/sys/kernel/random/uuid"))
5949                .unwrap()
5950                .into_rendered()
5951        };
5952        let mut before = open();
5953        assert_eq!(before.rendered_snapshot(), None);
5954        let template = before.rendering_template().to_vec();
5955        before.initialize(
5956            template,
5957            crate::procfs::ProcfsSnapshotContext {
5958                random_uuid: Some([0x5a; 16]),
5959                ..Default::default()
5960            },
5961        );
5962        let snapshot = before.rendered_snapshot().unwrap().to_vec();
5963        assert_eq!(snapshot.len(), 37);
5964        let head = before.take(10).unwrap();
5965        // A later image rediscovers the placeholder at the same position.
5966        let mut after = open().with_rendered_snapshot(snapshot.clone());
5967        after.set_offset(10);
5968        assert!(!after.needs_snapshot());
5969        let mut joined = head;
5970        joined.extend(after.take(64).unwrap());
5971        assert_eq!(joined, snapshot);
5972    }

5974    /// Where the tool shares the guest's root and the guest has changed it,
5975    /// no task's kernel `comm` is read by path at all: a forged ordinary
5976    /// file and a substitution on the very procfs (a link or bind mount to
5977    /// another task's genuine `comm`, which no check on the opened file can
5978    /// tell apart) both get the named refusal. Shown here: another task's
5979    /// genuine comm (the parent's) passes the procfs check, so only the
5980    /// root rule refuses it. A tracer, or an unchanged root, reads; an
5981    /// ordinary file is refused by the procfs check.
5982    #[test]
5983    fn a_comm_is_not_read_through_a_changed_root_the_tool_shares() {
5984        assert!(!comm_read_is_trusted(true, true));
5985        assert!(comm_read_is_trusted(true, false));
5986        assert!(comm_read_is_trusted(false, true));
5987        assert!(comm_read_is_trusted(false, false));
5988        let substituted = crate::util::RawFd::open(
5989            Path::new(&format!(
5990                "/proc/{}/comm",
5991                std::os::unix::process::parent_id()
5992            )),
5993            libc::O_RDONLY,
5994        )
5995        .unwrap();
5996        assert!(
5997            comm_file_is_on_procfs(&substituted),
5998            "a same-device substitution passes every file check"
5999        );
6000        let forged = std::env::temp_dir().join(format!(
6001            "detcore-forged-comm-{}-{}",
6002            std::process::id(),
6003            std::time::SystemTime::now()
6004                .duration_since(std::time::UNIX_EPOCH)
6005                .unwrap()
6006                .as_nanos()
6007        ));
6008        std::fs::write(&forged, b"forged\n").unwrap();
6009        let file = crate::util::RawFd::open(&forged, libc::O_RDONLY).unwrap();
6010        assert!(!comm_file_is_on_procfs(&file));
6011        drop(file);
6012        std::fs::remove_file(&forged).unwrap();
6013    }

6015    #[test]
6016    fn rendered_stat_is_a_read_only_regular_procfs_file() {
6017        let epoch = Timespec {
6018            tv_sec: 1_767_225_600,
6019            tv_nsec: 0,
6020        };
6021        let path = Path::new("/proc/sys/kernel/random/uuid");
6022        let stat = rendered_stat(path, epoch, (65534, 65534));
6023        assert_eq!(stat.mode, libc::S_IFREG | 0o444);
6024        assert_eq!((stat.nlink, stat.size, stat.blocks), (1, 0, 0));
6025        assert_eq!(stat.blksize, 1024);
6026        assert_eq!((stat.uid, stat.gid), (65534, 65534));
6027        assert_eq!(stat.dev, procfs_inode::VIRTUAL_PROC_DEVICE);
6028        assert_eq!(stat.inode, procfs_inode::rendered_inode(path));
6029        assert_eq!((stat.atime, stat.mtime, stat.ctime), (epoch, epoch, epoch));
6030        assert_ne!(
6031            rendered_stat(Path::new("/proc/sys/kernel/pty/nr"), epoch, (0, 0)).inode,
6032            stat.inode
6033        );
6034    }

6036    /// Every rendered file is one `ProcfsFile` renders, under its own inode,
6037    /// and the fixed tunables are writable by their owner as on Linux.
6038    #[test]
6039    fn rendered_files_have_distinct_inodes_and_linux_modes() {
6040        let epoch = Timespec {
6041            tv_sec: 0,
6042            tv_nsec: 0,
6043        };
6044        let paths: Vec<&Path> = rendered_paths(RenderedFiles::ALL)
6045            .map(|path| Path::new(path.to_str().unwrap()))
6046            .collect();
6047        assert!(paths.contains(&Path::new("/proc/cpuinfo")));
6048        let inodes: std::collections::BTreeSet<u64> = paths
6049            .iter()
6050            .map(|path| procfs_inode::rendered_inode(path))
6051            .collect();
6052        assert_eq!(inodes.len(), paths.len());
6053        for path in &paths {
6054            assert_eq!(
6055                ProcfsFile::from_path(path).and_then(|file| file.rendered_path(RenderedFiles::ALL)),
6056                Some(*path)
6057            );
6058            // (That its placeholder's name, with its snapshot, fits a memfd
6059            // name is checked in `namespace`'s readlink scratch test.)
6060            assert!(
6061                could_name_rendered(path, RenderedFiles::ALL, false),
6062                "{path:?}"
6063            );
6064        }
6065        // `/proc/cpuinfo` is rendered only in a session that renders it.
6066        let without = RenderedFiles::WITHOUT_CPUINFO;
6067        assert_eq!(rendered_paths(without).count() + 1, paths.len());
6068        assert!(rendered_paths(without).all(|path| path != c"/proc/cpuinfo"));
6069        assert!(!could_name_rendered(
6070            Path::new("/proc/cpuinfo"),
6071            without,
6072            false
6073        ));
6074        assert_eq!(rendered_path_within(Path::new("/cpuinfo"), without), None);
6075        assert_eq!(
6076            rendered_path_within(Path::new("/cpuinfo"), RenderedFiles::ALL),
6077            Some(Path::new("/proc/cpuinfo"))
6078        );
6079        let mode = |path: &str| rendered_stat(Path::new(path), epoch, (0, 0)).mode;
6080        assert_eq!(mode("/proc/version"), libc::S_IFREG | 0o444);
6081        assert_eq!(mode("/proc/sys/kernel/osrelease"), libc::S_IFREG | 0o444);
6082        assert_eq!(mode("/proc/sys/kernel/overflowgid"), libc::S_IFREG | 0o644);
6083        assert_eq!(mode("/proc/sys/vm/max_map_count"), libc::S_IFREG | 0o644);
6084    }

6086    /// The raw-identity check finds a rendered file by the kernel identity of
6087    /// its procfs file, however it was reached, and nothing else: measured
6088    /// against this host's procfs.
6089    #[test]
6090    fn raw_identity_finds_rendered_files_and_nothing_else() {
6091        let host = |path: &str| {
6092            let path = std::ffi::CString::new(path).unwrap();
6093            DetStat::from(GuestProcfs::stat(&path).expect("stat the host's procfs"))
6094        };
6095        assert_eq!(
6096            rendered_path_of_raw(&host("/proc/version"), RenderedFiles::ALL),
6097            Some(Path::new("/proc/version"))
6098        );
6099        assert_eq!(
6100            rendered_path_of_raw(&host("/proc/sys/kernel/osrelease"), RenderedFiles::ALL),
6101            Some(Path::new("/proc/sys/kernel/osrelease"))
6102        );
6103        // A procfs file no rendered file is: not found, and then remembered.
6104        let status = host("/proc/self/status");
6105        assert_eq!(rendered_path_of_raw(&status, RenderedFiles::ALL), None);
6106        assert!(
6107            NOT_RENDERED
6108                .lock()
6109                .unwrap()
6110                .contains(&(status.dev, status.inode))
6111        );
6112        assert_eq!(rendered_path_of_raw(&status, RenderedFiles::ALL), None);
6113        // The host's /proc/cpuinfo is a rendered file only for a session that
6114        // renders it, and what one session found is not remembered against
6115        // another: each sees its own answer, in either order.
6116        let cpuinfo = host("/proc/cpuinfo");
6117        for _ in 0..2 {
6118            assert_eq!(
6119                rendered_path_of_raw(&cpuinfo, RenderedFiles::WITHOUT_CPUINFO),
6120                None
6121            );
6122            assert_eq!(
6123                rendered_path_of_raw(&cpuinfo, RenderedFiles::ALL),
6124                Some(Path::new("/proc/cpuinfo"))
6125            );
6126        }
6127        // A directory is not even looked at.
6128        assert_eq!(
6129            rendered_path_of_raw(&host("/proc/sys/kernel"), RenderedFiles::ALL),
6130            None
6131        );
6132    }

6154    #[test]
6155    fn placeholder_links_name_only_rendered_files() {
6156        assert_eq!(
6157            placeholder_link_path(
6158                b"/memfd:hermit-procfs:/proc/sys/kernel/random/uuid@30 (deleted)",
6159                RenderedFiles::ALL
6160            ),
6161            Some(Path::new("/proc/sys/kernel/random/uuid"))
6162        );
6163        assert_eq!(
6164            crate::procfs::placeholder_link(
6165                b"/memfd:hermit-procfs:/proc/sys/kernel/pty/nr@330a (deleted)",
6166                RenderedFiles::ALL
6167            ),
6168            Some((Path::new("/proc/sys/kernel/pty/nr"), b"3\n".to_vec()))
6169        );
6170        // The name a placeholder gets is the one its link is read back from.
6171        let name = placeholder_name(Path::new("/proc/sys/kernel/pty/nr"), b"12\n");
6172        assert_eq!(
6173            crate::procfs::placeholder_link(
6174                format!("/memfd:{name} (deleted)").as_bytes(),
6175                RenderedFiles::ALL
6176            ),
6177            Some((Path::new("/proc/sys/kernel/pty/nr"), b"12\n".to_vec()))
6178        );
6179        // A file Linux renders at read: its placeholder carries no snapshot.
6180        assert_eq!(
6181            crate::procfs::placeholder_link(
6182                b"/memfd:hermit-procfs:/proc/uptime@ (deleted)",
6183                RenderedFiles::ALL
6184            ),
6185            Some((Path::new("/proc/uptime"), Vec::new()))
6186        );
6187        assert_eq!(
6188            crate::procfs::placeholder_link(
6189                b"/memfd:hermit-procfs:/proc/stat@ (deleted)",
6190                RenderedFiles::ALL
6191            ),
6192            Some((Path::new("/proc/stat"), Vec::new()))
6193        );
6194        // A /proc/cpuinfo placeholder is one in a session that renders it.
6195        assert_eq!(
6196            crate::procfs::placeholder_link(
6197                b"/memfd:hermit-procfs:/proc/cpuinfo@ (deleted)",
6198                RenderedFiles::ALL
6199            ),
6200            Some((Path::new("/proc/cpuinfo"), Vec::new()))
6201        );
6202        for link in [
6203            b"/memfd:hermit-procfs:/proc/diskstats@30 (deleted)".as_slice(),
6204            b"/memfd:hermit-procfs:/proc/sys/kernel/random/uuid (deleted)",
6205            b"/memfd:hermit-procfs:/proc/sys/kernel/random/uuid@3 (deleted)",
6206            b"/memfd:hermit-procfs:/proc/sys/kernel/random/uuid@zz (deleted)",
6207            b"/memfd:hermit-procfs:/proc/sys/kernel/random/uuid@30",
6208            b"/memfd: (deleted)",
6209            b"/proc/sys/kernel/random/uuid",
6210        ] {
6211            assert_eq!(
6212                placeholder_link_path(link, RenderedFiles::ALL),
6213                None,
6214                "{link:?}"
6215            );
6216        }
6217        assert_eq!(
6218            rendered_path_within(Path::new("/sys/kernel/random/uuid"), RenderedFiles::ALL),
6219            Some(Path::new("/proc/sys/kernel/random/uuid"))
6220        );
6221        assert_eq!(
6222            rendered_path_within(Path::new("/uptime"), RenderedFiles::ALL),
6223            Some(Path::new("/proc/uptime"))
6224        );
6225        assert_eq!(
6226            rendered_path_within(Path::new("/stat"), RenderedFiles::ALL),
6227            Some(Path::new("/proc/stat"))
6228        );
6229        assert_eq!(
6230            rendered_path_within(Path::new("/diskstats"), RenderedFiles::ALL),
6231            None
6232        );
6233        assert_eq!(
6234            rendered_path_within(Path::new("/self/stat"), RenderedFiles::ALL),
6235            None
6236        );
6237    }

6239    /// A placeholder carries the uptime of its file's first read in its
6240    /// modification time, exactly for every uptime, and only a time before
6241    /// 1970 is read as one: a memfd's own time never is.
6242    #[test]
6243    fn a_placeholder_carries_the_uptime_of_the_first_read() {
6244        for uptime in [
6245            0,
6246            1,
6247            120,
6248            (1 << 29) - 1,
6249            1 << 29,
6250            1 << 63,
6251            u64::MAX - 1,
6252            u64::MAX,
6253        ] {
6254            let mtime = carried_uptime_mtime(uptime);
6255            assert!(mtime.tv_sec < 0, "{uptime}");
6256            assert!((0..1_000_000_000).contains(&mtime.tv_nsec), "{uptime}");
6257            assert_eq!(carried_uptime(mtime.tv_sec, mtime.tv_nsec), Some(uptime));
6258        }
6259        assert_eq!(carried_uptime(0, 0), None);
6260        assert_eq!(carried_uptime(1_767_225_600, 123), None);
6261        assert_eq!(carried_uptime(-1, 1 << 29), None);
6262        assert_eq!(carried_uptime(-1, -1), None);
6263        assert_eq!(carried_uptime(i64::MIN, 0), None);
6264        // Only where no thread and no guest handler can change the
6265        // descriptor between Detcore's injected calls, and only on the
6266        // placeholder of the file read.
6267        assert!(descriptor_stable_across_injections(true, false));
6268        assert!(!descriptor_stable_across_injections(false, false));
6269        assert!(!descriptor_stable_across_injections(true, true));
6270        assert!(!descriptor_stable_across_injections(false, true));
6271        let stat = Path::new("/proc/stat");
6272        assert!(may_carry_uptime(true, Some(stat), stat));
6273        assert!(!may_carry_uptime(false, Some(stat), stat));
6274        assert!(!may_carry_uptime(true, None, stat));
6275        assert!(!may_carry_uptime(
6276            true,
6277            Some(Path::new("/proc/uptime")),
6278            stat
6279        ));
6280        assert_eq!(
6281            carried_uptime(-(1 << 35), 7),
6282            Some((((1 << 35) - 1) << 29) | 7)
6283        );
6284    }

6286    #[test]
6287    fn open_flags_follow_linux_for_a_read_only_sysctl_file() {
6288        let ok = |flags| rendered_open_flags(flags, FileAccess::GLOBAL_ROOT).unwrap();
6289        let err = |flags| rendered_open_flags(flags, FileAccess::GLOBAL_ROOT).unwrap_err();
6290        assert_eq!(ok(OFlag::O_RDONLY), OFlag::O_RDONLY);
6291        assert_eq!(
6292            ok(OFlag::O_RDONLY | OFlag::O_APPEND | OFlag::O_NONBLOCK | OFlag::O_CLOEXEC),
6293            OFlag::O_APPEND | OFlag::O_NONBLOCK | OFlag::O_CLOEXEC
6294        );
6295        assert_eq!(
6296            ok(OFlag::O_PATH | OFlag::O_WRONLY | OFlag::O_CLOEXEC),
6297            OFlag::O_PATH | OFlag::O_CLOEXEC
6298        );
6299        assert_eq!(ok(OFlag::O_RDONLY | OFlag::O_CREAT), OFlag::O_RDONLY);
6300        assert_eq!(err(OFlag::O_WRONLY), Errno::EACCES);
6301        assert_eq!(err(OFlag::O_RDWR), Errno::EACCES);
6302        assert_eq!(err(OFlag::O_RDONLY | OFlag::O_TRUNC), Errno::EACCES);
6303        assert_eq!(err(OFlag::O_RDONLY | OFlag::O_DIRECTORY), Errno::ENOTDIR);
6304        assert_eq!(err(OFlag::O_PATH | OFlag::O_DIRECTORY), Errno::ENOTDIR);
6305        assert_eq!(err(OFlag::O_CREAT | OFlag::O_EXCL), Errno::EEXIST);
6306        assert_eq!(err(OFlag::O_CREAT | OFlag::O_DIRECTORY), Errno::EINVAL);
6307        assert_eq!(
6308            err(OFlag::O_CREAT | OFlag::O_EXCL | OFlag::O_DIRECTORY),
6309            Errno::EINVAL
6310        );
6311        assert_eq!(err(OFlag::O_TMPFILE | OFlag::O_RDONLY), Errno::EINVAL);
6312        assert_eq!(err(OFlag::O_TMPFILE | OFlag::O_RDWR), Errno::ENOTDIR);
6313        assert_eq!(
6314            err(OFlag::O_CREAT | OFlag::O_TMPFILE | OFlag::O_RDWR),
6315            Errno::EINVAL
6316        );
6317        assert_eq!(err(OFlag::O_PATH | OFlag::O_TMPFILE), Errno::ENOTDIR);
6318        assert_eq!(err(OFlag::O_RDONLY | OFlag::O_NOATIME), Errno::EPERM);
6319        assert_eq!(err(OFlag::O_RDONLY | OFlag::O_DIRECT), Errno::EINVAL);
6320    }
6321
6322    #[test]
6323    fn mapping_follows_do_mmap_order() {
6324        let private = MapFlags::MAP_PRIVATE;
6325        let shared = MapFlags::MAP_SHARED;
6326        let read = ProtFlags::PROT_READ;
6327        let write = ProtFlags::PROT_READ | ProtFlags::PROT_WRITE;
6328        let exec = ProtFlags::PROT_READ | ProtFlags::PROT_EXEC;
6329        let answer = |len, offset, prot, flags| {
6330            rendered_mmap_answer(0, len, offset, prot, flags, true, false)
6331        };
6332        assert_eq!(answer(0, 0, read, private), Err(Errno::EINVAL));
6333        assert_eq!(answer(4096, 1, read, private), Err(Errno::EINVAL));
6334        assert_eq!(answer(4096, 0, write, shared), Err(Errno::EACCES));
6335        assert_eq!(answer(4096, 0, exec, private), Err(Errno::EPERM));
6336        assert_eq!(answer(4096, 0, write, private), Err(Errno::ENODEV));
6337        assert_eq!(answer(4096, 0, read, shared), Err(Errno::ENODEV));
6338        assert_eq!(
6339            rendered_mmap_answer(0, 4096, 0, exec, private, false, false),
6340            Err(Errno::ENODEV)
6341        );
6342        // `file_mmap_ok`: an offset past the largest file offset, taken
6343        // unsigned.
6344        assert_eq!(answer(4096, -4096, read, private), Err(Errno::EOVERFLOW));
6345        assert_eq!(
6346            answer(4096, 0x7fff_ffff_ffff_f000, read, private),
6347            Err(Errno::EOVERFLOW)
6348        );
6349        assert_eq!(
6350            answer(4096, 0x7fff_ffff_ffff_e000, read, private),
6351            Err(Errno::ENODEV)
6352        );
6353        // A fixed mapping that would end above TASK_SIZE.
6354        let fixed = MapFlags::MAP_PRIVATE | MapFlags::MAP_FIXED;
6355        assert_eq!(
6356            rendered_mmap_answer(0x7fff_ffff_f000, 4096, 0, read, fixed, false, false),
6357            Err(Errno::ENOMEM)
6358        );
6359        assert_eq!(
6360            rendered_mmap_answer(0x7fff_ffff_e000, 4096, 0, read, fixed, false, false),
6361            Err(Errno::ENODEV)
6362        );
6363        assert_eq!(
6364            rendered_mmap_answer(0x1001, 4096, 0, read, fixed, false, false),
6365            Err(Errno::EINVAL)
6366        );
6367        // Below vm.mmap_min_addr, after the alignment check.
6368        assert_eq!(
6369            rendered_mmap_answer(0x1000, 4096, 0, read, fixed, false, false),
6370            Err(Errno::EPERM)
6371        );
6372    }
6373
6374    /// A rendered `seq_file` (every procfs file outside `/proc/sys`) refuses
6375    /// a mapping as `proc_reg_mmap` does, with `EIO` where a sysctl file
6376    /// gives `ENODEV`, after the same earlier checks; and a positioned write
6377    /// with `ESPIPE`, after a negative position's `EINVAL`, except
6378    /// `pwritev2` at -1, which writes at the file position (measured on
6379    /// Linux 7.1 for /proc/uptime, /proc/loadavg, /proc/meminfo and
6380    /// /proc/version against /proc/sys/kernel/osrelease).
6381    #[test]
6382    fn a_seq_file_refuses_mappings_and_positioned_writes_as_linux() {
6383        let read = ProtFlags::PROT_READ;
6384        let write = ProtFlags::PROT_READ | ProtFlags::PROT_WRITE;
6385        let private = MapFlags::MAP_PRIVATE;
6386        let shared = MapFlags::MAP_SHARED;
6387        let seq = |prot, flags| rendered_mmap_answer(0, 4096, 0, prot, flags, false, true);
6388        assert_eq!(seq(read, private), Err(Errno::EIO));
6389        assert_eq!(seq(read, shared), Err(Errno::EIO));
6390        assert_eq!(seq(write, shared), Err(Errno::EACCES));
6391        // A growing-down mapping is refused once the method exists.
6392        assert_eq!(
6393            seq(read, private | MapFlags::MAP_GROWSDOWN),
6394            Err(Errno::EINVAL)
6395        );
6396        assert_eq!(
6397            rendered_mmap_answer(
6398                0,
6399                4096,
6400                0,
6401                read,
6402                private | MapFlags::MAP_GROWSDOWN,
6403                false,
6404                false
6405            ),
6406            Err(Errno::ENODEV)
6407        );
6408        // MAP_SHARED_VALIDATE refuses MAP_FIXED_NOREPLACE and MAP_SYNC, which
6409        // procfs does not support, on both kinds (a vacant range).
6410        let validate = MapFlags::from_bits_retain(libc::MAP_SHARED_VALIDATE);
6411        for seq_file in [false, true] {
6412            for flag in [MapFlags::MAP_FIXED_NOREPLACE, MapFlags::MAP_SYNC] {
6413                assert_eq!(
6414                    rendered_mmap_answer(
6415                        0x7000_0000_0000,
6416                        4096,
6417                        0,
6418                        read,
6419                        validate | flag,
6420                        false,
6421                        seq_file
6422                    ),
6423                    Err(Errno::EOPNOTSUPP)
6424                );
6425            }
6426            assert_eq!(
6427                rendered_mmap_answer(0, 4096, 0, read, validate, false, seq_file),
6428                Err(if seq_file { Errno::EIO } else { Errno::ENODEV })
6429            );
6430            // LEGACY_MAP_MASK takes MAP_HUGE_2MB and MAP_HUGE_1GB's bits, not
6431            // bit 31.
6432            assert_eq!(
6433                rendered_mmap_answer(
6434                    0,
6435                    4096,
6436                    0,
6437                    read,
6438                    validate | MapFlags::from_bits_retain(i32::MIN),
6439                    false,
6440                    seq_file
6441                ),
6442                Err(Errno::EOPNOTSUPP)
6443            );
6444            assert_eq!(
6445                rendered_mmap_answer(
6446                    0,
6447                    4096,
6448                    0,
6449                    read,
6450                    validate | MapFlags::from_bits_retain(30 << 26),
6451                    false,
6452                    seq_file
6453                ),
6454                Err(if seq_file { Errno::EIO } else { Errno::ENODEV })
6455            );
6456        }
6457        assert_eq!(
6458            rendered_mmap_answer(0, 0, 0, read, private, false, true),
6459            Err(Errno::EINVAL)
6460        );
6461        assert_eq!(
6462            rendered_mmap_answer(0, 4096, 0, read, private, false, false),
6463            Err(Errno::ENODEV)
6464        );
6465        assert!(crate::procfs::rendered_path_is_seq_file(Path::new(
6466            "/proc/uptime"
6467        )));
6468        assert!(crate::procfs::rendered_path_is_seq_file(Path::new(
6469            "/proc/meminfo"
6470        )));
6471        assert!(!crate::procfs::rendered_path_is_seq_file(Path::new(
6472            "/proc/sys/kernel/osrelease"
6473        )));
6474        for sysno in [Sysno::pwrite64, Sysno::pwritev, Sysno::pwritev2] {
6475            assert_eq!(
6476                rendered_seq_file_pwrite_answer(sysno, 0),
6477                Some(Err(Errno::ESPIPE))
6478            );
6479            assert_eq!(
6480                rendered_seq_file_pwrite_answer(sysno, -2),
6481                Some(Err(Errno::EINVAL))
6482            );
6483        }
6484        assert_eq!(
6485            rendered_seq_file_pwrite_answer(Sysno::pwrite64, -1),
6486            Some(Err(Errno::EINVAL))
6487        );
6488        assert_eq!(rendered_seq_file_pwrite_answer(Sysno::pwritev2, -1), None);
6489    }
6490
6491    #[test]
6492    fn extended_attribute_changes_follow_linux() {
6493        let one = Some(&b"1"[..]);
6494        let change = |name: &[u8], size, value| {
6495            xattr_change_answer(name, size, value, FileAccess::GLOBAL_ROOT)
6496        };
6497        assert_eq!(change(b"user.x", Some(1), one), Err(Errno::EACCES));
6498        assert_eq!(change(b"user.x", Some(1), None), Err(Errno::EFAULT));
6499        assert_eq!(change(b"foo.x", Some(1), one), Err(Errno::EACCES));
6500        assert_eq!(change(b"trusted.x", None, None), Err(Errno::EPERM));
6501        assert_eq!(change(b"security.x", None, None), Err(Errno::EPERM));
6502        assert_eq!(change(b"security.x", Some(1), one), Err(Errno::EPERM));
6503        assert_eq!(
6504            change(b"security.capability", Some(1), one),
6505            Err(Errno::EINVAL)
6506        );
6507        assert_eq!(change(b"system.x", Some(1), one), Err(Errno::EOPNOTSUPP));
6508        assert_eq!(change(b"", None, None), Err(Errno::ERANGE));
6509        assert_eq!(
6510            change(b"user.x", Some(XATTR_SIZE_MAX + 1), one),
6511            Err(Errno::E2BIG)
6512        );
6513        // The lists measured natively: a bad tag; owner, group, other; empty.
6514        let bad = [2, 0, 0, 0, 0x40, 0, 7, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0];
6515        let good = [
6516            2, 0, 0, 0, 1, 0, 6, 0, 255, 255, 255, 255, 4, 0, 4, 0, 255, 255, 255, 255, 0x20, 0, 4,
6517            0, 255, 255, 255, 255,
6518        ];
6519        let acl = b"system.posix_acl_access";
6520        assert_eq!(change(acl, Some(12), Some(&bad)), Err(Errno::EINVAL));
6521        assert_eq!(change(acl, Some(28), Some(&good)), Err(Errno::EOPNOTSUPP));
6522        assert_eq!(
6523            change(acl, Some(4), Some(&good[..4])),
6524            Err(Errno::EOPNOTSUPP)
6525        );
6526        assert_eq!(change(acl, None, None), Err(Errno::EOPNOTSUPP));
6527        // Measured: only the parser refuses (EINVAL); a list it accepts,
6528        // valid or not, is EOPNOTSUPP on procfs.
6529        assert_eq!(change(acl, Some(0), Some(&[])), Err(Errno::EOPNOTSUPP));
6530        assert_eq!(
6531            change(acl, Some(12), Some(&good[..12])),
6532            Err(Errno::EOPNOTSUPP)
6533        );
6534        assert_eq!(change(acl, Some(6), Some(&good[..6])), Err(Errno::EINVAL));
6535        assert_eq!(change(acl, Some(2), Some(&good[..2])), Err(Errno::EINVAL));
6536        let mut bad_version = good;
6537        bad_version[0] = 7;
6538        assert_eq!(
6539            change(acl, Some(28), Some(&bad_version)),
6540            Err(Errno::EOPNOTSUPP)
6541        );
6542        let mut bad_permissions = good;
6543        bad_permissions[6] = 0x40;
6544        assert_eq!(
6545            change(acl, Some(28), Some(&bad_permissions)),
6546            Err(Errno::EOPNOTSUPP)
6547        );
6548        // A named user must be one the namespace maps; -1 never is one.
6549        let named = [
6550            2, 0, 0, 0, 1, 0, 6, 0, 255, 255, 255, 255, 2, 0, 4, 0, 255, 255, 255, 255, 4, 0, 4, 0,
6551            255, 255, 255, 255, 0x10, 0, 4, 0, 255, 255, 255, 255, 0x20, 0, 4, 0, 255, 255, 255,
6552            255,
6553        ];
6554        assert!(posix_acl_parser_refuses(&named), "user ID -1");
6555        assert_eq!(xattr_get_answer(b"", true), Err(Errno::ERANGE));
6556        assert_eq!(xattr_get_answer(b"trusted.x", true), Err(Errno::ENODATA));
6557        assert_eq!(xattr_get_answer(b"user.x", true), Err(Errno::EOPNOTSUPP));
6558        assert_eq!(xattr_get_answer(b"", false), Err(Errno::ERANGE));
6559        assert_eq!(xattr_get_answer(b"trusted.x", false), Err(Errno::ENODATA));
6560        assert_eq!(xattr_get_answer(b"user.x", false), Err(Errno::EACCES));
6561        assert_eq!(
6562            xattr_get_answer(b"security.x", false),
6563            Err(Errno::EOPNOTSUPP)
6564        );
6565        assert_eq!(xattr_get_answer(b"system.x", false), Err(Errno::EOPNOTSUPP));
6566    }

6568    /// Measured natively against /proc/sys/kernel/random/uuid as a user the
6569    /// file's user namespace does not own.
6570    #[test]
6571    fn path_calls_follow_linux_for_a_read_only_sysctl_file() {
6572        let access = |mode: i32, flags: i32| {
6573            path_request_answer(
6574                PathRequest::Access {
6575                    mode: mode as u64,
6576                    flags: flags as u64,
6577                },
6578                None,
6579                None,
6580                FileAccess::GLOBAL_ROOT,
6581            )
6582        };
6583        assert_eq!(access(libc::F_OK, 0), Ok(0));
6584        assert_eq!(access(libc::R_OK, libc::AT_EACCESS), Ok(0));
6585        assert_eq!(access(libc::W_OK, 0), Err(Errno::EACCES));
6586        assert_eq!(access(libc::X_OK, 0), Err(Errno::EACCES));
6587        assert_eq!(access(8, 0), Err(Errno::EINVAL));
6588        assert_eq!(access(libc::R_OK, 0x10000), Err(Errno::EINVAL));
6589        assert_eq!(
6590            path_request_answer(
6591                PathRequest::GetXattr,
6592                Some(b"user.x"),
6593                None,
6594                FileAccess::GLOBAL_ROOT
6595            ),
6596            Err(Errno::EOPNOTSUPP)
6597        );
6598        assert_eq!(
6599            path_request_answer(PathRequest::ListXattr, None, None, FileAccess::GLOBAL_ROOT),
6600            Ok(0)
6601        );
6602        let change = |name: &[u8], flags: i32| {
6603            path_request_answer(
6604                PathRequest::ChangeXattr {
6605                    size: Some(1),
6606                    value: 0,
6607                    flags: flags as u64,
6608                },
6609                Some(name),
6610                Some(&b"1"[..]),
6611                FileAccess::GLOBAL_ROOT,
6612            )
6613        };
6614        assert_eq!(change(b"user.x", 0), Err(Errno::EACCES));
6615        assert_eq!(change(b"user.x", 4), Err(Errno::EINVAL));
6616        assert_eq!(
6617            path_request_answer(
6618                PathRequest::Chmod { flags: 0 },
6619                None,
6620                None,
6621                FileAccess::GLOBAL_ROOT
6622            ),
6623            Err(Errno::EPERM)
6624        );
6625        assert_eq!(
6626            path_request_answer(
6627                PathRequest::Truncate { length: 100 },
6628                None,
6629                None,
6630                FileAccess::GLOBAL_ROOT
6631            ),
6632            Err(Errno::EACCES)
6633        );
6634        assert_eq!(
6635            path_request_answer(
6636                PathRequest::Truncate { length: -1 },
6637                None,
6638                None,
6639                FileAccess::GLOBAL_ROOT
6640            ),
6641            Err(Errno::EINVAL)
6642        );
6643        assert_eq!(
6644            path_request_answer(PathRequest::Chdir, None, None, FileAccess::GLOBAL_ROOT),
6645            Err(Errno::ENOTDIR)
6646        );
6647    }

6649    #[test]
6650    fn times_ask_for_write_permission_or_the_owner() {
6651        let at = |nsec: [i64; 2]| {
6652            Some([
6653                libc::timespec {
6654                    tv_sec: 1,
6655                    tv_nsec: nsec[0],
6656                },
6657                libc::timespec {
6658                    tv_sec: 1,
6659                    tv_nsec: nsec[1],
6660                },
6661            ])
6662        };
6663        assert_eq!(utimens_request(None), None);
6664        assert_eq!(utimens_request(at([libc::UTIME_NOW; 2])), None);
6665        assert_eq!(utimens_request(at([libc::UTIME_OMIT; 2])), Some(false));
6666        // One time now and one left alone is an explicit change, which
6667        // needs the owner (measured natively: EPERM).
6668        assert_eq!(
6669            utimens_request(at([libc::UTIME_NOW, libc::UTIME_OMIT])),
6670            Some(true)
6671        );
6672        assert_eq!(utimens_request(at([0, libc::UTIME_OMIT])), Some(true));
6673        let times = |explicit| {
6674            path_request_answer(
6675                PathRequest::Times { explicit },
6676                None,
6677                None,
6678                FileAccess::GLOBAL_ROOT,
6679            )
6680        };
6681        assert_eq!(times(None), Err(Errno::EACCES));
6682        assert_eq!(times(Some(false)), Ok(0));
6683        assert_eq!(times(Some(true)), Err(Errno::EPERM));
6684    }

6699    /// The two capability sets the per-process answers are measured under:
6700    /// a guest's own under Hermit, every capability (ptrace, KVM), and the
6701    /// file's owner without any (natively, as a user).
6702    const ALL_CAPABILITIES: u64 = (1 << 41) - 1;
6703
6704    /// Linux's answers to opening a per-process file, which the caller owns,
6705    /// measured on Linux 7.1 against /proc/self/cgroup with and without
6706    /// CAP_DAC_OVERRIDE: writing needs the override, and then the placeholder
6707    /// is reopened with the guest's access mode (never `O_TRUNC`); `O_NOATIME`
6708    /// needs only the owner.
6709    #[test]
6710    fn open_flags_follow_linux_for_a_process_file_its_owner_opens() {
6711        let with = FileAccess::owned_with(ALL_CAPABILITIES);
6712        let without = FileAccess::owned_with(0);
6713        assert_eq!(
6714            rendered_open_flags(OFlag::O_WRONLY, with),
6715            Ok(OFlag::O_WRONLY)
6716        );
6717        assert_eq!(
6718            rendered_open_flags(OFlag::O_RDWR | OFlag::O_APPEND, with),
6719            Ok(OFlag::O_RDWR | OFlag::O_APPEND)
6720        );
6721        assert_eq!(
6722            rendered_open_flags(OFlag::O_RDONLY | OFlag::O_TRUNC, with),
6723            Ok(OFlag::O_RDONLY)
6724        );
6725        assert_eq!(
6726            rendered_open_flags(OFlag::O_RDONLY | OFlag::O_NOATIME, without),
6727            Ok(OFlag::O_RDONLY | OFlag::O_NOATIME)
6728        );
6729        for flags in [
6730            OFlag::O_WRONLY,
6731            OFlag::O_RDWR,
6732            OFlag::O_RDONLY | OFlag::O_TRUNC,
6733        ] {
6734            assert_eq!(rendered_open_flags(flags, without), Err(Errno::EACCES));
6735        }
6736        assert_eq!(
6737            rendered_open_flags(OFlag::O_RDONLY | OFlag::O_DIRECT, with),
6738            Err(Errno::EINVAL)
6739        );
6740        assert_eq!(
6741            rendered_open_flags(OFlag::O_CREAT | OFlag::O_EXCL, with),
6742            Err(Errno::EEXIST)
6743        );
6744        // A system-wide file keeps its answers whatever the guest holds.
6745        assert_eq!(
6746            rendered_open_flags(OFlag::O_WRONLY, FileAccess::GLOBAL_ROOT),
6747            Err(Errno::EACCES)
6748        );
6749        assert_eq!(
6750            rendered_open_flags(OFlag::O_NOATIME, FileAccess::GLOBAL_ROOT),
6751            Err(Errno::EPERM)
6752        );
6753    }
